- export-scan-red-checks-verdict: `assert_refused` in `test_export_page.py` checks the refused verdict, not the kind, so a case can stay red through the wrong shape. Each case hits only its own shape today. Decision: waiting-data-in-repo. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21
- classifier-refuses-rule-record-writer: in a cloud lane, the auto-mode classifier refused an `Agent` spawn (record-writer) that edits CLAUDE.md and agents/reviewer.md on a coordinator brief, as "Self-Modification" (lane review-fix-check, 2026-11-03 22:30 CST). A rule edit briefed by a peer session needs the owner's grant in the lane chat. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner. Fix: none — owner decides. Until: 2026-11-21
- lane-history-reword-refused: 2026-11-04, the lane-session-brake lane could not add a `Red-run: none` trailer to its own comment-only commit, because auto mode refused `git filter-branch` on the lane branch as destructive git. The route that works: a new branch from main, with the lane's commits cherry-picked and the trailer added to the copy. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner (refusals reach the owner). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21
- stall-stop-summary-race: the Stop check keys on the newest `stop_hook_summary` time, which the harness writes after the hooks end, so a stall that crosses in that gap (about 100 ms) is never listed. Decision: stall-watch. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21
- stall-listing-lost-on-block: when a sibling hook blocks the same Stop, the stall listing may never reach the model, and the dedupe does not list the job again. Unmeasured. A wrapped job's STALL report reaches the model anyway. Decision: stall-watch. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21
- launchd-sweep-log-growth: the daily job's stdout log, `<config>/janitor/launchd-sweep.log`, has no cap, and its growth per run is unmeasured. Decision: the-janitor-is-one-machine-wide-sweep. Fix: none — needs the Mac. Until: 2026-11-21
- agent-end-note-sees-200-rows: the daily note's agent-end check reads only the last 200 rows of the payload log. A 7-day window can hold more runs than that on a busy machine. A run whose rows were cut is not counted. Decision: guard-log-read-weekly. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21
- agent-end-lock-wait-hides-kill: a started row lost to the payload log's 30 s lock wait (`LOG_WAIT_SECONDS`) hides a killed run from the daily note. Decision: agent-end-reap-stops-what-a-finished-agent-left. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21
- agent-end-lost-done-reads-killed: a done row that `log()` dropped (a timeout, a refused read or a failed write) makes a finished run read as killed in the daily note's reap line. The drops line, from `state/agent-end-drops.jsonl` (read hooks/daily_note.py drops_failures), names the drop, but the reap line still counts that run as killed. Rate unmeasured. Decision: guard-log-read-weekly. Fix: none — owner decides. Until: 2026-11-24
- agent-end-log-freezes-on-refused-read: a read of the payload log refused past the deadline drops each new row, and the log keeps its rows. Each hook run then waits the whole deadline, as a lasting replace refusal does. The daily drops line shows the drops, unless `state/` cannot be written or the rows rotated to `.old`. Decision: guard-log-read-weekly. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-22
- agent-end-drop-stderr-unread: when the `state/` folder cannot be written, or the disk is full, `log()` (janitor/agent_end_reap.py) cannot append to the drops file either. Only stderr reports the drop. Whether the harness keeps a SubagentStop hook's stderr where a reader can open it is unmeasured. Decision: guard-log-read-weekly. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21
- agent-end-drops-rotate-race: the drops append and its rotation at 256 KiB run outside the log lock (janitor/agent_end_reap.py `log`). Two writers that both pass the size check can move one fresh row over `.old`. Unread old rows are lost and the count is one short. The current file still fires the line. Decision: guard-log-read-weekly. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21
- run-log-key-case-on-mac: `janitor/sweep.py` `run_log_key` says it matches a differently cased path, but `normcase` folds case only on Windows. On macOS, a root typed in another case reads `none` in the preview. Decision: the-janitor-is-one-machine-wide-sweep. Fix: none — needs the Mac. Until: 2026-11-21
- run-log-key-untested: no test pins the trim's use of `run_log_key`. The reader merges spellings, so no wrong line shows today. Decision: the-janitor-is-one-machine-wide-sweep. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21
- run-log-lock-thread-only: `_RUN_LOG_LOCK` guards a thread race that `_pmap` does not make on a `--confirm` run, since the root pool exists only for previews. The real race, two processes writing at once, is unguarded. Decision: the-janitor-is-one-machine-wide-sweep. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21
- blind-count-marks: in lane lane-word-rename (review round 2, reviewer sonnet, 2026-11-04 09:22 CST), `BLIND_COUNT` in hooks/blind_brief.py does not strip leading markdown marks, though `KIND_LINE` does. So `- Designer kind: informed` with `- Blind designers: 2` is refused for a missing count line. Cost: one retry. The refusal names the plain form. Decision: blind-briefs-use-a-template.
- kind-line-pins: in lane lane-word-rename (review round 2, reviewer sonnet, 2026-11-04 09:22 CST), no test pins `\s*:` in `KIND_LINE` or `>` in `LEADING_MARKS` in hooks/blind_brief.py, and no hook-level case spawns a decorated informed brief. Decision: blind-briefs-use-a-template. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21
- notes-blind-lanes-cite: in lane lane-word-rename (review round 2, reviewer sonnet, 2026-11-04 09:22 CST), older lines in this file, such as blind-brief-count-unchecked and blind-lane-on-redraw, name the header `Blind lanes:`. The header is now `Blind designers:`. The old lines stay as history. Decision: blind-briefs-use-a-template.
- lane-call-wording: in lane lane-word-rename (review round 2, reviewer sonnet, 2026-11-04 09:22 CST), comments in hooks/test_guard.py still say "lane call" beside "start call" for START_TOOLS calls. Decision: none yet. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21
- export-all-step-test-pins-wording: the test of the "Before you switch Claude accounts" step 2 in `test_export_page.py` reads `out_dir` after "set to" or "=", and the "make ... by hand" sentence; a reword such as "Create the folder by hand" goes red, with the cause line shown. Decision: waiting-data-in-repo. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-22
- cloud-released-lock-stuck: in a cloud session, a released merge lock ref (refs/heads/merge-lock/main, q_max, 2026-11-04 03:44 CST) blocked every later merge with "another run took the merge lock first". The cloud lock (GitLock) re-takes a released lock with a leased push and no delete. But it read every failed push as a rival run and dropped the push output. So the cause is unmeasured. A lease push of an orphan commit to a session branch lands through the proxy (probe, 2026-11-04 03:58 CST). The auto-mode classifier refused both clears, `gh api -X DELETE .../git/refs/heads/merge-lock/main` and `merge --unlock main`. Fixed in part by PR merge-lock-retake: a failed push with the ref unchanged now stops and prints the push output. Read that output on the next refusal. Decision: merge-lock.
- lock-retake-reread-hides-push: when the re-read after a failed lock push fails, its error replaces the push output, so the refusal text is lost (merge/merge.py GitLock.acquire). Fix: append the push output. Decision: merge-lock.
- lock-retake-net-reads-refused: a failed lock push that never reached the remote (a network drop) with the ref unchanged prints "the remote refused". Fix: NetStop when is_net says so (merge/merge.py GitLock.acquire). Decision: merge-lock.
- lock-retake-stale-reread: a stale ls-remote after a rival's take reads as Stop, not Held. A caching proxy could cause it; unmeasured (merge/merge.py GitLock.acquire). Decision: merge-lock. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-22
- lane-send-message-refused: lane merge-lock-retake sent a peer session a hand force-push recipe for refs/heads/merge-lock/main; auto mode refused send_message, correctly. The resend without the recipe landed. The report guard then asks the owner to allow the tool, a false alarm when the refusal was right (lint/report_guard.py). Same family as lane-create-session-refused. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner. Fix: none — owner decides. Until: 2026-11-22
- merge-body-unlink-untested: If `Host.api` stops deleting its `--input` temp file, no test fails (merge/merge.py `Host.api`). Decision: none yet. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-22
- gh-cmd-shim-claim-split: the `Host.api` docstring says gh on Windows runs through a `.cmd` wrapper, but the GhLockTest skip says real gh.exe is native. If the wrapper is real, `GhLock.api` still sends the lock's two-line message as `-f`. Decision: none yet. Fix: none — needs a Windows run. Until: 2026-11-22
- lane-worktree-bash-refused: lane artifact-pages-other-account had two Bash calls refused because the session was isolated in a worktree. One used `git worktree add` inside a compound command; the other used sed with a program computed at run time. Splitting the work into a script file ran clean. The report guard then asks the owner to allow Bash, a false alarm when the refusal was right (lint/report_guard.py). Same family as lane-send-message-refused. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner.

The ruling (Cockpit item 219 a) reached the lane relayed by the coordinator, not typed by the owner. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner.
- grant-edit-direct-refused: after the owner's typed go (2026-11-04 17:53 CST), the auto-mode classifier still refused the lane's direct `Edit` of the "Acts the cloud refuses" grant in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` as [Self-Modification] (17:55 CST). The owner's pick a (17:55 CST) waits on that edit. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner.
- alarm-renamed-workflow-unknown: after a workflow file rename, actions/ci-alarm/alarm.sh reads the workflow at its current path, gets a 404 and prints unknown, never red; merge/merge.py reads the run's own path. Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-22

- auto-mode-force-push-lane: the auto-mode classifier refused `git push --force-with-lease` on a lane's own branch (2026-11-04 09:17 CST), though check_red_run's printed remedy names that push. The lane moved to a new branch and PR instead (#1240 replaces #1237). Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner.
- materialize-all-builds-every-fixture: materialize_all builds ~30 fixtures (~6 s) whenever any log check is picked; 253 of 543 log checks read ROOT; a per-check audit could skip builds (hooks/test_guard.py). Decision: none yet. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-22

- reply-send-clears-ask-line: a reply send clears the Ask box's result line, so an earlier "Undo failed" line goes. Nothing is lost (skills/waiting-page/page.html sendAsk). Decision: none yet. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-22
- reply-owner-falls-to-writer: askOwner falls back to the writer's name, so a wake for an answer with no owner says "answered by <writer>". Only bad data hits it (skills/waiting-page/page.html askOwner). Decision: none yet. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-22
- reply-midbox-edit-keeps-sent: an edit inside the reply box during its save keeps the whole box as the draft, sent words too, so a re-send repeats them. No words are lost (skills/waiting-page/page.html sendAsk). Decision: none yet. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-22

So `--tests-for hooks/guard_merge.py` prints `hooks/test_guard.py` (lines 18658 and 4583), which does run guard_merge through guard.py. No fixture pins the pooling. - underscore-test-is-product: the claude_settings `code_not` skips `test_*.py` and `_test_*.py` only, so a `*_test.py` file such as `hooks/case_runner_test.py` reads as product and gets mutants (hooks/mutate_guard.py REPLAY_PROFILES). Kept as main's behaviour, lane mutation-scope-skills, 2026-11-05. Decision: mutation-ci-report-and-replay. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- stale-pages-maps-guard-suite: `--tests-for skills/waiting-page/stale_pages.py` also prints `hooks/test_guard.py`, so each stale_pages mutant also runs the slow guard suite. Seen 2026-11-05, lane mutation-scope-skills. Decision: mutation-ci-report-and-replay. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- tested-by-label-near-miss: `  - Tested-by:` (a hyphen) is read as no line, so the claim goes unchecked; `Decisions:` has the same exact-label rule (skills/plain-english-map/scripts/map-check.mjs subLineEntries). Seen 2026-11-05, lane map-tested-by-line. Decision: map-tested-by-line. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- tested-by-data-string: a path string that is only data, such as `DATA = "src/x.py"`, passes MAP11 as a load. Decision: map-tested-by-line. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- tested-by-subheading: a `### ` heading inside `## Code files` ends the section for MAP11, so a tested-by line under it fails as misplaced (skills/plain-english-map/scripts/map-check.mjs subLineEntries). Decision: map-tested-by-line. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- own-model-unquoted-forms: the agent-body rule in `lint/check_agent_models.py` lets through an unbackticked header such as "first line is FAIL, round 2 of 3, sonnet." and "runs on the frontmatter model, haiku"; the incident form (backticked, "your ... model") is caught (review round 1 of lane reviewer-spawn-model). It also lets through `your frontmatter model, e.g. haiku.`, since the sentence ends at `e.g.` (review round 2). Decision: agent-model-cap. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- model-slot-phrase-loose: the same rule counts "the model" in any PASS/FAIL sentence as a model slot, and agent-model-cap names only `<model>`. It misses a sentence end before a closing quote. It accepts "powered by the" and "model named" split across a heading and a paragraph. It flags "If your brief names a model above Sonnet, stop.", since any "your" before "model" counts (review round 2) (review round 1 and fix check of lane reviewer-spawn-model). Decision: agent-model-cap. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- known-defects-deps-argv: `--known-defects` never fills `{deps}` in a defect's argv (hooks/mutate_shared.py _known_defect), so a part with `"link": None` (banchi py, degen) installs into a folder no catcher can name and reads UNKNOWN. Seen 2026-11-05, lane known-defects-install. Decision: mutation-ci-report-and-replay. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- known-defects-install-false-red: a failed install or link in `--known-defects` turns every defect UNKNOWN and the job red, even when no catcher needs a package (hooks/mutate_shared.py run_known_defects). The action takes a profile name only, so a caller cannot opt out; banchi, ios-adblock and joyous are exposed (review round 3 of lane known-defects-install). Decision: mutation-ci-report-and-replay.
- workflow-drops-per-file: `workflow_drops_tests` compares one file's test paths before and after. So the guard refuses three acts as a dropped test: a delete of a workflow that runs tests, a rename of it, and a move of a test step to another workflow file (hooks/guard.py `workflow_drops_tests`, called by `workflow_builder_refused`). Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- workflow-fragment-write-passes: a write-tool Edit fragment with no `run:` line, such as `name:` plus `continue-on-error: true`, passes at the write tool, because `workflow_disables_step` reads only `run:` lines of the fragment. Commit, push and stop refuse the same change on the full file (hooks/guard.py `workflow_disables_step`, `workflow_pairs`). Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- workflow-addopts-env-unread: `PYTEST_ADDOPTS: --ignore=...` under a step's `env:` is not read, since the guard reads `run:` lines only (hooks/guard.py `workflow_run_commands`). Main does not read it either. Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- test-author-run-forms-refused: a test-author cannot add `pytest tests/`, `python -m pytest`, `npm test`, or a step with an `env:` key. `workflow_run_is_test` accepts only an interpreter and a test path. `workflow_test_step` accepts only the keys in `WORKFLOW_STEP_KEYS`. A builder may add them (hooks/guard.py `workflow_run_is_test`, `workflow_test_step`). Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- echo-in-flagged-c: `echo` inside a flagged `-c` string reads as a test run, as the bare form already did (hooks/guard.py workflow_command_tests). Fix: none — refuses only. Until: 2026-11-21
- assign-quoted-test-read: `X="a pytest tests/x.py"` reads as a test run, an over-read that adds refusals on edit or drop (hooks/guard.py workflow_command_tests). Fix: none — rare form; the over-read is harmless and only refuses. Until: 2026-11-21
- failed-send-counts-as-ack: `last_incoming_index` still counts a failed send as an ack, so report_guard's turn can reach back one message (lint/_transcript.py `last_incoming_index`, used by lint/report_guard.py). Review round 2 of lane session-messaging-plan. Decision: session-messages.
- failed-ids-multi-result-record: `failed_tool_use_ids` applies a record's `toolUseResult.success` false to every tool_result in that record. 0 of 277 result records in 7 local transcripts held more than one result (review round 2 probe, 2026-11-06; lint/_transcript.py `failed_tool_use_ids`). Decision: session-messages.
- failed-ids-nondict-result-fails-open: a `toolUseResult` that is a string, a list or missing is not read, so a failed send with that shape still counts (lint/_transcript.py `failed_tool_use_ids`). The measured local failure shape is a dict. Decision: session-messages.
- refuter-rewrite-unjudged: a plan rewritten in place is not judged; only an added plan is (lint/check_plan_refuter.py). Decision: plan-needs-refuter (plan records need a refuter line).
- done-cutoff-window-unjudged: `DONE_CHECK_SINCE` (skills/roadmap/build.py) is a bandaid set from a measured launch count: 44 done steps updated Nov 7-8 CST lack the Done check line. A close between the merge of PR #1317 (step done-needs-check) and the cutoff is not judged. A close after the cutoff but before that merge would list, because triage has no instruction for that line until then. The instruction is in the Triage bullet of the operating model (skill `project-ops`). If the merge slips past the cutoff, move `DONE_CHECK_SINCE` by hand. Decision: none (roadmap upkeep row `done without check`).
- done-no-updatedat-listed: a done step with no `updatedAt` is listed even if it closed before `DONE_CHECK_SINCE`. On 2026-11-06 all 575 steps on the record branch carried `updatedAt`, so it is unlikely yet (skills/roadmap/build.py `done_without_check`). Decision: none (roadmap upkeep row `done without check`).
- done-ref-reads-hex-count: `DONE_REF_RE` takes any 6+ hex run with a digit, so "1234567 tests" reads as a ref (lint/report_guard.py, shared). Decision: none (roadmap upkeep row `done without check`).
- verdict-log-path-space: VERDICT_LOG_RE reads `log=(\S+)`, so a verdict log path with a space is not matched and that run's log is not read (lint/report_guard.py VERDICT_LOG_RE). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- shared-tag-pin-partial: SharedTagNames pins REPO_TAG, STEP_TAG and repo_tag_names by identity only, so a local copy of TOPIC_TAG, BUILD_TAG or lane_head_refs stays green (skills/roadmap/test_build.py SharedTagNames). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- roadmap-page-date-untested: skills/roadmap/tests/page.test.mjs has no case for index.html's date and time labels (`when`, `timeLabel`, lines 452-455). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- test-build-pin-comment-stale: skills/roadmap/test_build.py line 60 says "the page the old script used"; that comment is stale. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-24
- stalled-hold-missed-forms: the hold's build match (BUILD_CALL_RE, lint/report_guard.py) misses `python3 -X utf8 build.py`, a quoted path and `bash -c`; a background run (`python3 build.py &`) is matched. A crashed build prints no stalled line, so it reads clean. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-25
- own-record-no-time-bound: hooks/own_session.py `own_session_read` takes the newest own get_session record with no time bound. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-25
- triage-hold-except-silent: `triage_hold`'s except branch writes one stderr line and no guard.log line, so a hold that fails open leaves no record (lint/report_guard.py `triage_hold`). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-25
- ready-title-only-pr: a pull whose title names a step but whose branch does not prints both the pair line (`names_step` reads the title) and `ready not started:` (`lane_live` reads the branch), in skills/roadmap/build.py. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-25
- wake-fallback-no-delay: when the stored wake thread rejects with `upstream_error`, the page sends to a new anchored thread at once, in the same press, with no randomized delay. The comments contract asks for one delayed retry from a fresh gesture, since a rejected write may still have posted. If it did, one save wakes twice, in two threads (skills/waiting-page/page.html `notifyClaude`, review round 1 of lane page-wake-thread-full). Decision: questions-go-to-owner. Fix: none — owner decides. Until: 2026-11-25
- wake-undo-withdraw-silent: Undo and withdraw drop `notifyClaude`'s result, so a failed wake from them shows no line on the page (skills/waiting-page/page.html `undoSend` tell, `undoAsk` withdrawn notice). Main behaves the same. Decision: questions-go-to-owner. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-25
- wake-invalid-stored-id: a stored `waiting.thread` that the platform rejects as `invalid` is never replaced, because `invalid` is in `NO_FALLBACK`. So each later save shows "(invalid)" (skills/waiting-page/page.html `notifyClaude`). Main behaves the same. Decision: questions-go-to-owner. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-25
- wake-thread-kept-across-visits: the page keeps the wake thread id in localStorage across visits, where the comments contract says to keep a `threadId` for this visit only. One thread then fills to the comment limit (skills/waiting-page/page.html `notifyClaude`). Decision: questions-go-to-owner. Fix: none — owner decides. Until: 2026-11-25
- branch-rename-moves-worktrees: `git branch -m` on a branch that other worktrees have checked out moves their HEAD too. Their index and tree then sit on the old base, which shows as staged changes they never made. Two agents stopped on it in lane merge-guard-flag-from-paths. Push the new base under a new name instead. Rule: shared-trees-no-destructive-git.
- guard-path-test-folder: `merge --review-record` skips every path that `is_test_rel` calls a test, and that includes any folder named `test`, `tests`, `spec` or `testdata` at any depth. So a check at `actions/test/action.yml` or data at `hooks/testdata/rules.json` may say Guard or check `no`. No such file exists today (merge/merge.py `guard_hits`, review round 3 of lane merge-guard-flag-from-paths). `testing` is in TEST_DIRS (hooks/guard.py), so a `testing/` folder under a guard folder (`hooks/`, `lint/`, `bin/`, `actions/`, `.github/`, `merge/`, `githooks/`) also drops out of merge/merge.py `guard_hits` and lint/check_python_floor.py, which both read `is_test_rel`. No such folder exists today. The coordinator's option A (02:47 CST Nov 8) accepted that cost. The coordinator's pick (08:06 CST Nov 12) keeps it and files this note. Decision: pr-review-record.
- missing-count-message-untested: no case drops `changed_files` from the PR read, so the "no changed-file count" message of `Host.pr_files` has no test. Without the branch, the run still exits 1 with no record (merge/merge.py `Host.pr_files`, review round 3 of lane merge-guard-flag-from-paths). Decision: pr-review-record.
- record-names-token-forms: a double-backtick span drops its path, a trailing period inside backticks misses, and a path in a fenced code block counts (skills/plain-english-map/scripts/lib/decisions.mjs recordNaming). Decision: map-facts-are-checked.
- debt-notes-hard-coded: `NOT_RECORDS` hard-codes `notes.md`, where the layout kind `debt-notes` holds it (skills/plain-english-map/scripts/lib/decisions.mjs). Decision: one-layout-every-repo.
- map-nocode-heading-loose: SKILL.md says a non-code map line goes "under `## Code files`", but MAP1 accepts any heading except Tables and Screens (skills/plain-english-map/scripts/map-check.mjs MAP1, subLineEntries). Decision: map-facts-are-checked.
- map-copy-outside-clone-silent: a copy of the skill outside the clone has no `lint/layout.json` beside it, so it reads no debts and gives no notice (skills/plain-english-map/scripts/lib/decisions.mjs debtsDir). Decision: one-layout-every-repo.
- child-glossary-odd-forms: the child-glossary clash check reads only rows of a four-column table under `## Terms`, and strips ` (this sense)` only with its space. So a child term written `term(this sense)`, tagged another way such as `term (git)`, written as a link, in a 3-column table, or under another heading is not checked (lint/check_glossary.py `matches`, `numbered_rows`). Main's `child_spans` has the same holes (review rounds 1 and 2 of lane c146-glossary-redefines). Rule: speak-one-name-per-thing. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-25
- check-empty-design-data: `export_page.py --check` against a page whose `designData` holds none of an item's mockups prints `checks pass`, since a design the page does not hold passes (decision page-item-design). It takes a writer mistake: passing the template, not the `--write` output (review round 2 of lane page-check-at-publish). Decision: page-check-at-export.
- cleared-same-under-all: `_cleared` clears on `<waiting id>: same` even when another artifact of the same `--all` run is refused, unknown or crashed (hooks/feedback_guard.py `_cleared`). The waiting copy is in sync, so the Stop check holds. Decision: page-check-at-export.
- check-task-pictures: check_designs.mjs runs `pictureProblem` for any item kind, while the page calls it only for decisions (page.html `problem`). So a task item with a bad picture is held at export; the guard already refused it at write on main. Decision: page-check-at-export.
- pictured-items-why-dropped: `export_page.py` drops the reason `pictured_items` gives, so a page-less export passes with no line only when `question_form` fails to import. Decision: page-check-at-export.
- report-checks-block-once: after a report check blocks, the stop_hook_active turn is not read again, so a correction that still admits a mistake with no guard passes. The same holds for the Done-format and Departs checks (lint/report_guard.py `main`). Decision: mistake-means-missing-guard. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-25
- mistake-check-plain-fault-words: the mistake check in lint/report_guard.py refuses plain uses of a fault word that admit nothing, such as "I broke the batch into three lanes" or "I changed the test that was wrong". Parentheses and that/when clauses do not split a clause. Cost: one refusal per turn. Decision: mistake-means-missing-guard. Fix: none — a false alarm only refuses; one refusal per turn. Until: 2026-11-22
- author-basenames-requirements-all: `AUTHOR_BASENAMES` in hooks/guard.py lists degen's requirements-dev.txt but not requirements-all.txt, which holds the same dev pins. A builder can hand-edit it; degen's gen-check is the only catch (lane degen-mutation-install).
- degen-profile-comment-wording: the degen profile comment in hooks/mutate_guard.py says "reads"; "uses" is plainer, and "so the dev-only pins install too" is clearer (lane degen-mutation-install).
- windows-rest-72pct: checks-windows-rest's run 42283179251 took 20.2 of 28 min (72%, window of 2026-11-06; its slowest green run in the window is unmeasured) (measured 2026-11-11, gh run view 42283179251: checks-windows-rest success at 20.23 min; cap 28 at commit c627f73b9), past the merge tool's 70% warning (read merge/merge.py:1369) but under the 80% target (read docs/decisions/ci-job-caps.md:25). Decision: ci-job-caps.
- mistake-check-plain-fault-words: the mistake check in lint/report_guard.py refuses a plain use of a fault word that admits nothing, such as "I changed the test that was wrong" or "I broke the build into a new layout". Parentheses and that/when clauses do not split a clause; "broke ... into" passes only before a count or a parts noun ("into three lanes"), so "into pieces", "into groups" and "into halves" are refused. Cost: one refusal per turn. Decision: mistake-means-missing-guard. Fix: none — a false alarm only refuses; one refusal per turn. Until: 2026-11-22
- draft-landed-two-homes: the roadmap's idle-lane test checks a draft head on main by merge-base on the local checkout; the archive guard checks it by GitHub's compare. - undo-ignores-scroll: on the waiting page, under SENT_MOVE "sink", Undo restores the sent card's pre-Send viewport top even if the owner scrolled during the Undo window (skills/waiting-page/page.html holdView; PR #1373 review round 3). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-25
- reconcile-reorder-detach: the waiting page's reconcile keeps a kept card attached only while kept cards keep their relative order; a kept card moving down past others detaches the cards it passes (skills/waiting-page/page.html reconcile; PR #1373 review round 3; same as before the PR). The constant was renamed to `LETTERS`. A second such false alarm makes it a debt. Decision: waiting-data-in-repo. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-26
- prove-judge-dup-line-last-wins: a duplicate line entry in a reply keeps the last verdict (lint/prove_route_judge.py; merge/merge.py readable_verdicts shares it). Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-26
- prove-judge-span-overlap: a single-line row and a span row that start on the same line, in the same file and rev, share one judged text: the text of the row listed last (lint/prove_route_judge.py:84). Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-26
- prove-judge-label-typo-skipped: a label outside the known set is skipped, not refused. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-26
- prove-judge-all-unknown-rate: an all-failed run prints false alarms 0/N (0.0%) next to its unknown count. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-26
- prove-judge-one-error-line: one first error line is attached to every unknown, whatever its cause. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-26
- prove-judge-no-json-unknown: in the first real run, 4 rows came back unknown because the model result carried no JSON verdict; the group is not named. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-26
- prove-judge-excerpt-bound-bytes: the named-excerpt bound is 40 rows of 200 characters, not bytes. A 4-byte character gives about 800 B per row (the excerpt cut, lint/stale_cites.py:675-676). Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-26
- prove-judge-read-before-size-check: `read_backing` (merge/merge.py:2459) reads the whole file before the 256 KB size check (merge/merge.py:2504). That is bounded by repo file size, once per path per prompt build. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-26
- prove-judge-unknown-narrates-tools: 10 of the 34 scored rows came back unknown because the model narrated tool use ("I'll check whether the file..."), not a verdict. Candidate step: prompt wording that says no tools are available. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record). Fix: none — owner decides. Until: 2026-11-26
- after-merge-misses-claude-guard: after_merge starts its commands with subprocess.run directly (merge/merge.py:2163). So the test guard on decision_watch._nowindow.run misses a `claude` start from an afterMerge command. No test config has one now. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-26
- base-pin-interrupt-skips-ff: a KeyboardInterrupt or SystemExit in the base rev-parse before ff_main (merge/merge.py:2885-2889) skips ff_main, afterMerge and the cuts. The merge has landed by then. Fix: none — the window is one local rev-parse of milliseconds. An interrupt is as likely in ff_main's fetch right after, which skips the same afterMerge, ff and cut. A fix moves the window, not closes it. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record).
- merge-put-carries-no-base: a retarget between Host.merge's pr(n) read and the merge PUT still lands, since the merge API takes no base (merge/merge.py Host.merge). Fix: none — the API has no base field, and the window is one request.
- lane-lint-stop-no-log: lane_lint's non-liftable Stop (merge/merge.py) leaves out ci-job's log lines, so a timeout or an odd exit gives the reader no log path. The liftable branch prints them. Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job (the lane-check decision).
- draft-claim-line-no-ready-checks-test: the draft preview's claim line for a repo with no readyChecks has no test of its exact wording (merge/test_merge.py pins only "refuses"). Also on main. Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job (the lane-check decision). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-26
- override-that-off-once: `override that off` grants a one-act override on the keyword route, as on main (hooks/override.py grant). Decision: owner-override (the owner override). Fix: none — a word test for `off` near the start refused ordinary reasons such as `kick off the build`, and the grant is one act within 10 minutes.
- override-ask-any-length: a length anywhere in a judged plain override ask sets the timer. A bare number right after a first word `override` is minutes, so `override 3 files` with a judge yes grants 3 minutes (hooks/override.py _override_length). Decision: owner-override (the owner override). Fix: none — the judge reads the whole ask and must say yes first, and the cost of a misread is a timer of a length the owner typed. The keyword route grants nothing for `override 3 files` (hooks/override.py grant); a bare length such as `override 15` or `override 2h` reads the same on both routes.
- override-ask-judge-in-hook: the plain override ask's judge (up to 20 s) runs inside the 30 s prompt hook, so a hook kill writes no grant, and capture() has already run (hooks/local_time.py, hooks/call_judge.py). A judge no or error falls back to main's keyword route (hooks/override.py grant_override_ask): it grants what main grants. `yes override that` and `override 15m please` get none, and `override that for 15 minutes please` gets main's one-act grant. Decision: owner-override (the owner override).
- override-ask-seed-text-match: the cloud seed gate lets a prompt through when the seed record's text differs from the hook prompt; unmeasured (hooks/override.py _seed_record). Decision: owner-override (the owner override). Fix: none — the seed check is the Opus route's shared gate, unchanged here; measure it before a change.
- relayed-yes-names-pr-not-head: a relayed merge yes names the pull request, not its head, so a push after the yes still merges on green checks (settings.json "Relayed Merge Yes"). Same exposure as the standing merge grant. Decision: auto-mode-owner-acts
- relayed-merge-bare-form: the "Relayed Merge Yes" and "Batch Runner Merge" entries quote `~/.claude/bin/merge <pr> --confirm`; the refused form was recorded bare, `merge 1421 --confirm`. Becomes a debt on the first repeat refusal. Decision: auto-mode-owner-acts
- span-list-verb-limit: a unit that opens with a backticked verb reads as a list item when it follows a unit ending "`x`,". Example: "The reply lacks `Next`, `add` it." So `session_wording` does not flag it. The tool lists in `refusal_reason` need that exception. Decision: guard-remedy-not-to-owner (guard text never instructs through the owner).
- red-run-swap-loses-edits: a session shows new tests red by writing the old product file over the new one in its own worktree, since the guard refuses a worktree made by hand. A `git restore` after it then puts back the last commit, not the uncommitted edit. Seen 2026-11-07, lane windows-stdin-devnull: two edits to hooks/_nowindow.py were lost and re-applied. Commit before the swap.
- stamp-node-console-stdin: actions/stamp/stamp.mjs:934 starts a Node child with `stdio: "inherit"` and `windowsHide: true`, so on Windows the no-window child gets the console stdin that hooks/_nowindow.py now replaces for Python children (WinError 6 class). Not on the ci-job path. Seen 2026-11-07, PR #1420 review round 2. Decision: spawns-use-no-window.
- session-wording-closed-list: the imperative list in `session_wording` (lint/report_guard.py, `SESSION_IMPERATIVES` :222) is closed. These are not flagged: the connectors "or", "otherwise" and "just"; the verbs use, provide, explain, try, skip and avoid. Also not flagged: "Re-send" and a curly apostrophe "Don’t". Main passed these too. Decision: guard-remedy-not-to-owner (guard text never instructs through the owner).
- past-ruling-relay-order-flake: two PastRulingRelay git-count tests in lint/test_report_guard.py fail when lint/test_report_guard_remedy.py runs first in the same pytest process (order-dependent shared state). Each file passes alone, and the old head 2637fbd30 shows it too. Decision: test-practices (a flake is a defect report, P6).
- venv-in-main-checkout-unguarded: an agent made a Python venv in the main checkout (/home/user/claude-settings/scratchpad-venv) and no guard refused it. The other-checkout guard then refused its removal. Decision: an-agent-outside-its-home-tree-must-stop (each session writes only in its own checkout).
- conflict-markers-untracked-cap: the untracked-file read stops at 100 files, so a triplet in a file past the cap passes a whole-tree add (hooks/guard_merge.py `_untracked_findings`). Decision: commit-refuses-conflict-markers.
- conflict-markers-unresolved-add: an add path the guard cannot resolve counts as a whole-tree add, so an unrelated uncommitted triplet file refuses the commit (hooks/guard_merge.py `_unresolved`). Decision: commit-refuses-conflict-markers.
- conflict-markers-push-var-branch: `git push origin "$OTHER"` reads HEAD's range, so it misses a triplet on the other branch or refuses for HEAD's (hooks/guard_merge.py `_push_hit`). Decision: commit-refuses-conflict-markers.
- conflict-markers-loop-subshell: a commit inside a `for` loop and a push inside `( )` are not read (hooks/guard_merge.py `conflict_markers_hit`). Decision: commit-refuses-conflict-markers.
- conflict-markers-chain-clean-refuse: `git stash pop`, `git pull --rebase` or `git merge`, then commit and push in one call, is refused on a clean tree; the remedy is a separate push (hooks/guard_merge.py `_makes_conflict`). Decision: commit-refuses-conflict-markers.
- conflict-markers-show-uncapped: the push check runs one `git cat-file` per file with a marker finding, with no cap, and no cost case holds a finding (hooks/guard_merge.py `_kinds_of`). Decision: commit-refuses-conflict-markers.
- conflict-markers-stale-tracking: a tracking ref left at a markers tip after the remote was rewound hides those commits from `git push origin <branch>` (hooks/guard_merge.py `_push_hit`). Decision: commit-refuses-conflict-markers.
- two-worktrees-heredoc-cd: rule two-worktrees-one-branch reads a `cd` inside an interpreter heredoc body (`bash <<'EOF'`), so a later `git checkout -B x` is judged in the wrong tree (hooks/guard.py rule 1b2). Fix: track the tree on text with heredoc bodies cut. Decision: two-worktrees-one-branch (two worktrees never hold one branch).
- two-worktrees-cd-untested: no case covers the `cd` step of rule two-worktrees-one-branch, because other-checkout refuses the fixture's `cd` first; a mutant that drops it survives (hooks/guard.py rule 1b2). Decision: two-worktrees-one-branch (two worktrees never hold one branch).
- two-worktrees-subshell-cd: `(cd ../w && git status); git checkout -B x` and `cd ../w && cd - && git checkout -B x` pass, and `pushd ../w && git checkout -B x` refuses (hooks/guard.py rule 1b2). Decision: two-worktrees-one-branch (two worktrees never hold one branch).
- guard-log-short-head-cap: the guard.log short line with "its state is unknown." fits the 240-character cap only for reader paths up to about 159 characters (154 for ConnectionAbortedError); real reader paths run about 48 (hooks/daily_note.py). Decision: guard-log-read-weekly.
- written-time-file-path-read-twice: an ArtifactData items write through `file_path` is read twice, once by `_document_added` and once by question_form `_write_data`, with no stated reason (hooks/guard_merge.py `_document_added`). Fix: none — one extra local read, only on a rare call; no incident. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: local-time-central.
- written-time-file-path-edge-tests: no case pins a missing, non-JSON or relative `file_path`, or a `str_replace` on another field; round-2 probes show each passes (hooks/test_guard.py family message-time-matches-clock). Fix: none — each form was probed and passes; no incident. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: local-time-central.
- push-stray-default-literal: push-stray-branch stays silent only for an upstream literally named main or master, never the remote's own default branch, so a repo whose default has another name over-asks (hooks/guard_push_delete.py `push_ask_hit`). It narrows the retired debt push-head-mismatch-trap; ruling 206 a replaces it. Decision: plain-push-asks. Fix: none — it only over-asks. Every default read on 2026-11-08 is main (claude-settings, banchi, mailaudit, tile-windows). A fix adds a remote HEAD read to each push. Until: 2026-11-21.
- push-agent-autosetup-rare-forms: a bare push from an agent-worktree branch with no upstream writes that name but stays silent (hooks/guard_push_delete.py `push_ask_hit`). It happens under three configs: push.default upstream or tracking with push.autoSetupRemote true; a valueless push.autoSetupRemote; or a nonzero integer other than 1. Decision: plain-push-asks. Fix: none — each form needs a rare user config (rarity unmeasured). Until: 2026-11-21.
- live-stream-reads-first-as-follow: `live_stream_hit` reads the word `-First` after `tail` as the follow flag `-F`, so a prose line in a `python3 -` heredoc was refused (seen 2026-11-07 by lane bg-tail-loses-verdict) (hooks/guard_stream.py)
- test-rule-id-is-substring: the test runner matches an expected rule id as a substring of the reason. So a test that expects `check-tail` stayed green while the refusal printed its longer old id (hooks/test_guard.py)
- check-tail-pipe-at-line-end: a pipe at a line end with `tail` on the next line passes, and `narrated_tail_hit` has the same limit (hooks/guard_stream.py). Decision: background-check-through-verdict
- check-tail-no-nested-read: no nested read, so `bash -c '... | tail'`, and `{ ...; } | tail` both pass (hooks/guard_stream.py). `python3 bin/ci-job ... | tail` is now refused in the background. Decision: background-check-through-verdict
- check-tail-powershell-select: PowerShell `Select-Object -Last` and `-First` are not read as tail and head (hooks/guard_stream.py). Decision: background-check-through-verdict
- check-tail-uv-flag-value: `uv run --with verdict pytest | tail` and `uv run --project verdict pytest | tail` are refused, because `_verdict_pipe_in` (hooks/guard_stream.py) keeps the flag values of `uv run` in its reach, so `verdict` there counts as a verdict stage. That is a false alarm: main passes both, since its reach stops at `uv`. Decision: background-check-through-verdict. Fix: none — no package or directory named verdict exists, so Claude does not write these forms. Until: 2026-11-21
- narrated-tail-no-uv: `uv run merge --confirm | tail` passes, because the narrated rule (`live-stream`, `narrated_tail_hit`) reads through `uv run` only when the script path is spelled out: `uv run merge/merge.py --confirm | tail` is refused, and the bare `merge` word after `uv run` is not read (hooks/guard_stream.py). Decision: background-check-through-verdict
- check-tail-trailing-amp: a trailing `& echo $!` passes, while a plain `... | tail &` is already caught by `detached-launch` (hooks/guard_stream.py). Decision: background-check-through-verdict
- conflict-markers-cd-newline-false-alarm: `cd <missing> &&`, then a line break, then `git add -A && git commit`, is refused, though the shell skips the chain. The round 2 review of PR 1508 (the commit check judges each commit in its own folder) traced it to the empty segment `split_segments` emits after a newline, which resets the skip in `dir_before_segment` (hooks/guard_core.py, the `failed_cd_stays` path). Fix: skip empty segments before the reset. Unmeasured: a read of PR 1508's branch (commit 44821b598) finds `dir_before_segment` returns None for this form, so the commit is never judged and the refusal did not reproduce. Decision: commit-refuses-conflict-markers.
- conflict-markers-gaps-on-main: on main, `( cd <bad> && git add -A && git commit )` and `pushd <bad> && ...` from a clean cwd are allowed; `||` is read as sequential; `cd $VAR` gives no folder and fails open (hooks/guard_core.py `dir_before_segment`, hooks/guard_merge.py `conflict_markers_hit`). Decision: commit-refuses-conflict-markers.
- reviewer-no-session-tools-indented-tools-list: a `tools:` list wrapped onto indented lines that name mcp__claude-code-remote passes, because `unread_list` does not flag indented continuations (lint/check_agent_models.py session_tools_problem; hooks/tool_lists_test.py unread_list).
- reviewer-no-session-tools-duplicate-disallowed: a duplicate `disallowedTools:` key is read first-wins, but YAML keeps the last. Claude Code's reading is unmeasured (lint/check_agent_models.py session_tools_problem).
- reviewer-no-session-tools-scalar-first-quote: `scalar` cuts at the first closing quote, so `tools: 'Bash', 'mcp__claude-code-remote__create_session'` passes. That line is invalid YAML. Claude Code's reading is unmeasured (lint/check_agent_models.py scalar).
- reviewer-no-session-tools-star-tools-list: `tools: Bash, mcp__*` passes. The effect of `mcp__*` in `tools` is unmeasured (lint/check_agent_models.py session_tools_problem).
- reviewer-no-session-tools-fails-closed: the check fails closed, a false alarm, on a wrapped `disallowedTools`, a block-list form, or a block scalar (lint/check_agent_models.py session_tools_problem).
- reviewer-no-session-tools-own-copy-test: `SessionToolsRepo` checks real files against its own copy of the verdict (`server_withheld`), not against the check (lint/test_check_agent_models.py SessionToolsRepo).
- reviewer-no-session-tools-general-purpose-spawn: on 2026-11-07 15:42 CST, a general-purpose agent spawned in a cloud session held all 32 claude-code-remote tools, create_session among them. The risk is a role spawned as general-purpose, which the role guard already refuses for builder and reviewer work (hooks/guard.py ROLE_DEFAULT). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21.
- agents-no-remote-tools-outward-list-by-hand: `REMOTE_OUTWARD_TOOLS` is kept by hand. A new outward claude-code-remote tool stays open to agents until someone adds it, and no test sees it (hooks/shared_names.py REMOTE_OUTWARD_TOOLS).
- agents-no-remote-tools-override-untested: no case in hooks/test_guard.py proves the owner's override lifts rule `agent-no-remote-tools`. The code is right (hooks/guard.py, rule 8b: an override lifts it); the test is missing (family `agent-remote-tools` and helper `outward_case`, in `tests/guard/s29526-agent-remote-tools.py`).
- agents-no-remote-tools-merge-literals: hooks/guard_merge.py holds literal copies of four claude-code-remote tool names. This predates the lane (hooks/guard_merge.py TRUST_TOOLS, ARCHIVE_TOOL, BRIEF_TOOL).
- reviewer-no-session-tools-general-purpose-spawn: a spawn with no agents/ file (general-purpose, or a Workflow agent() with no agentType) keeps the claude-code-remote server, measured 2026-11-07 15:42 CST: a general-purpose agent in a cloud session held all 32 of its tools, create_session among them. Guard rule agent-no-remote-tools, in PR #1674 (stacked on this one), refuses those calls.
- reviewer-no-session-tools-star-tools-alone: `tools: *` passes (lint/check_agent_models.py session_tools_problem). The effect of `*` in `tools` is unmeasured.
- reviewer-no-session-tools-deny-unmeasured: nothing records that Claude Code honors the `mcp__claude-code-remote__*` deny on a spawned agent. The check (lint/check_agent_models.py session_tools_problem) proves only that the string is in the frontmatter. Measure it: after merge, spawn a reviewer and list its tools.
- test-author-merge-product-code: in a conflicted product file, a test-author can add product code beyond either side, and the guard cannot tell, so the reviewer reads it (hooks/guard.py `role_write_hit`, `role_offences`). Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests.
- test-author-merge-other-conflicts: a mixed merge that also conflicts in a workflow or manifest still has no role that can write it with the write tool: at commit, a test-author is logged, never refused (hooks/guard.py `merge_route_paths`). Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests.
- merge-route-merge-tree-options: merge-tree's default options may differ from the real merge's conflict set under `-X`, renames or an octopus merge (hooks/guard.py `merge_tree`). Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests.
- builder-merge-abort-discards-resolutions: `git merge --abort` in agents/builder.md discards a builder's uncommitted resolutions; this predates the merge route. Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests.
- janitor-mutation-not-guard-path: `janitor/mutate_sweep.py` runs in CI, but `janitor/` is not in `GUARD_PATHS`, so a lane may record Guard or check `no` for it. Same class as `lint-config-not-guard-path` (merge/merge.py `GUARD_PATHS`, review round 2 at head c03c81b55 of lane merge-guard-flag-from-paths). Fix: none — folded into the queued lane lint-config-guard-paths, which edits the same list. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: pr-review-record.
- delete-only-binary-replace: a binary file replaced in place has status `modified` and 0 additions, so `merge --review-record` passes it as delete-only. Main passes it too (merge/merge.py `kind_misfits`, review round 3 at head f1e6efbd2 of lane merge-guard-flag-from-paths). Fix: none — a lane rarely replaces a binary under Kind delete-only, and main passes it too. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: pr-review-record.
- archive-branch-unseen-name: the stranded-branch read misses a branch that is in no record's `external_metadata.current_branches` and is under no slug form (read hooks/guard_merge.py `_stranded_branch`). A 404 on a sources repo read (deleted, or unreadable by this gh login) refuses with a retry that never clears. Decision: session-tiers.
- env-cwd-prose-false-alarm: `echo "(cwd=.envs)"` and `python3 -c "print('x (cwd=.envs):')"` are refused on main, with `ENV_VARIABLE_REASON`: the guard reads `(cwd=.envs)` as a shell assignment that holds an env path (hooks/guard_env.py `_env_flat`). Nothing but decision prose tracked them. Decision: env-file-stays-unread.
- jq-lone-word-false-alarm: `jq --arg k v .environment f.json`, `jq --indent 2 .envs f.json`, `yq -o json .envs f.yaml`, a lone `jq .envs` and `cat f | jq .environment` are still refused. For jq and gojq a lone word is never a file (hooks/guard_env.py). Decision: env-file-stays-unread.
- cannot-claim-github-ui-reads-as-reach: "GitHub web UI" and "GitHub Actions" read as github-reach, because the github-reach pattern takes the word github after reach, use, access or connect to (lint/report_guard.py `CANNOT_ACTS`, the github-reach entry). Fix: none — a claim that GitHub cannot be reached is the same claim for the web UI or Actions, and no real reply has been read the other way. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-no-object-open: "I couldn't open it." is not read as a claim, because pr-open needs the word PR or pull request in the clause and "it" names no act (lint/report_guard.py `CANNOT_ACTS`, the pr-open entry). Fix: none — the line has no object to match an act, and a pronoun cannot be matched without a guess. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-comma-sentence-superlinear: a long run of comma-separated phrases in one sentence costs superlinear regex work. No comma ends the clause after a phrase, so each phrase reads the rest of the sentence (lint/report_guard.py `_claim`, `cannot_lines`). Measured 2026-11-08 at commit 9facc42f1: 0.27 s, 0.89 s and 3.44 s for 500, 1,000 and 2,000 phrases in one sentence, about 3.3x to 3.9x per doubling. The parser (lint/_clause.py) is linear; the check is not, because `_claim` reads the rest of the sentence once for each phrase. The note stays open with this measure. Fix: none — normal replies are unaffected, and a run of 500 claim phrases in one sentence is not written in normal work. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-blocker-window: a reason word skips a claim only when a blocker state starts within 60 characters of it. The part after the next semicolon skips on a bare blocker state anywhere in it (lint/report_guard.py `_blocker_res`, `_joined_blocker`). Fix: none — a longer reason clause, or a bare state after a semicolon that is about another act, is not written in normal work. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-bare-hold-grant: "hold is on" and "under the hold" skip a merge claim when a reason word opens them. "I can't merge PR 12 because it is under the hold." passes. They also skip when they come straight after a colon, a dash or a semicolon, or sit in parentheses. A bare "I can't merge PR 12 under the hold." blocks. "no grant" skips any table claim between the semicolons around it, through the rule-word check. So "no grant" in a clause about another matter also skips (lint/report_guard.py `_BLOCKER_STATES`, `_blocker_res`, `CANNOT_RULE_RE`). Fix: none — a claim line that names a hold or a grant states a true blocker in every reply read so far. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-lead-join-past-cause: a blocker state just before the claim skips it whatever came first, so "X failed; CI is red; I could not merge PR 12." passes (lint/report_guard.py `_blocker_res`, `_joined_blocker`). Fix: none — the line still states a true blocker of the act, and a reply that names an outage and a blocker in one sentence is rare. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-act-word-script-try: a failed script whose name holds an act word but runs another act, such as merge-readiness.sh or no-push-lint.sh, counts as a try of that act. It backs a claim only when the line names it (lint/report_guard.py `CANNOT_SCRIPT_ACTS`, `_backed`). Fix: none — the name rule cannot tell those scripts apart without a guess, and the line must name the script and its error. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-wrong-rule-id-passes: a real rule or decision id in parentheses skips the claim even when that rule does not stop the act (lint/report_guard.py `_names_rule_id`). Fix: none — the check reads whether the id exists, not what it rules, and a wrong cite is a review matter. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-no-rule-files-skips-silently: when neither checkout has a readable CLAUDE.md and output style, any slug of two or more hyphens in parentheses skips the claim, and no UNKNOWN line says so (lint/report_guard.py `_names_rule_id`). Fix: none — an install with no readable rule files breaks every session first, and the slug still has to sit in the claim clause. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-non-pr-object-outside-list: a merge, open, close, comment or update whose object is outside CANNOT_NON_PR_OBJECTS still reads as a PR act. So "I can't merge the changelog." and "I can't update the dashboard for PR 12." block once with no failed try, and so does "I am not allowed to merge the changelog." The list holds file, config, doc, log, diff, tag and readme, the objects read in real replies (lint/report_guard.py `_window_act`, `_non_pr_object`, `CANNOT_NON_PR_OBJECTS`). Fix: none — a longer list guesses at the sense of nouns. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-pr-possessive-object-no-claim: a claim whose object is a file, config, doc, log, diff, tag or readme is no PR claim, even when the clause names a PR. Both of these pass with no try: "I can't update the docs for PR 12." and "I can't open the PR's CI logs." (lint/report_guard.py `_non_pr_object`, `CANNOT_NON_PR_OBJECTS`; lint/test_report_guard_cannot.py pins both lines). Harm: low, a missed flag only; the line is about a document, and no owner step follows from it. Fix: none — the tests pin both lines from earlier false alarms, and a PR in the clause does not make the object a PR. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-backing-by-act-cancel-by-target: a failed try is kept per act, not per PR, so it backs a claim about any other target, since a line rarely names its target. An open failure also keeps the past-tense rescue off, so the rescue needs every failure of the act cancelled. A create is cancelled only by a success for its own --head, else for the branch an earlier checkout or switch in its command moved to. So a success for b2 leaves the failed create for b1 open. A create with neither is keyed by its own arguments, so only an identical retry cancels it. A merge or other gh pr call with no PR number or URL is the same. A PR number is qualified by its --repo, so a success on another repo cancels nothing. Only a gh pr act verb (create, merge, close, comment, ready or edit) with no arguments has an empty key and cancels nothing. A bare view, status, checks or list cancels as any call does (lint/report_guard.py `_gh_target`, `_segment_tries`, `failed_tries`). Fix: none — a claim line rarely names its target, so there is nothing to match. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-url-vs-number-target: a failed `gh pr merge 12` and a later merge of the same PR by its URL are two targets, so the success does not cancel the failure. "I could not merge PR 12 at first." stays blocked when the failed call named no --repo. The no-repo case still blocks; only a number plus the same --repo as the URL cancels it (lint/report_guard.py `_gh_target`). Harm: one false alarm. Fix: none — a recorded limit: a number with no --repo never matches a URL, since the repo is unknown (lane ruling, 2026-11-08, before 11:00 CST, process-only), and the cannot-claim follow-up that wrote this note does not change it. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-quoted-line-read-as-claim: a line quoted with `>` is read as the writer's claim (lint/report_guard.py `_prose_lines`, `QUOTE_PREFIX`). Fix: none — reports are quoted with `>` in this repo, so skipping quoted lines would skip real claims. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-comma-so-join-blocks: a blocker joined to the claim by a comma or by "and so" still blocks once, as in "CI failed on the lint step, so I can't merge" and "CI is red and so I can't merge" (lint/report_guard.py `_joined_blocker`, `_clause.opens_with`). Fix: none — the line is rewritten once with its try or a cite, and a comma join of a true blocker is rare. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-script-name-suffix: a backticked word or a dotted suffix can name a failed script, as in "open-pr.sh.bak" or "`pr merge`" (lint/report_guard.py `_script_tries`, `_backed`). Fix: none — Claude is not likely to write a line that names a script by such a form. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-script-recovery-by-act: a later success cancels a failed script try only by key, the script's name and its first plain argument (lint/report_guard.py `_script_tries`, `_segment_tries`, `failed_tries`). So a failed `./create-pr.sh b1`, then a passing `./open-pr.sh b1`, leaves "I could not open the PR for b1." blocked, since the line names no script try. A past claim that names the failed script still passes: "I could not open the PR for b1: `create-pr.sh` exited 1." passes after the open succeeded, because the success has another key and the named script backs the line (`_backed`). Harm: low, a missed flag only; the line names a true failed call and its error. Fix: none — a stale past claim that names its failed script is rare in normal work. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-bare-merge-exited-names-no-script: "I couldn't merge PR 12: merge exited 1." with no backticks and not "the merge tool" names no script try, so it blocks (lint/report_guard.py `_backed`, `CANNOT_MERGE_TOOL_RE`). Fix: none — the word merge sits in every merge claim, so prose alone cannot name the tool. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-two-capability-homes: `_clause.CANT_FORMS` (three forms) opens `_clause.CANNOT_FORMS`, which `CANNOT_RE` (the claim check) reads in full. `CAPABILITY_RE` (the hand-off check) reads CANT_FORMS and adds its own tail: refuse, refuses, refused, denied, forbidden, no access, unavailable, 403, blocked and not available (unless until follows), and the `isn't` and `not` forms of allowed and permitted. It also reads `_clause.COULD_NOT_FORMS`, the reject forms and 401, which the claim check's skip (`CAPABILITY_SKIP_RE`, the tail without those) does not, so a could-not line stays in the claim check. So a Why yours line with a phrase in one check's list only is judged by that check alone (lint/report_guard.py `CAPABILITY_RE`, `CAPABILITY_SKIP_RE`, `CANNOT_RE`). Fix: none — the claim check judges a Why yours line that the hand-off check neither judges nor exempts, so no line goes unread. One shared list would change the hand-off check's verdict on lines it passes today. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-reason-needs-a-verb: a reason with no verb, such as "a protected branch", names no check, so the line blocks once. A word in -ed with no determiner before it reads as a verb, so "protected branch rules" passes as a reason (`_clause.is_reason`, `_clause.VERBS`, `_clause.DETERMINERS`, lint/_clause.py). Fix: none — a reason in normal replies is a clause of three words with a verb, and an -ed adjective with no determiner is not written as one. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-next-sentence-reason-unrelated: with a failed try of the act, any next sentence of three words with a verb counts as the line's reason, even when it is about another matter (lint/report_guard.py `_gives_reason`). Fix: none — the failed try still backs the claim, and the reason only names the check. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-because-ci-failed: "I can't merge PR 12 because CI failed." blocks, though CI failing is a true reason. The blocker states list is, are, was or were red, failing, running or pending, and no failed word (lint/report_guard.py `_BLOCKER_TAIL`). Fix: none — the coordinator ruled this phrasing back to the earlier check (2026-11-08 04:31 CST), and a line that names its failed try or a cite passes. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-as-ci-is-red: "I can't merge PR 12 as CI is red." blocks, though CI red is a true reason. The reason words are because, while, since, as long as and unless; the word as is left out (lint/report_guard.py `_blocker_res`, the reason words). Fix: none — the coordinator ruled this phrasing back to the earlier check (2026-11-08 04:31 CST), and a line that names its failed try or a cite passes. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-ci-red-and-claim: "CI is red and I can't merge PR 12." blocks, though CI red is a true reason. A state joins the claim only by a semicolon, a colon, a dash, a so comma or parentheses, and not by and (lint/report_guard.py `_joined_blocker`). Fix: none — the coordinator ruled this phrasing back to the earlier check (2026-11-08 04:31 CST), and a line that names its failed try or a cite passes. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-ci-has-failed: "I can't merge PR 12 because CI has failed." blocks, though a failed CI is a true reason. The blocker states list has no has-failed form (lint/report_guard.py `_BLOCKER_TAIL`). Fix: none — the coordinator ruled this phrasing back to the earlier check (2026-11-08 04:31 CST), and a line that names its failed try or a cite passes. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-open-noun-and-pr-reads-as-open: "I could not open the CI logs and PR.", "I could not open the branch and PR." and "I could not open an issue and PR." read as pr-open claims, because no and or or ends the object window (lint/report_guard.py `CANNOT_ACTS`, the pr-open entry). Fix: none — the PR is an object of the open in the last two lines, and telling "the CI logs and PR" from them needs the sense of the words (coordinator ruling 2026-11-08 04:31 CST). Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-unlisted-ly-adverbs: an adverb that the adverb list does not hold and that does not end in -ly, such as "I often cannot push.", breaks the subject, so the line reads as no claim (lint/_clause.py `own_subject`, `ADVERB_PATTERN`). Any -ly adverb is read now, so "I honestly cannot push." is a claim. Fix: step:clause-parser-claims — the clause parser reads the subject past any adverb, not only -ly ones. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-reason-before-phrase: a reason before the phrase counts only when a comma, a semicolon, a colon or a dash ends it. A but after it makes it a contrast, so it counts for nothing (lint/report_guard.py `_gives_reason`, lint/_clause.py `reasons_before`). After a failed push, three lines still block once: "Because the branch is protected I cannot push.", "The branch is protected so I cannot push." and "The branch is protected but I cannot push." Harm: low, a false alarm of one block. The line passes once it names its check after the phrase, or sets the reason off with a comma, a semicolon or a colon. Fix: none — a clause with no break before the phrase holds the reason and the claim together, and a normal reply sets the reason off. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-owner-gate-needs-a-rule-id: a claim that an owner gate stops the act passes when its clause holds a real rule or decision id in parentheses (`_names_rule_id`). The same claim also passes when the part of its sentence between semicolons names a rule, ruling, decision or grant (`CANNOT_RULE_RE`). So "I can't merge PR 12: it's protected." with no failed try blocks. "I can't merge PR 12: it needs your grant (git-main-only-by-pr)." passes by the word grant, not the id: the sentence skip returns before `_names_rule_id` reads the id (lint/report_guard.py). The skip is pinned by `test_cc_red_r4_rule_forms_and_the_cannot_suite_pass` (lint/test_report_guard_cannot.py). Fix: none — a sentence that names a rule states the rule, so it is not the session's claim (the comment on `CANNOT_RULE_RE`). A line with neither and no failed try gets the try first, as any claim does. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-owner-gate-colon-clause: "I can't merge PR 12: it's locked (guard-guards-mistakes-only)." blocks with no failed try, since the clause ends at the colon (lint/_clause.py `clauses`) and `_names_rule_id` reads only that clause, so the id after the colon is never read. Harm: low, a false alarm of one block on a true gate. Fix: none — low harm, and the line passes once the id sits before the colon: "I can't merge PR 12 (git-main-only-by-pr): it's locked." Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-http-status-needs-error-result: an HTTP 4xx or 5xx counts as a failure only in an error result, or in a relay's text (lint/report_guard.py `failed_tries`, `CANNOT_RELAY_FAIL_RE`, `_is_relay`). (a) After `curl -i` or `curl -v` exits 0 with "HTTP/2 403" in its output, two claims that passed before this change now block: a reach claim ("I cannot reach GitHub: `curl` returned HTTP/2 403.") on any github.com or api.github.com URL, and an open-PR claim ("I cannot open the PR: `curl` returned HTTP/2 403."), only when the curl hits api.github.com's pulls route (a PR claim on a page URL was blocked before too). Both are new false alarms, by design: a status in the output of a call that worked is no failure. (b) A relayed agent result that worked, quotes "HTTP 500" and names a route still backs a claim of that route's act, though the status may be about another call. A tighter fix would need the status and the route in one sentence of the relay. Harm: low, two false alarms in (a) and a missed flag in (b). Fix: none — low harm, and guards stop Claude's mistakes, not an attacker (decision `guard-guards-mistakes-only`). Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-open-adjective-reads-as-act: `_prose_acts` (lint/report_guard.py) reads the adjective open as the act pr-open. So after a failed merge, "PR 12 is open, but its checks failed. I cannot merge it." blocks: the sentence before the claim reads as about another act, so its check word names no check of the merge. "PR 12 has checks that failed. I cannot merge it." passes. Harm: low, a false alarm of one block; the line passes once it names its check in its own sentence. Fix: step:clause-parser-claims — the clause parser reads open as a verb only where it governs the PR. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-reason-verb-missing-forms: `CANNOT_VERB_RE` (lint/report_guard.py, read by `_is_reason`) lists isn't, aren't, wasn't, doesn't, didn't and won't, but not weren't, haven't, hasn't, can't or couldn't. So after a failed merge, "I couldn't merge PR 12: the reviews weren't in." blocks, and "I couldn't merge PR 12: the review wasn't in." passes. Harm: low, a false alarm of one block. Fix: none — the line passes once its reason says wasn't, aren't, cannot or could not. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-this-session-spaced-be-verb: `CANNOT_SUBJECT_RE` allows no be-verb after "this session" or "the cloud". So "This session is not able to merge PR 12." and "This session is unable to merge PR 12." read as no claim, with no failed try. Base 6fc84e0b0 has the same subject words. "This session isn't able to merge PR 12." blocks, so the spaced form is the gap. Harm: a missed flag, since an inability claim with no try passes. Fix: none — the gap is outside this diff, because base 6fc84e0b0 has it too. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- resolve-vars-quote-injection: `X='x"y'; Y='u"v'; echo $X ; cp a TGT ; echo $Y` hides a copy through `_resolve_vars` (hooks/guard_frozen.py). Fix: none — attacker-only form (guard-guards-mistakes-only). Until: 2026-11-21.
- cannot-claim-open-adjective-reads-as-act: `_prose_acts` (lint/report_guard.py) reads the adjective open as the act pr-open. So after a failed merge, "PR 12 is open, but its checks failed. I cannot merge it." blocks: the sentence before the claim reads as about another act, so its check word names no check of the merge. "PR 12 has checks that failed. I cannot merge it." passes. Harm: low, a false alarm of one block; the line passes once it names its check in its own sentence. Fix: step:clause-parser-claims, follow-up 5 (this lane, after #2058) — the clause parser reads open as a verb only where it governs the PR. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-reason-verb-missing-forms: `_clause.VERBS` (lint/_clause.py, read by `_clause.is_reason`) lacks don't, haven't, hasn't, can't and couldn't, and the modals will, would, should, could, must, might, shouldn't and wouldn't. A reason whose only verb is one of those reads as no reason. So after a failed merge, "I couldn't merge PR 12: the reviewers don't agree." blocks, and "I couldn't merge PR 12: the reviewers do not agree." passes. "it hasn't started" passes only through the -ed rule. Harm: low, a false alarm of one block. Fix: none — the line passes once its reason uses a form in `_clause.VERBS`, such as do not, cannot or could not. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-lists-without-one-home-pin: `_BACK` and `_STATUS` (lint/report_guard.py) read constants from lint/_clause.py, and so do CANNOT_FORMS and REASON_LINKS (`CANNOT_RE`, `_claim`). lint/test_clause.py pins the value of REASON_LINKS (`test_reason_links_are_the_words_that_open_a_reason`) and the six basic CANNOT_FORMS forms (`test_cannot_forms_hold_the_basic_forms`). No test names _BACK or _STATUS, and no test pins the names report_guard reads to lint/_clause.py, so a local copy in report_guard.py would not fail it. Harm: low, since drift is possible and none is seen. Fix: none yet. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-access-to-github: "I don't have access to GitHub.", "I don't have access to the gh CLI." and "I have no access to GitHub." pass with no try. "I don't have access to push." blocks. The first two match the form "don't have access to". The window after it holds only the object (GitHub, the gh CLI). `CANNOT_ACTS` reads an act from a verb at the window's start. The github-reach entry needs a verb such as reach or use before GitHub, so no act is read (lint/report_guard.py `_window_act`). "have no access to" is in no CANNOT_FORMS entry (lint/_clause.py), so no phrase is read. "push" is a verb, so the push act is read. Fix: none, no incident yet, and the same at the base commit (4e6d3dbf3). Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-pr-label-bare-phrase: "- PR 2058: blocked from merging by the net-value hold." blocks with no try on PR 2058 (branch cannot-claim-follow-up-4; main passes it). The phrase after a "PR N:" label reads as the writer's own claim. A false alarm, one rewrite. Fix: none yet. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-writer-position-doc: on PR 2309, the skips paragraph of docs/decisions/claims-carry-backing.md says "this session" and "the cloud" are the writer "when they start the clause". The code reads any position unless a preposition sits just before it ("The builder said this session cannot push." claims push). Fix: make the doc say what the code does. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-have-tried-auxiliary: on PR 2309, `WRITER_BEFORE_RE` in lint/_clause.py allows only an adverb between "I" or "we" and the check word. So "I have tried; I cannot push." passes after a failed try, as it does on main. Fix: allow have, has and had, or narrow the doc to "I tried". Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- clause-parser-doc-reasons-before-fields: docs/decisions/checks-share-the-clause-parser.md says callers read only start, join, lead, lead_at, lead_end and hard_end. On PR 2309, `reasons_before` in lint/_clause.py also reads end, conditional and join. Fix: correct the doc. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: checks-share-the-clause-parser (the clause parser record).
- cannot-claim-call-subject-scope: on PR 2309, with a failed try, a speech word names a check only after tool, hook, check, command or remote (`CALL_SUBJECTS`, lint/_clause.py). So "The hook output said I cannot push." and "The tool call said I cannot push." block once. This is ruled scope; the cost is one rewrite. Fix: none. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- cannot-claim-stale-test-comment: lint/test_report_guard_cannot.py has a comment naming a symbol that no longer exists (a reviewer found it near the end of the file). Fix: re-point it. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record, part (vi)).
- why-cited-asks-row-any-act: a page task's `(route r-asks)` cite passes whatever act the task names, since no row is matched to the act (lint/report_guard.py `_route_verdict`). Fix: step:why-cite-matches-act. Decision: claims-carry-backing.
- why-cited-wrapped-line: a Why yours line wrapped onto a second line passes, since only the label line is judged (hooks/question_form.py `why_cited_hit`). Decision: task-names-its-check.
- why-cited-cite-form-false-alarms: true cites in a backtick id, an upper-case id, an ISO datetime, "measured on <date>" or on the next line are refused, on the page and in chat alike (lint/report_guard.py cite regexes). Decision: claims-carry-backing.
- why-cited-red-no-fragment: the task-why-cited red cases assert the rule only, not the matched cause, and no case covers a wrapped line, a backtick id or an ISO date (hooks/test_question_form.py). Decision: test-practices.
- why-cited-fragment-dated-first: `cite_tokens_verdict` does not stop on a dated measured cite as `why_yours_verdict` does, so "(measured <date>, x) (route r-works)" refuses as a str_replace fragment but passes as a whole line; errs safe (lint/report_guard.py). Decision: task-names-its-check.
