- why-cited-mutants-no-page-suite: mutate_guard REPORT_TARGETS runs no suite with hooks/test_question_form.py for lint/report_guard.py, so `cite_tokens_verdict` mutants show as survived (hooks/mutate_guard.py). Decision: test-practices.
- why-cited-gate-substring-copy: the `"(route"` and `"(measured"` substring gate in hooks/question_form.py `why_cited_hit` restates the cite regexes' openers. Decision: task-names-its-check.
- session-wording-mutants-survive: the remedy suite misses these mutants of `session_wording` (lint/report_guard.py). It misses dropping ":" as a unit end, the apostrophe in `_WORD`, and the connectors also, first and next. It also misses the modals need to, needs to, have to and ought to, and 11 of the 12 SESSION_PHRASES. Only "would reach the owner" has a control. Decision: guard-remedy-not-to-owner (guard text never instructs through the owner).
- correction-step-copy-in-stalled-test: lint/test_report_guard_stalled.py (line 387) holds its own capitalised copy of the correction-step phrases, while CORRECTION_STEP (lint/test_report_guard.py, line 41) is lower case, so the copy can drift. Point the stalled test at CORRECTION_STEP and compare the lowered reason. Decision: guard-remedy-not-to-owner (guard text never instructs through the owner).
- homes-line-reads-cwd-only: `Homes.homes_line` (hooks/feedback_guard.py) lists the kind homes of the Stop's cwd repo only. `_locate` now judges a home in the sidecar's repo too, so the reason text can name homes of the wrong repo. Decision: owner-feedback-inbox.
- override-config-dir-fallback-copy: hooks/override.py, hooks/precompact_handoff.py and hooks/sim_boot_record.py each keep a `config_dir` fallback. It is a copy of `shared_names.config_dir`, in the ImportError branch. A hook must run when shared_names.py is absent or stale, as it is when an install lands the hook before its sibling. hooks/test_shared_names.py `case_config_dir_copies_sit_only_in_the_import_fallback` refuses a copy outside that branch. Known limit: hooks/daily_note.py has no fallback, so it skips the note while shared_names.py is absent. Retire when installs land hooks and shared_names.py together.
- once-home-standing-word-list: `STANDING` (hooks/feedback_guard.py) is a word list, so a standing grant worded outside it ("without asking me", "until I say stop", "as they come in", "permanently") passes with a `once:` home. Harm is small: the grant is not recorded durably, so a later session asks again. Decision: owner-feedback-inbox. Fix: none — rare and harmless (a grant worded outside the list is stored only as one-shot, so a later session asks again). Until 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-ts-tests-unlisted: Node 22.18+ also collects `.test.ts`, `.mts` and `.cts` files, which `mod-tests.sh list` (skill_files) does not list and TEST_FILE does not flag. No such test file exists (.github/scripts/mod-tests.sh). Decision: none. Fix: none — no .ts test file exists, and the skill writes its tests as .mjs. Until: 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-pattern-cells-untested: no fixture holds a `-test` or `.cjs` file, so dropping either pattern from skill_files stays green (lint/test_windows_split.py CiCoverageCollector). Decision: none. Fix: none — Claude names its tests .test.mjs or test_*, never -test or .cjs. Until: 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-exact-mod-cmd: only a run line equal to MOD_CMD counts the collector's files, so a split into `mods` and `skill` steps reads as every mod test missing, a loud false alarm (lint/test_windows_split.py `runs_collector`). Decision: none. Fix: none — the false alarm is loud and names each file, and all three steps match today. Until: 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-rename-escapes: renaming a real test to `_test_*` takes it out of CiCoverage's scope; the lint cannot tell it from a helper (lint/test_windows_split.py TEST_FILE). Decision: none. Fix: none — a rename to dodge the check is deliberate, not a mistake (guard-guards-mistakes-only). Until: 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-off-pr-shard: a file named only in a job that skips pull_request (a shard job such as checks-windows-guard-2) reads as covered but gates no PR (lint/test_windows_split.py CiCoverage). Decision: none. Fix: none — each off-PR shard runs a suite that a pull-request job also runs. Until: 2026-11-21.
- stale-cites-value-top-level-only: py-facts `assigned` reads top-level Name targets only, so a constant in a module-level `if`, or a tuple target, makes a value cite of it a false alarm or a miss. STALE002's `names_in` reads into if and try. Decision: stale-cites-at-merge. Step: value-cites-nested-binds
- stale-cites-value-span-wraps: a code span wrapped across a line break is missed, since a span is read one line at a time. Decision: stale-cites-at-merge. Step: value-cites-wrapped-span
- restore-staged-no-guard: a builder ran git restore --staged in its own worktree; it only unstages, nothing is lost. Fix: none — rare and harmless
- verdict-pipe-lookup-redirect: `command -v verdict 2>/dev/null | head` is refused, because a redirect token hides the lookup from `_is_lookup` (hooks/guard_stream.py `verdict_pipe_hit`). Decision: verdict-pipe-refused.
- verdict-pipe-paren-unpinned: no case or mutant pins the glued-paren read that refuses `(verdict x | tail)` (hooks/guard_stream.py `verdict_pipe_hit`). Decision: verdict-pipe-refused.
- stale009-idiom-false-alarms: STALE009 fires on idioms such as "tool calls", "calls for" and "check runs" (docs/decisions/hook-inputs-carry-token-counts.md:64). Round 1 of #1539 sampled 10 of 226 full-tree hits: 8 real, 1 borderline, 1 false (lint/stale_cites.py `CODE_CLAIM`). Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record).
- stale009-misses-subject-forms: STALE009 misses plural subjects ("hooks read"), a backticked noun ("The `ledger` reads"), an anchored file subject ("`hooks/guard.py:12` refuses"), and the verbs blocks, allows and sends (lint/stale_cites.py `CODE_CLAIM`). Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record).
- stale009-unknown-path-untested: STALE009's unknown line for a .mjs, .js, .cjs, .ts or .sh cite has no test. Round 1 of #1539 ran it in memory only. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record).
- red-run-shared-helper-copy: a changed tracked helper that a new test imports by name (lint/_test_git.py, testing/*.py) cannot be copied into the red checkout under a new name. An overwrite leaves it dirty, so the remove is refused and the checkout stays (agents/test-author.md red-run paragraph). Decision: when-a-worktree-may-be-removed
- red-run-trailer-names-copy: the Red-run trailer's command names the renamed red copy (test_x_red.py), which exists nowhere in the repo, so a replay of the trailer fails; nothing replays it now (agents/test-author.md). Decision: red-run-trailer-on-test-commits
- mutate-shard-pop-unpinned: no test pins the MUTATE_SHARD pop in hooks/mutate_guard.py run_suite (:2888). hooks/test_mutate_shared.py pins only the pop in run_suite_file. PR #1466. Decision: ci-job-caps (each CI job's cap leaves its slowest run under 80%).
- sweep-reads-mutate-shard: janitor/mutate_sweep.py calls mutate_shared.run_mutants (:1602), and run_mutants reads MUTATE_SHARD from the environment (hooks/mutate_shared.py:295). A MUTATE_SHARD set in a dev shell shards the sweep. PR #1466. Decision: ci-job-caps (each CI job's cap leaves its slowest run under 80%).
- shard-caps-no-provisional-tag: the -b and -c job caps in .github/workflows/checks.yml (:1226, :1250) have a "Provisional" comment but no `# provisional` tag. The new-job caps at :577 and :1023 have the tag. No check on main reads the tag. PR #1466. Decision: ci-job-caps (each CI job's cap leaves its slowest run under 80%).
- shard-times-unmeasured: the mutate shard step times are unmeasured, so the 60 min caps on the -b and -c jobs stay provisional until 5 green runs (.github/workflows/checks.yml :1224). PR #1466. Decision: ci-job-caps (each CI job's cap leaves its slowest run under 80%).
- ack-docstring-lags-asks: the last_incoming_index docstring (lint/_transcript.py :296-300) says a peer message after a send is an ack that starts no turn. A later sentence says a message that asks starts a turn. PR #1451. Decision: questions-go-to-owner (questions go to the owner).
- ack-frame-fallback-untested: no test covers the frame fallback in peer_body (lint/_transcript.py :277-290), which reads the text from the message frame when origin.body is missing. PR #1451. Decision: questions-go-to-owner (questions go to the owner).
- report-guard-lazy-import: lint/report_guard.py :952 imports peer_body inside _turn_acts. The module already imports from _transcript at :133. PR #1451. Decision: questions-go-to-owner (questions go to the owner).
- alias-allow-mutants-unpinned: the import-alias reader has no allow-side mutants pinned. The T11 header in skills/plain-english-map/tests/tested-by.test.mjs (:39) names two mutants, T11c and T11c2, and both are fail-side. PR #1472. Decision: map-tested-by-line (the map names a file's tests on a checked tested-by line).
- t11b-label-reused: the label T11b names a test in skills/plain-english-map/tests/fact-lines.test.mjs (:802) and another in tested-by.test.mjs (:500). PR #1472. Decision: map-tested-by-line (the map names a file's tests on a checked tested-by line).
- join-literal-reads-as-path: on PR #1462 (branch map11-path-joins), MAP11 (the map check on tested-by lines) reads an all-literal path join, os.path.join("a", "b"), as the string "a/b". The coordinator kept this so the join form and the string form agree (ruling 2026-11-07 15:51 CST, pick A). Six temp-tree fixture paths then count as loads: lint/test_check_claude_md_size.py -> .claude/CLAUDE.md; lint/test_repo_colour.py -> .claude/layout-extras.json; lint/test_rule_audit.py -> hooks/guard.py and hooks/config_watch.py (fixture dict keys); lint/test_check_agent_models.py -> docs/decisions/agent-model-cap.md; lint/test_retired_repos.py -> docs/design/repo-colours.json. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: map-tested-by-line (the map names a file's tests on a checked tested-by line).
- split-sentence-eight-copies: the worktree-isolation split sentence is copied into 8 agent files; its one home could be COMMON in hooks/agent_brief.py, and claude-code-guide gets no copy (agents/*.md). Decision: none
- split-sentence-command-term: the split sentence says "command" for a whole shell call, which GLOSSARY.md names a shell call (agents/*.md). Decision: none
- designer-context-test-one-kind: the designer context case accepts informed or directed, so a sentence that names only one kind stays green (hooks/test_blind_brief.py near line 571). The harm is a refused call, not an unsafe act. Decision: design-skills
- answered-reason-test-phrase-only: the reason-text case asserts only the phrase "inline code span", so a reason that invites a claim into a code span stays green (lint/test_report_guard.py near line 4314). Decision: answered-needs-source
- verdict-zero-unittest-pass: bin/verdict reads no unittest "Ran N tests" count, so a run by path of a TestCase file with no __main__ block reads PASS with zero tests run. No such test file exists now (bin/verdict). Decision: none
- credential-words-colon-residual: the `credential-name` shape lets a `:` form letters-only value that reads as words pass, such as `SECRET_KEY: supersecretpassword`, and about 3.6% of random 16-letter lower-case strings (PR #1460 round 3 sample, 200,000 strings); main refused them (skills/waiting-page/export_page.py `credential_is_no_secret`). Fix: none — Claude rarely writes a real secret as plain lower-case words after a credential label, and round 3 judged the net value zero. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: waiting-data-in-repo.
- map-dirname-dotdot-place: `dirname` of a name bound to text ending in `..` returns the parent of the normalized place. The shell returns HERE. So with `UP="$HERE/.."` and `DIR=$(dirname "$UP")`, `$DIR/x` resolves from the wrong folder. Code: skills/plain-english-map/scripts/lib/fact-readers.mjs:254, PR #1625 (branch map11-shell-binds). Decision: map-tested-by-line (the tested-by line decision).
- map-dirname-root-dot: `dirname` of a name resolved to the repo root returns "." where the real parent is outside the repo. This holds for `$(cd "$HERE/.." && pwd)`, `realpath` and `readlink -f`. It is a false load only when the tail names a tracked root path. Code: skills/plain-english-map/scripts/lib/fact-readers.mjs:254, PR #1625 (branch map11-shell-binds). Decision: map-tested-by-line (the tested-by line decision).
- map-rebind-forms-unseen: a bind after `if`, `elif`, `while`, `until` or `!` is not seen. Neither is a second name after `export` or `readonly`, `A=1 ROOT=/tmp`, or `printf -v`, `mapfile`, `getopts`, `let` or `eval`. So the name keeps its first place. A bind inside an earlier value, such as `X=$(ROOT=/tmp; ..)`, is seen. Code: skills/plain-english-map/scripts/lib/fact-readers.mjs:263-282, PR #1625 (branch map11-shell-binds). Decision: map-tested-by-line (the tested-by line decision).
- map-declare-later-name-voids: a later name on a `declare`, `typeset` or `local` line voids the name at any place, even the same place. The record keeps a same-place bind. The void is conservative: it can only miss a load, which is a false fail. Code: skills/plain-english-map/scripts/lib/fact-readers.mjs:266-271, PR #1625 (branch map11-shell-binds). Decision: map-tested-by-line (the tested-by line decision).
- map-shellloads-comment: the comment above `shellLoads` omits `typeset`, which `bindPattern` reads. Its line 288 runs 192 characters. Code: skills/plain-english-map/scripts/lib/fact-readers.mjs:284-289, PR #1625 (branch map11-shell-binds). Decision: map-tested-by-line (the tested-by line decision).
- tested-by-t11-narrates-history: the test comments at skills/plain-english-map/tests/tested-by.test.mjs:1306 and :1384 say T11aa and T11af were re-pointed to FAIL at lane pick 2026-11-07 16:38 CST. Their asserts already expect no load (`mustNotLoad`), so the wording narrates history. The block header at :1292 repeats it. Code: PR #1615 (branch map11-anchor-forms). Decision: map-tested-by-line (the tested-by line decision).
- cloud-comment-edit-refused: in a cloud lane session, the auto-mode classifier refused `Bash` running `gh api -X PATCH` on an existing issue comment ("External System Writes"). So a lane cannot edit its own posted PR comment (PRs #1472, #1512, 2026-11-07 17:57 CST). The owner decides whether to allow it. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner.
- hand-written-review-records: when `merge --review-record` could not page the PR comments, a lane wrote review records by hand on PRs #1472 and #1512 (2026-11-07). Records come only from the merge tool (coordinator ruling 2026-11-07 17:56 CST). Fix: none — cause fixed by #1634; #1601 checks the head. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: pr-review-record.
- retire-excuse-silent-refusal: when the silent-undo check refuses a Retire-run cite, it prints only the delete finding, never why the cite failed (lint/check_silent_undo.py `retire_excuses`). The debt-close check prints the reason in the same merge run. Decision: debt-close-runs-retire.
- retire-excuse-green-test-no-run-read: a cited debt whose Retire test runs green is excused without a read of the cited run, because the test on the head is the proof (lint/check_debt_retire.py `retired`). Decision: debt-close-runs-retire.
- merge-pre-put-read-stop: the pre-PUT read in `Host.merge`, `self.pr(n)`, raises a plain Stop from `Host.pr` on failure. The claim stays, but the stop reads as a refusal, not as "unknown, rerun" (merge/merge.py `Host.merge`). Fix: in `Host.merge`, wrap the pre-PUT `self.pr(n)` read so a Stop from `Host.pr` is raised as `Unproven`, with the rerun remedy. A 401/403 read (`HTTP_DENIED`) stays a plain Stop, as `unknown_unless_denied` does. Decision: standing-merge-grant (the repo's merge grant, its rule that only a proven refusal reverts a claim).
- unread-reads-revert-claim: three reads that could not run still revert the claim after its push. Each raises a plain Stop or returns a plain string, so the caller treats it as a refusal. The first is a lint run that exits non-zero with unknown lines, and the second is a `ci-scope.sh` exit, both in `small_base_move`. A `stamp --check` that exits 1 also reverts, in `prove`, which cannot tell a crash from a taken number. A RuntimeError out of `host.merge` reverts too, because `except Exception` sends it to `fail`, though the merge may have landed. Each needs a deterministic fault. Fix: split each case. In `small_base_move`, the lint run returns UnreadText unless a `FAIL` line is printed, and a `ci-scope.sh` non-zero exit or a run with no flags returns UnreadText. In `prove`, `stamp --check` exit 1 is a refusal only when stamp prints a problem line, and a stack trace or no output is `unproven`. stamp.mjs prints its problems with a bare `console.error`. The fix adds a `PROBLEM:` prefix to each problem line in the `--check` branch of `actions/stamp/stamp.mjs`, and `prove` reads only prefixed lines as refusals. A Node stack trace has no prefixed line. In `confirm`, the `except Exception` after `host.merge` calls `unproven` when `merging` is true, as the OSError branch does. Decision: standing-merge-grant (the repo's merge grant, its rule that only a proven refusal reverts a claim).
- reviewed-head-push-config-silent: when git cannot read the push remote (corrupt config), the guard lets the push through with no stderr line, though its record says one line (hooks/guard_merge.py `reviewed_head_push_hit`). Decision: pr-review-record.
- reviewed-head-push-multi-source: a push of several refspecs asks unless every source equals the PR head, so `git push origin feat other` asks about feat's PR even when feat sends nothing (hooks/guard_merge.py `_sends_nothing`). Decision: pr-review-record.
- reviewed-head-push-cases-thin: no case sets `branch.<b>.pushRemote`, `remote.pushDefault` against an origin branch remote, several refspecs or a detached HEAD, so a mutant of the remote order may live (hooks/test_guard.py). Decision: pr-review-record.
- ansi-c-quote-escape-unread: shlex cannot read `$'…'`, so a `\'` inside one makes the merge-confirm-repo clause skip the segment, as `role_call_of` does on main. Example: `merge 5 --base-red-ok $'did\'nt' -C /other --confirm`. Decision: merge-confirm-repo
- written-time-narrow-catch: `_document_added` catches only OSError and ValueError, and `_written_texts` catches nothing. A malformed shape (deep-nested `data`, a non-iterable `writes`, a non-iterable MultiEdit `edits`) crashes the guard, which logs guard-crash and skips later rules for that call. The tool schema rejects those shapes (hooks/guard_merge.py `_written_texts`). Fix: none — the tool schema rejects those shapes; no incident. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: local-time-central.
- written-items-copy: `WRITTEN_ITEMS` copies question_form `WAITING_ITEMS` with no stated reason, and judges `items` on any artifact URL, not only the waiting page (hooks/guard_merge.py `WRITTEN_ITEMS`). Fix: none — no false alarm seen on another artifact; no incident. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: local-time-central.
- written-time-recursion: `_document_added` does not catch RecursionError from deeply nested JSON. A `file_path` document nested that deep crashes the guard for that call (hooks/guard_merge.py `_document_added`). Fix: none — it needs a hand-built nested document; no incident. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: local-time-central.
- written-time-no-offset: an `added` with no offset passes, because its zone is unknown (hooks/guard_merge.py `_ISO_INSTANT`). It stays a note (coordinator ruling, 2026-11-08 08:33 CST). Fix: none — page writers write an offset; no incident. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: local-time-central.
- written-time-step-other-fields: a step Write judges only `updatedAt`, so a `blocked.at` set ahead passes. A step Edit with a bare fragment such as `T14:30:00-06:00` passes too, since fragments are judged for `added` only (hooks/guard_merge.py `_written_texts`). A Write that sets `at` ahead usually sets `updatedAt` ahead, which refuses. Fix: none — an ahead `at` Write usually sets `updatedAt` ahead too; no incident. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: local-time-central.
- written-reason-steer-false-alarm: `WRITTEN_REASON` says to write the current time for every judged value. For a fixture or prose false alarm, that steers Claude to overwrite a real future value (hooks/guard_merge.py `WRITTEN_REASON`). Fix: none — the decision lists each false alarm with its own fix; no incident. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: local-time-central.
- scratchpad-owner-windows-untested: the scratchpad-owner cases in `tests/guard/s34371-scratchpad-owner.py` skip on Windows, since they use POSIX shell forms and paths, so no case covers the rule there (family `scratchpad-owner`, helper `spo_register`). Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only its own read copy of a scratchpad file, and never overwrites another agent's).
- scratchpad-owner-perl-ruby-flags: WORKFLOW_VALUE_FLAGS is Python-shaped, so `perl -W script`, `ruby -W script` and `bash -r script` skip the script as a flag value, and `perl -I d script` reads `d` as the script (hooks/guard.py `WORKFLOW_VALUE_FLAGS`, `workflow_script_index`). Rare misses. Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only its own read copy of a scratchpad file, and never overwrites another agent's).
- scratchpad-owner-false-refusals: rule scratchpad-owner refuses acts that run nothing (`bash -n F`, `node --check F`, `chmod +x F`, `ln -s F x`, `bash -s -- F`), and lets `bash - F` and `sh - F` run F unrefused, because the lone-`-` stdin skip applies to every interpreter (fix: python and node only). Rare forms (review round 3 of PR #2023, the scratchpad-owner guard). The harm rose: the rule once asked on these acts, and now it refuses them, so the owner has no prompt to approve and must type an override. Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only its own read copy of a scratchpad file, and never overwrites another agent's)
- scratchpad-owner-rare-writes: rule scratchpad-owner neither judges nor records a write by `echo x >| F`, `touch`, `dd of=`, `curl -o`, `wget -O`, `perl -pi`, `unlink` or `shred -u`. So B may overwrite, remove or run such a file of A's unrefused. It reads `make -f F` as no run too (`deno run F` is read). Fix: none, rare forms in an agent's scratchpad work. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only its own read copy of a scratchpad file, and never overwrites another agent's)
- scratchpad-owner-globs: rule scratchpad-owner expands no glob or loop, so `python3 probe*.py`, `for f in S/*.py; do python3 "$f"; done`, `find S -exec python3 {} \;` and `ls S/*.py | xargs python3` run another agent's file unrefused (`scratchpad_run_files`). Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only its own read copy of a scratchpad file, and never overwrites another agent's)
- scratchpad-owner-more-runners: `python3 -m cProfile F`, `-m pdb F`, `-m unittest F`, `go run F` and `php F` run another agent's file unrefused (`SCRATCHPAD_RUNNER`, `WORKFLOW_RUNNER_MODULES`). Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only its own read copy of a scratchpad file, and never overwrites another agent's)
- scratchpad-owner-cd-in-subst: a `cd` inside an unquoted `$(...)` or backtick substitution does not carry, so `out=$(cd S && python3 s.py)` runs another agent's file unrefused; the double-quoted form is refused (`scratchpad_command_words`, `scratchpad_run_files`). Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only its own read copy of a scratchpad file, and never overwrites another agent's)
- scratchpad-owner-silent-except: `scratchpad_writes` in hooks/guard.py sits in one `except Exception: pass`, so a new error skips every write record of the call unseen. Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only its own read copy of a scratchpad file, and never overwrites another agent's)
- scratchpad-owner-verb-wrapper: after `uv run`, `bun run`, `npx tsx` or `deno run`, a wrapper with its own flags hides the run file. Examples are `stdbuf -oL` and `ionice -c3`, so `uv run stdbuf -oL python3 F` runs another agent's file unrefused. `timeout`, `env` and `nice` are unwrapped (`scratchpad_verb_run`). The same reader misses `uv run bash -c "python3 F"`, `cat F | uv run python -`, `uv run --directory D python a.py`, `tsx --tsconfig t.json F` and `deno run --preload p.ts F`. `setsid -w python3 F` passes this rule too. Main judges none of these. Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only a file it wrote)
- scratchpad-owner-runner-named-file: a scratchpad file named like a runner word and run by path, such as `S/py`, is read as the runner and not as the file. The words are `py`, `uv`, `bun`, `npx`, `tsx`, `ts-node`, `pytest`, `stdbuf` and `ionice`. So it runs unrefused (`scratchpad_script`). Main refuses it, and has the same blind spot for `env`, `time` and `source`. Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only a file it wrote)
- scratchpad-owner-unwrap-mutant: the script-word unwrap line in `scratchpad_script` (env, timeout, nice) has no mutant of its own; the case `arg-pin-timeout` kills a deletion, so the gap is small. Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only a file it wrote).
- builder-smart-value-false-alarm: on this branch, a builder's PowerShell `Set-Content -Path docs/a.md -Value “hooks/test_x.py”` is refused, though main allows it. The guard reads the -Value operand as the written path. It names a test path, but the write goes to docs/a.md. The straight-quote form is refused on main too. A false alarm (hooks/guard.py builder_shell_test_hit, hooks/guard_frozen.py _shell_write_hit).
- pin-repoint-class-d-cite: commit 60a5e9fba (re-points the builder shell-write pin to the PowerShell tool) says in its message that the test is a stale pin under class d, with no owner ruling. The pin it re-pointed is this branch's own (commit f8aaeb61d), so class a fits. Fix: none. The commit text cannot change, so this note records the class.
- checkout-glob-pathspec-variable-operands: a `git checkout` operand that is a glob (`tools/*`), pathspec magic (`:/Makefile`) or a variable (`$f`) discards a dirty file and passes, on main and in `lone_operand_is_dirty_file` (hooks/guard_shared_tree.py). Fix: none — Claude does not write glob, pathspec-magic or variable operands to git checkout in normal work. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: agent-worktree-discard-is-noted.
- agent-ruling-a-rows-allow-or-note: the agent-owned "A" ruling rows in hooks/test_guard.py pass whether the guard allows or allows and notes. Fix: none — a regression goes red first in the shared and hand-made rows, so only log noise escapes. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: agent-worktree-discard-is-noted.
- design-delete-local-read-allows: on the local `git branch -D` path, a failed design-file or origin-ref read allows the delete, while the remote path asks (hooks/guard_branch_worktree.py `design_branch_delete_ask`, after #1181, the design-branch guard PR). Decision: design-branch-delete-guard (the design-branch delete guard), after #1181.
- heredoc-feeds-data-header-only: `_feeds_data` reads only the header line. A cat or tee body drops when its output reaches a shell by a later-line pipe, `>(...)` or another path spelling, and another heredoc names an interpreter. Not a mistake form (hooks/guard_core.py `_feeds_data`, after PR #1186). Related: heredoc-run-other-path. Decision: env-file-stays-unread (no session reads or writes an env file's contents; a quoted heredoc body is text).
- heredoc-data-header-keeps-body: a header that only carries data, such as `git commit -F - <<EOF` or `cat <<EOF | wc -l`, keeps its body when the whole-command test fires, as on main (hooks/guard_core.py, after PR #1186). Decision: env-file-stays-unread (no session reads or writes an env file's contents; a quoted heredoc body is text).
- frozen-path-readonly-test-run: frozen-path refused a read-only test run with `GUARD_UNDER_TEST` set to a scratchpad copy of main's guard.py. Possible false alarm: the record covers writes only (hooks/guard_frozen.py, rule frozen-path). Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars (rule 7 resolves shell variables before it matches a write target).
- waiter-clock-unescaped-op: an unescaped `>` or `<` inside `[ ]` passes as an order, because shlex drops the backslash. The shell reads a redirect, so the loop ends at once and writes a stray file (hooks/guard_stream.py `clock_test`).
- waiter-clock-epoch-unbounded: an epoch literal has no upper bound, so a millisecond value passes and the loop never ends (hooks/guard_stream.py `CLOCK_LITERAL`).
- waiter-long-pause-in-loop: under run_in_background a later `done` counts as the command after a long pause, so `sleep infinity` in a clock-loop body passes (hooks/guard_stream.py `waiter_verdict` `long_ok`).
- alias-string-target-per-char: PR #1472 (branch map-import-alias) iterates a string `paths` target per character (import-reader.mjs ~:78). An import then misses its file, or loads a root file of that name. tsc rejects that config. Decision: map-tested-by-line.
- alias-root-tsconfig-nested: PR #1472 (branch map-import-alias) applies the root tsconfig's aliases to every file, a test under a nested tsconfig too (import-reader.mjs ~:76-83, tsconfig-paths.mjs ~:23). The note map-alias-links-unread does not say so. Decision: map-tested-by-line.
- alias-dollar-pattern-replace: PR #1472 (branch map-import-alias) reads `$$`, `$&`, `` $` `` and `$'` in an import specifier as replacement patterns (import-reader.mjs ~:79). With src/x$y.ts tracked, `@/x$$y` loads that file and misses src/x$$y.ts. Decision: map-tested-by-line.
- carried-run-shallow-message: PR #1512 (branch merge-unreviewed-guard-head) fails closed in a shallow clone cut before the fork point. Its message (merge/merge.py ~:3320) does not name `git fetch --unshallow`, as ~:1888 does. Decision: pr-review-record.
- carried-run-reedit-overstated: PR #1512 (branch merge-unreviewed-guard-head): docs/debts/carried-run-reedit-false-alarm.md:3 overstates. Only an overlapping or adjacent re-edit by main refuses, not any re-edit. Decision: pr-review-record.
- carried-run-unreadable-comment: PR #1512 (branch merge-unreviewed-guard-head) makes `--reviewed` read the PR open, even when the carried run breaks before reaching it. Its message (merge/merge.py ~:3288) does not name which comment. Decision: pr-review-record.
- review-record-writes-nothing: PR #1512 (branch merge-unreviewed-guard-head): decision pr-review-record ~:67 says "It writes nothing", which is false: a carried run's git fetch and merge-tree write refs and objects. No test pins a carried run's git fetch count or gh call count. Decision: pr-review-record.
- push-wrong-branch-no-upstream: a test-author may push its own worktree branch in place of the lane branch. With no upstream, or one cut from main, that push passes with no ask; so does `-u origin HEAD`, by ruling 206 a (rule push-stray-branch). A guard would need the lane branch (hooks/guard_push_delete.py `push_ask_hit`). Reasoned from a q_max push on 2026-11-08, not measured. Decision: plain-push-asks.
- lane-start-allow-sonnet-only: the lane-start-settings allow cases in hooks/test_guard.py all use model sonnet, and none pins that opus passes (probed: it does today), so a later rule that judged create_session on Opus could stop every lane start with no test going red. Decision: agent-model-cap (the agent model cap).
- written-time-shell-variable-steer: the false-alarm fix "put the value in a shell variable" also passes a real ahead write (hooks/guard_merge.py _UPDATED_AT). Fix: none — the decision lists it as a known miss; no incident. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: local-time-central.
- written-time-mixed-quote: `d["updatedAt"]="<ahead>"; e['updatedAt']='<behind>'` passes, since only the last literal is judged; main refused it (hooks/guard_merge.py `_written_texts`). Fix: none — the decision lists an ahead value that is not last; no incident. Until: 2026-11-22. Decision: local-time-central.
- guard-imports-outside-guard-paths: `hooks/guard.py` imports `janitor/sweep.py` (`sim_keep_code`, the simulator-boot-twin refusal) and `skills/waiting-page/stale_pages.py` (`matches`, the page-stale refusal). Guards also read `skills/waiting-page/splice.py`, `skills/waiting-page/page.html` (hooks/question_form.py) and `skills/impeccable/SKILL.md`. Neither is on the `GUARD_PATHS` or `GUARD_FILES` list, so `merge --review-record` passes Guard or check `no` for an edit to either. Main has no path check (merge/merge.py `GUARD_FILES`, fresh-chain review round 1 at head bdea700ba of lane merge-guard-flag-from-paths). Fix: none — folded into the queued lane lint-config-guard-paths, which edits the same list. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: pr-review-record.
- docs-only-non-md-docs: Kind `docs-only` refuses any path that is not a `.md` under `docs/` or the root `README.md`, so a docs-only edit to `docs/design/*.html` mockups or other non-Markdown docs is refused. A `.md` outside `docs/` is refused too. It fails safe: the lane posts Kind `normal` and owes one round (merge/merge.py `is_doc`, `kind_misfits`, run on the PR's diff by `--review-record`). Fix: none — the refusal costs one review round, and widening docs-only to HTML would let a screen change skip review. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: docs-only-prs-skip-review.
- other-checkout-git-read-forms: other-checkout skips only `stash list|show` and `clean -n` among its git verbs, so `git -C <other> add -n`, `commit --dry-run` and `apply --check` are refused though they change no file (hooks/guard.py `session_home_git_hit`). Rare in normal work. Measured in round 3 of lane git-dash-c-other-checkout. Decision: an-agent-outside-its-home-tree-must-stop.
- now-central-cmd-unrun: PR #2153 (branch time-from-one-helper): no test runs bin/now-central.cmd; lint/test_now_central.py checks only two substrings in it. Decision: local-time-central.
- now-central-scope-arm: PR #2153 (branch time-from-one-helper): .github/scripts/ci-scope.sh sends an edit to lint/test_now_central.py alone to the `code` job only, so its macOS and Windows steps skip on such a PR. Decision: local-time-central.
- now-central-reads-test-clock: PR #2153 (branch time-from-one-helper): bin/now-central reads LOCAL_TIME_NOW through local_time.clock(), so a leaked test export prints a pinned time with no warning. Decision: local-time-central.
- written-time-shell-variable-steer: the false-alarm fix "put the value in a shell variable" also passes a real ahead write (hooks/guard_merge.py _UPDATED_AT). Fix: none — the decision lists it as a known miss; no incident. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: local-time-central.
- relayed-head-heal-merge: the merge tool's heal merge ("Merge origin/<base> and rebuild the records page", merge/merge.py) on top of a relayed head makes a rerun with `--head` refuse and ask for a new yes. Fails closed. A relayed yes never covers it: the heal brings in content the owner never saw (coordinator, 2026-11-08 13:32 CST). Decision: auto-mode-owner-acts
- relayed-head-page-rebuild: the tool's page-only "Rebuild the records page" commit, made only on a resumed run, also makes a `--head` rerun refuse. Fails closed. Decision: auto-mode-owner-acts
- relayed-head-fetch-blip: a fetch error in the `--head` compare prints the head-moved refusal, not a network retry, so a blip may cost a needless new yes. Decision: auto-mode-owner-acts
- merge-go-lacks-head-template: auto-mode-owner-acts and standing-merge-grant say the coordinator names the head in each merge go. No template or skill tells it to. A go with no head leaves the runner to pick its own sha. A go with no head may also make auto mode refuse the runner. settings.json "Batch Runner Merge" covers only the head the go names. It never covers a merge with no `--head`. Unmeasured. Decision: auto-mode-owner-acts
- head-check-live-not-relayed: the head check in merge/merge.py compares a run's passed sha against the live head, not the relayed sha, so a runner that copies the live head passes. The help text, "a moved head is refused" (merge/merge.py ~:3561), holds only with the relayed sha. Decision: auto-mode-owner-acts
- merge-docs-confirm-no-head: merge/merge.py:7, README.md:140 and docs/decisions/session-tiers.md:63 show `merge <pr> --confirm` with no `--head`. A batch runner is now refused without `--head`, so all three mislead it. Decision: auto-mode-owner-acts
- head-moved-text-names-owner: the head-moved refusal (merge/merge.py:2966, HEAD_MOVED) says "A new yes from the owner is needed." The rule needs a new yes for a moved head. The risk is the route. The runner holds only a relayed yes or a coordinator go. The wording may send it to ping the owner, not the coordinator that relays a yes. Its triggers are the notes relayed-head-heal-merge and relayed-head-page-rebuild. Unmeasured. Decision: auto-mode-owner-acts
- merge-head-refusal-on-draft: merge/merge.py:3628 refuses a batch runner's `--confirm` with no `--head`, and says "the merge needs the head the CI ran on." The check's condition has no draft test, so it also refuses a draft's records step, where nothing merges. The design passes `--head` on that step (skills/project-ops/templates/operating-model.md:41). A runner that omits it pays one retry. Decision: auto-mode-owner-acts
- fixcheck-mutant-in-process: in-process brief-marker cases (hooks/test_guard.py) read the repo's hooks/shared_names.py, not the mutated copy, so only subprocess cases kill a mutant on a shared row. Decision: agent-model-cap.
- mutate-anchor-files-scope: hooks/shared_names.py joined `mutate_shared.anchor_files` for one mutant, but the list applies to every guard mutant (hooks/mutate_shared.py `anchor_files`). Decision: agent-model-cap.
- fix-check-remedy-wording: the `--fix-check` refusal in PR #1572's merge/merge.py says "give sonnet as MODEL"; it should say to run the fix check on sonnet, then give sonnet as MODEL. Reasoned from PR #1572 round 3, not measured.
- fix-check-model-const-name: REVIEW_FIX_GUARD_MODEL in PR #1572's merge/merge.py now covers every record; REVIEW_FIX_MODEL would name it truly. Reasoned from PR #1572 round 3.
- mutate-guard-help-runs-all: `python3 hooks/mutate_guard.py --help` ignores --help and starts every guard mutant; a session ran it by mistake (PR #2296).
- push-stray-narrow-fetch: in a clone whose fetch refspec omits a branch, an implied push to that existing origin branch, off its upstream's name, still asks push-stray-branch. No remote-tracking ref exists to read (hooks/guard_push_delete.py `push_ask_hit`). It only over-asks. Review round 1 of #2228, 2026-11-08. Decision: plain-push-asks.
- push-stray-stale-ref: a stale remote-tracking ref makes push-stray-branch silent, so a re-push of a lane head that GitHub deleted on merge recreates the branch with no ask (hooks/guard_push_delete.py `push_ask_hit`). A fix needs a network read per push. Review round 1 of #2228, 2026-11-08. Decision: plain-push-asks.
- push-main-url-forms: push-main reads one `remote.<name>.url`. A remote with several url or pushurl values, or a `url.<base>.insteadOf` rewrite, can send a push somewhere other than the URL the guard judges (hooks/guard_push_delete.py `_push_goes_to_network`). Claude does not write these configs. Review round 1 of #2199, 2026-11-08. Decision: plain-push-asks.
- push-main-no-repo-silent: when the guard's working directory is not a git repo, the remote's URL cannot be read and a main push stays silent. Git fails a plain push there, but not one that names the repo with `--git-dir`, `--work-tree` or `GIT_DIR` (hooks/guard_push_delete.py `_push_goes_to_network`). Claude rarely writes these forms. Review round 1 of #2199, 2026-11-08. Decision: plain-push-asks.
- push-main-quoted-substitution: a double-quoted command substitution with a space inside, as the remote, such as `"$(git remote get-url origin)"` before `main`, is blanked, so the push stays silent (hooks/guard_push_delete.py `PUSH_QUOTED`). Main is silent too. Review round 3 of #2199, 2026-11-08. Decision: plain-push-asks.
- cross-repo-ssh-port-origin: branch lane/child-question-checks-home-rb: an origin of form `ssh://git@github.com:22/owner/name` gives no page repo (lint/layout.py `repo_name`), so question-checked-with-repo passes every item there. scp and https forms match. Probed in review round 1, 2026-11-08. Decision: cross-repo-question-checked.
- cross-repo-update-creates: branch lane/child-question-checks-home-rb: an ArtifactData `update` with neither `repo` nor `checked_with` passes question-checked-with-repo. If an update can create a document (unverified), a new cross-repo item skips the rule (hooks/question_form.py `cross_repo_hit`). Decision: cross-repo-question-checked.
- refusal-guard-repeats-raised: lint/report_guard.py `refusal_reason` blocked four Stops in a row for one classifier refusal of `Bash` that the replies had raised in prose; only the exact phrase "`<tool>`: refusal was right" or an Input Needed line clears it. Seen 2026-11-08 in lane child-question-checks-home. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner.
- layout-read-non-utf8: lint/layout.py `_read` raises a raw UnicodeDecodeError for a non-UTF-8 extras or layout file (such as UTF-16 from a PowerShell redirect), where main raised LayoutError. Callers that catch LayoutError miss it and fall to their catch-all: hooks/feedback_guard.py `inbox_stop` passes with a stderr line, and `export_check` passes with a fail-open system message. Probed in review round 3, 2026-11-08. Decision: cross-repo-question-checked.
- default-branch-page-only: lint/layout.py `waiting_page` reads only the page from the default branch. In a checkout with no extras file, record_branch and the export folder fall back to defaults, so hooks/feedback_guard.py `waiting_copy` looks in the wrong place for a repo that sets either. The filing check (`Homes.filed_numbers`) fails open there. The export check (`export_check`) blocks a Stop when the default record branch exists with no commit under the default folder, and passes only when the ref is absent. claude-settings sets neither key. Decision: cross-repo-question-checked.
- default-branch-read-cost-untested: no test pins the stated cost of lint/layout.py `_default_branch_extras` (at most two reads of the default branch, none when the checkout has its own extras file), as rule outcomes-mechanize-rules asks. Decision: cross-repo-question-checked.
- subst-comment-skip-misreads: the `#` comment skip in hooks/guard_env.py `_substitutions` reads ` #` inside `${x// #/y}` as a comment, which hides a later `$(...)`. It also denies `(true)#$(...)` and a `#` after a line continuation, where bash reads a comment. Main had the first miss too. Fix: none — rare spellings. Until: 2026-11-22
- push-wrapped-cd-denies: `bash -c 'cd /tmp && git push origin main'` now denies as an ask-alone chain, where the plain `cd /tmp && git push origin main` asks (hooks/guard_merge.py `_push_calls`). Stricter, not a hole. Fix: none — Claude rarely wraps `cd && push main` in bash -c. Until: 2026-11-22
- sim-slot-udid-copies: the pro slot's UDID lives in `SLOT_UDID` (bin/sim-lease) and in three copies, so a device swap edits four files. family `simulator` in `tests/guard/s41900-simulator.py`: `LEASE_SLOT_UDID` copies it on purpose, so a missing constant fails an assert and not an import. hooks/mutate_guard.py's "the pro slot maps to another device" mutant uses the literal as its anchor; the prefix `"pro": "` is unique in bin/sim-lease and would do. skills/ios-apps/SKILL.md lists it for the reader. Fix: none — each copy is a deliberate oracle, anchor or reader list; `git grep` for the old UDID at each swap. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: sim-lease-tool.
- kept-answer-closed-waits-no-signal: a closed item with `waits` gets no `wait:` line (wait_lines skips closed items), so its kept-answer remedy "when its wait has ended" has no signal in the tool's output; the writer checks the wait by hand (skills/waiting-page/stale_pages.py). Lane page-grant-counts-closed, round 2. Decision: relayed-grant-read-from-page.
- kept-answer-files-written-unpinned: no test snapshots the tree for `--items` and `--answers` runs, so "no file written without --write" is untested for them (skills/waiting-page/test_stale_pages.py). Lane page-grant-counts-closed, round 2. Decision: relayed-grant-read-from-page.
- load-docs-keyed-false-unused: `load_docs(keyed=False)` has no caller and no test (skills/waiting-page/stale_pages.py load_docs). Lane page-grant-counts-closed, rounds 2 and 3. Decision: relayed-grant-read-from-page.
- kept-answer-bare-id-shapes: in a bare list, an item whose `id` is a list or dict crashes `--answers` with TypeError (exit 1, no verdict). An item with no `id` never matches, silently. The store gives string ids (skills/waiting-page/stale_pages.py kept_answer_lines). Lane page-grant-counts-closed, round 3. Decision: relayed-grant-read-from-page.
- ci-coverage-scope-names-missed: TEST_FILE skips `test_*.js`, `test_*.ps1`, `*.test.cjs`, `*-test.mjs`, `*_test.mjs`, `test-*.mjs` and `test.mjs` outside the collected folders, so such a file in no job passes (lint/test_windows_split.py TEST_FILE). Fix: none — no such file exists, and Claude names its tests test_*.py, test_*.sh or *.test.mjs. Until: 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-echo-counts: names_path counts any run line that names the file, so `echo lint/test_x.py` or a py_compile line reads as a run (lint/test_windows_split.py names_path). Fix: none — no step names a test file without running it. Until: 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-indirect-run-alarm: a run that does execute the file, such as `cd lint && python3 test_x.py`, `-m unittest lint.test_x`, a discover pattern or a backslash path, reads as missing, a loud false alarm (lint/test_windows_split.py names_path). Fix: none — every step names its suite by repo path today. Until: 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-stdin-test-order: the stdin case plants its files in the order `list` printed them. On a filesystem that lists newest-first (tmpfs), the stdin reader lands last and the case passes against the bug (lint/test_windows_split.py test_a_mod_test_that_reads_stdin_does_not_hide_the_files_after_it). Fix: none — CI runs on ext4 and NTFS, whose order repeats. Until: 2026-11-21.
- mail-not-merged-text: docs/decisions/session-messages.md ("it is not in main", "(not merged)") and skill waiting-page section "Message another session" ("not merged") turn false when PR 1371 merges. Decision: session-messages.
- mail-comment-bug-term: a hooks/mail.py comment says "a bug"; the glossary term is "defect". Decision: none.
- mail-store-named-public: the public repo names the private mailbox store by its repo name (decision session-messages); a name only, no secret. Decision: session-messages.
- mail-safe-trailing-newline: `safe` matches with `$`, which accepts one trailing newline, so `--re 'x\n'` splits the header and the message is never listed; use fullmatch (hooks/mail.py `safe`). Decision: session-messages.
- mail-shared-cache-fetch: sessions on one machine fetch into one shared bare cache, so a ref lock can give one false "mail unknown" or "not sent"; it heals on the next turn (hooks/mail.py `fetch`). Decision: session-messages.
- mail-push-timeout-landed: a push timeout after the server took the ref prints "not sent", and a resend gets a new id; `push` retries only "[rejected]" (bin/mail `send`, hooks/mail.py `push`). Decision: session-messages.
- mail-listing-unpruned: one `listing-*.json` per session address stays in the cache forever (hooks/mail.py `listing_path`). Decision: session-messages.
- mail-lines-docstring-process: the `mail_lines` docstring says it starts no process, yet it starts up to four git processes through hooks/mail.py (hooks/local_time.py `mail_lines`). Decision: session-messages.
- feedback-failed-send-reason: when a send was made and its result failed, the Stop block still says "the transcript shows no send", so the reader cannot tell the guard ignored a failed send (hooks/feedback_guard.py `page_problem`, `repo_problem`, `answer_problem`). Decision: session-messages.
- transcript-nondict-message: a user record whose `message` is not a dict raises in `failed_tool_use_ids`, and the inbox check then passes whole; real transcripts write dicts (lint/_transcript.py `tool_results`). Decision: session-messages.
- feedback-mail-send-no-proof: a `bin/mail send` call is no proof to the feedback guard, so a local send to the page writer by mail gets one block at Stop (hooks/feedback_guard.py `_proves`). Decision: session-messages.
- pin-note-newer-or-shallow: under the pin note, a pin newer than this copy, off main, or behind a shallow cut also exits 0 with a note, not 3 (bin/ste-changed `pin_report`). The decision names old pins only. Decision: action-pins-follow-sessions.
- small-base-move-pin-note: merge's small-base-move proof runs `ci-job checks --lint`, so it passes on pin drift and drops the note unread (merge/merge.py). Decision: action-pins-follow-sessions.
- notes-trailer-whole-file: a `Drops-lines: docs/debts/notes.md -- <reason>` trailer silences every revival on the branch, not just the one it means, so it can hide notes a merge of an old main brought back: 14 were hidden that way on #1643 (lint/check_silent_undo.py `revived_notes`). Fix: step:notes-trailer-per-slug (a follow-up PR makes the trailer name each note it drops). Decision: notes-merge-union (debt notes merge by union).
- touched-tests-ps-sh-unmapped: a touched install.ps1 or tools/*.ps1 selects no test, and a touched `x.sh` maps to `test_x.sh` only, never `x.test.sh` (.github/scripts/touched-tests.py `candidates`). Decision: draft-until-reviewed.
- touched-tests-mods-source-unmapped: a touched mods/ source file with no `x.test.mjs` beside it or in its tests folder maps to no test, though checks.yml runs every mod test (.github/scripts/touched-tests.py `candidates`). Decision: draft-until-reviewed.
- touched-tests-scalar-comment: a `runs-on: "windows-latest" # c` line reads as no runner, so the selector exits 1, a loud false alarm (.github/scripts/touched-tests.py `one_line_scalar`). Decision: draft-until-reviewed.
- touched-tests-flow-steps: a flow-style step `- {run: ...}` or a backslash test path in checks.yml is not read, so that test prints "no CI job runs it" (.github/scripts/touched-tests.py `ci_jobs`). checks.yml uses neither. Decision: draft-until-reviewed.
- touched-tests-exit-cases-thin: no case covers the selector's exit-1 branches (matrix or list runs-on, no jobs, unreadable checks.yml); a mutant that drops `if not jobs` lives (lint/test_touched_tests.py). Decision: draft-until-reviewed.
- classifier-refuses-lease-push, fourth: on 2026-11-08 06:50 CST the auto-mode classifier refused `Bash` for a lane's `git push --force-with-lease=<branch>:<sha>` onto its own PR branch (PR #1949, lane revive-1042), which decision stale-safe-force-push allows off the default branch. It went to the owner in the lane report, and the coordinator chose a replacement PR (#1971). Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner.
- touched-tests-ps1-runner-unproven: the lane touched-test run starts a `*.test.ps1` with `powershell` from Git Bash; no CI run has run that branch yet (.github/workflows/lane-touched-tests.yml). Decision: draft-until-reviewed. Fix: PR 2 (workflow half), after this PR.
- touched-tests-runner-form: the draft run starts `*.sh` with `bash` and `*.mjs` with `node --test`, where checks.yml runs some with `sh` or plain `node`, so a result may differ from the batch's (.github/workflows/lane-touched-tests.yml). Decision: draft-until-reviewed. Fix: PR 2 (workflow half), after this PR.
- touched-tests-check-blocks-merge-tool: the merge tool counts every check on a head. A red draft-run check on a lane head blocks a merge of that head, and only a new push clears it (merge/merge.py `classify`). Decision: draft-until-reviewed. Fix: PR 2 (workflow half), after this PR.
- touched-tests-hung-macos-slot: the `run` job's provisional 60 min cap lets a hung draft macOS job hold 1 of the 5 macOS slots the batch shares (.github/workflows/lane-touched-tests.yml). Decision: ci-job-caps. Fix: PR 2 (workflow half), after this PR.
- touched-tests-name-before-command: the selector exits 1 when a run block names a test before its command line (in a comment, an echo or an escaped quote). A command line that starts with `/bin/bash` does the same (.github/scripts/touched-tests.py `line_command`). Decision: draft-until-reviewed. Fix: none — the exit is loud and names the line, one edit to checks.yml clears it, and checks.yml has no such form today (167 tests probed). Until: 2026-11-21.
- touched-tests-windows-default-shell: a Windows step with no `shell:` key or with `shell: cmd` that names a backslash test path prints "no CI job runs it" (.github/scripts/touched-tests.py `ci_jobs`). Decision: draft-until-reviewed. Fix: none — checks.yml names no backslash test path on a Windows line today, and the miss prints as a skip, never as a pass. Until: 2026-11-21.
- touched-tests-command-flags-dropped: `commands_*` keeps the interpreter word and drops its flags, such as `-File` (.github/scripts/touched-tests.py `line_command`). Decision: draft-until-reviewed. Fix: none — the workflow runs each interpreter with fixed arguments, and nothing reads the flags. Until: 2026-11-21.
- touched-tests-record-stale: decision draft-until-reviewed names only `*.test.mjs` for a mods file and leaves out the chain exit and the line joins (docs/decisions/draft-until-reviewed.md). Decision: draft-until-reviewed. Fix: PR #2012 (workflow half) rewrites that sentence next.
- ci-coverage-quoted-disable-forms: a quoted `if: 'false'`, `if: "${{ false }}"` or `continue-on-error: 'true'` still counts its step as covered (lint/workflow_steps.py `disables`, through hooks/guard.py WORKFLOW_DISABLE_STEP). Fix: none — no workflow here quotes these values. Until: 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-bare-dash-step: a step whose dash stands alone on its line (`      -`) is not read as its own step, so a disabled one escapes or merges with a live one (lint/workflow_steps.py step_blocks). Fix: none — checks.yml writes each step as `- name:` or `- run:`. Until: 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-job-header-comment: a comment after a job header (`  checks:  # x`) hides the job from job_re, so its job-level keys are not read (lint/workflow_steps.py job_re, shared with job_block). Fix: none — no job header in checks.yml carries a comment. Until: 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-disable-comment-untested: no CiCoverageGating row puts an inline comment on a disabling key, so dropping drop_comment from `disables` stays green (lint/workflow_steps.py `disables`). Fix: none — the read has worked on every workflow so far. Until: 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-disable-case: WORKFLOW_DISABLE_STEP is case-sensitive, so `continue-on-error: True` or `if: False` counts as covered (hooks/guard.py WORKFLOW_DISABLE_STEP, shared by lint/workflow_steps.py). Fix: none — it is unverified that GitHub reads `True` as true, and no workflow here writes it. Until: 2026-11-21.
- ci-coverage-gating-name: `gating_text` and GATING_KEYS name the kept text and the disabling keys, while CiCoverageGating calls the disabled step "gating" (lint/workflow_steps.py, lint/test_windows_split.py). Fix: none — rename to disabling terms when next touched. Until: 2026-11-21.
- touched-tests-batch-skip-unrun: no draft run has run on a `batch-` head, so the skip is read from the case block and not seen in a run (.github/workflows/lane-touched-tests.yml, the `touched` step). Decision: draft-until-reviewed. Fix: none — a proof branch named batch-* reads as a real batch to the batch runner's tooling; a local probe of the case block covers it (batch-63l skips; "batch-x; echo PWNED" runs nothing). Until: 2026-11-21.
- touched-tests-skill-js-arm: a map-skill `*.test.js` would run from the repo root, where the map-skill arm runs `*.test.mjs` from its tests folder (.github/workflows/lane-touched-tests.yml, the run loop). Decision: draft-until-reviewed. Fix: none — no such file is tracked, so the arm never fires. Until: 2026-11-21.
- touched-tests-ps1-path-unrun: the PowerShell step's `-File $path` with a space in the path was read, not run, because the review host has no pwsh (.github/workflows/lane-touched-tests.yml, the PowerShell step). A failure there is red, never green. Decision: draft-until-reviewed. Fix: none — no tracked path holds a space, and a mangled path fails `-File` loudly. Until: 2026-11-21.
- resolver-quoted-export-word: `export "D=T"`, `declare "D=T"` and `readonly "D=T"` set D in the shell, but the resolver reads an unquoted first part only (hooks/guard_frozen.py `_resolve_into`, the declare arm). Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — Claude does not quote a whole assignment word in normal work. Until: 2026-11-22.
- resolver-assign-after-substitution: after `$(...)` or backticks the resolver stops reading assignments, so `E=$(date) D=T; echo y > $D` and `export E=$(date) D=T; ...` allow (hooks/guard_frozen.py `_resolve_into`, the command-start state). Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — a substitution before a second assignment on one line is rare, and the first has no frozen target. Until: 2026-11-22.
- resolver-rare-setters: `command export D=T`, `builtin export D=T`, `D=T $EMPTY`, `source /dev/stdin <<'EOF'` with `D=T` in the body, `source <(echo D=T)`, `eval "$(cat <<'EOF' ...)"`, set D but are not read (hooks/guard_frozen.py `_resolve_into`). A wrapper such as `sudo`, `nohup`, `exec`, `xargs` or `timeout` before `env D=T bash <<'EOF'` also hides the child's variable, because env mode is read only at the command start. Without a child script that form sets nothing in the parent shell. Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — each form is rare in normal work. Until: 2026-11-22.
- resolver-stage-unknown-clears: `export D=T; D=$P | cat; echo y > $D` — the stage assignment has an unknown value, so the resolver forgets T while the shell keeps it (hooks/guard_frozen.py `_resolve_into`). `export D=T; D=U | cat; echo y > $D` reads U while the shell keeps T. Main allows both strings too, so neither is a shift from main. Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — contrived. A stage that sets the frozen value itself (`D=T | cat`) still refuses. Until: 2026-11-22.
- resolver-deep-substitution-text: past 20 nested quoted `$(`, an assignment inside reads as text, so it allows (hooks/guard_frozen.py `_NEST_LIMIT`). Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — no Claude command nests 20 deep. Until: 2026-11-22.
- resolver-subst-heredoc-apostrophe: `_subst_end` does not know heredoc bodies or comments inside `"$(...)"`, so a lone `'` there leaves the opening quote unmated. The closing quote then pairs with a later `"` and hides `D=T` before a later `"$D"`. Example: `x="$(bash <<'SH'` with `echo it's` in the body, then `D=T; echo y > "$D"` (hooks/guard_frozen.py `_subst_end`). The standard `git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF' ... EOF)"` form is safe, because guard.py strips data heredoc bodies first. Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — rare, and the frozen write in the commit-message form is still caught. Until: 2026-11-22.
- resolver-escaped-var-in-heredoc: an escaped `\$D` in an unquoted-delimiter heredoc body reads the parent's value, as main does (hooks/guard_frozen.py `_resolve_into`, the heredoc arm). Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — the read is the safe direction. Until: 2026-11-22.
- resolver-ps-method-on-literal: `$D = '~/.claude/hooks/a.py'.ToLower(); Set-Content $D x` allows, and `.Replace(...)` and `.TrimEnd('/')` behave the same (hooks/guard_frozen.py `_resolve_into`, the PowerShell `$D = v` arm). The value word ends at `(`, so the arm forgets D. Main refuses it. Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — rare, and PowerShell only. Until: 2026-11-22.
- resolver-stage-assign-persists: an assignment in a pipeline stage or a background command (`D=T | cat`, `D=T &`) stays set for later text, but the shell does not keep it (hooks/guard_frozen.py `_resolve_into`). Main does the same. Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — the read errs toward a refusal. Until: 2026-11-22.
- resolver-heredoc-rescan-cost: the resolver reads each heredoc body alone, so it runs about 10 times slower than main on a long body. A 3000-line body takes 0.032 s against 0.003 s, and a 10k-line script 0.19 s against 0.023 s. The whole frozen check is 5 to 10 percent slower. Eight thousand lines of unclosed `cat <<E` headers take 4.1 s against 0.5 s on main (`_heredoc_end`), which is not a shape Claude writes. Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — the cost is small beside a hook call. Until: 2026-11-22.
- guard-space-before-paren-write: `( cd /tmp && cp x <frozen path> )` and `(true; cp x <frozen path> )` allow, because a space before `)` makes `)` the copy target. The form without the space refuses. Main allows both too, so this is not from this change (hooks/guard_frozen.py, the write-target read). Decision: none. Fix: none — a separate gap, noted for the owner. Until: 2026-11-22.
- push-stray-non-origin-case: no test or mutant pins the remote name in the stray rule's ref read `refs/remotes/<pushed>/<current>`, so a mutant that hardcodes origin survives. The code is right today (hooks/guard_push_delete.py `push_ask_hit`). Review round 2 of #2228, 2026-11-08. Decision: plain-push-asks.
- probe-note-keys-on-folder: the config-dir note in hooks/probe.py keys on the folder, not the write target, so `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<scratchpad>` with a write to its `settings.json` reads `allow` with no note. The likely probe form, a write under `~/.claude`, is refused on the probe call itself. Decision: none
- probe-note-symlink-out: a probe folder under the config dir that links out, such as a local-install `skills/<name>`, gets no config-dir note, though the frozen-path check reads its literal spelling as frozen (hooks/probe.py `config_overlap_note`). Unrun. Decision: none
- probe-note-cases-unpinned: no case in hooks/test_probe.py pins the config-dir note with no `--cwd`, or a name-prefix sibling folder, so a `startswith` swap passes (hooks/probe.py `config_overlap_note`). Decision: none
- probe-misses-project-dir: hooks/probe.py leaves the outer CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR, so the guard's stranded-cwd and separate-clone checks are not judged, and the docstring list of unjudged checks omits them (guard.py reads it near its stranded-cwd check). Decision: none
- probe-unreadable-log-reads-allow: an unreadable `guard.log` in the probe's own temp dir reads as allow, because the `OSError` branch cannot be told apart from the normal no-log case (hooks/probe.py `crash_reason`). The log is the probe's own fresh file, so Claude cannot hit it by mistake. Decision: none
- probe-bytecode-line-unpinned: no case pins `sys.dont_write_bytecode` at the reader import in hooks/probe.py, so a stray `__pycache__` can land beside an install on a deny, ask or noted verdict. Decision: none
- other-checkout-perl-ruby-bodies: a three-argument perl `open(F, ">", path)` or a ruby `File.write` in a heredoc body that writes into another checkout is allowed. Only python and node bodies are read for it (hooks/guard_frozen.py `_interpreter_write_hit`). Same gap as perl-ruby-bodies-unread. Decision: an-agent-outside-its-home-tree-must-stop.
- brief-discard-heading-prior-sentence: a heading line with an earlier negated sentence ("Do not run tests. Run these steps:") covers a list that orders a discard (hooks/guard.py `_brief_heading`). Decision: brief-destructive-git.
- brief-discard-gerund-false-alarm: "avoid running", "without running", "instead of", "rather than", "will not", bare "not", and a `$ ` line under "Never run:" before a discard span are denied (hooks/guard.py `_brief_prohibits`). Decision: brief-destructive-git.
- brief-discard-reason-no-marker: the brief-destructive-git refusal does not name the `Must deny:` and `Add a case:` markers, so a test-author brief that quotes a case gets no hint (hooks/guard.py `BRIEF_DESTRUCTIVE_REASON`). Decision: brief-destructive-git.
- brief-discard-workflow-unread: a Workflow `agent()` prompt held in a variable is not read, so its discard order passes silently (hooks/guard.py `_brief_spawns`). Decision: brief-destructive-git.
- brief-discard-list-aside: a prohibition list with an aside between spans ("Never run `a` (it hides work), `b`.") or a dash join is denied at the later span; only a bare comma joins spans (hooks/guard.py `_brief_prohibits`). Decision: brief-destructive-git.
- brief-discard-colon-window: the 4-word prohibition window crosses a colon, so "If there are no conflicts: run `<discard>`" passes (hooks/guard.py `_brief_prohibits`). Decision: brief-destructive-git.
- brief-discard-tilde-fence: a `~~~` fence is not read, so a discard in it passes (hooks/guard.py `_brief_commands`). Decision: brief-destructive-git.
- brief-discard-marker-whole-line: a `Must deny:` or `Add a case:` marker exempts every later span on its line, an order too (hooks/guard.py `brief_destructive_hit`). Decision: brief-destructive-git.
- red-run-chain-unscoped: the chain refusal is not tied to the push's repo or refspec. A `cd /other` push, a `--delete` push or a tag push after a test commit is refused (hooks/guard_merge.py, lands with #1582 (red-run push check)). Decision: red-run-trailer-on-test-commits, item 9 (push time).
- red-run-add-u-untracked: `git add -u` reads as a whole-tree add, so an untracked test file gives a false refusal. The marker read does the same (hooks/guard_merge.py, lands with #1582 (red-run push check)). Decision: red-run-trailer-on-test-commits, item 9 (push time).
- red-run-chain-other-verbs: a cherry-pick, merge, rebase or am followed by a push is read before it lands, so it is allowed. That is no hole against main; CI still judges it (hooks/guard_merge.py, lands with #1582 (red-run push check)). Decision: red-run-trailer-on-test-commits, item 9 (push time).
- red-run-archive-push-refused: a push to `archive/<branch>` is judged like any push. `_push_hit` reads the destination only for the range base, and `_red_run_lines` takes none, so an abandoned branch whose last commit is an untrailered test commit is refused. The refusal quotes only the first two lines of `check_red_run.REMEDY` (the trailer and the amend-then-push line), so it never names the replacement PR, which only the CLI prints (hooks/guard_merge.py, lands with #1582 (red-run push check)). Decision: red-run-trailer-on-test-commits, item 9 (push time).
- red-run-remedy-chained-push: REMEDY[1] ends "then `git push`". Chained in one call with the amend it names, that push is refused as red-chain, so Claude bounces once (lint/check_red_run.py `REMEDY`, lands with #1582 (red-run push check)). Decision: red-run-trailer-on-test-commits, item 9 (push time).
- red-run-docstring-row-missing: the docstring table in hooks/guard.py omits the red-chain refusal, and its `frozen-path` row lost one space of alignment, so its verdict column sits one left of its neighbours (hooks/guard.py docstring table, lands with #1582 (red-run push check)). Decision: red-run-trailer-on-test-commits, item 9 (push time).
- refusal-raised-different-act: a different act refused by the same tool with identical reason text passes after a raise, since the key is the tool and the reason (lint/report_guard.py `pending_refusals`). Unmeasured. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner (a refused tool call reaches the owner).
- refusal-raised-not-swept: state/refusals-raised is not swept, so a resumed session keeps its pairs and a repeat of one stays silent. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner (a refused tool call reaches the owner).
- refusal-reason-exact-match: the match is exact text, so a reason that carries an id or a timestamp never matches and the repeat still blocks. Unmeasured. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner (a refused tool call reaches the owner).
- relayed-head-page-rebuild: the tool's page-only "Rebuild the records page" commit, made only on a resumed run, also makes a `--head` rerun refuse. Fails closed. Decision: auto-mode-owner-acts
- relayed-head-fetch-blip: a fetch error in the `--head` compare prints the head-moved refusal, not a network retry, so a blip may cost a needless new yes. Decision: auto-mode-owner-acts
- review-unparsed-two-ci-lines: a record with the CI line twice and text after the first one is refused as unparsed with no CI-line cause. The cause check reads the last CI line (lint/report_guard.py `_text_after_ci_line`). Decision: pr-review-record. Fix: none — Claude does not write two CI lines, and the refusal still names the re-post. Until: 2026-11-21
- review-unparsed-bad-kind-and-note: a record with a bad Kind line and a note after the CI line names only the CI-line cause (lint/report_guard.py `review_record_problem`). Decision: pr-review-record. Fix: none — re-posting through merge regenerates the whole body, so the remedy still works. Until: 2026-11-21
- review-remedy-omits-fix-check-net-value: the refusal remedies for a missing and an unparsed review record omit merge's optional `--fix-check` and `--net-value`. A lane re-posting a record with a Fix check line must find them in `merge --help` (lint/report_guard.py REVIEW_NO_RECORD, REVIEW_UNPARSED). Decision: pr-review-record. Fix: none — the lane that writes a Fix check line knows the flag, and the remedy still names the command. Until: 2026-11-21
- brief-discard-nul-unpinned: the brief scan reads a NUL as a space, and a Workflow `${x}` template reaches the scan as a NUL, but no `brief-destructive-git:` case carries a NUL or `${` (hooks/guard.py `_brief_prohibits`). Decision: brief-destructive-git.
- brief-discard-any-role-comment: the step 9e2 comment in hooks/guard.py says "Any role", but read-only roles are skipped; the decision record is right. Decision: brief-destructive-git.
- brief-discard-wrapped-prohibition: a prohibition hard-wrapped across lines ("Never run these commands in the shared tree:" then a span on the next line, or a comma list split across lines) is denied, because the text before a span is read per line (hooks/guard.py `_brief_prohibits`). Decision: brief-destructive-git.
- ste-lint-dirty-stash-fails: a `git add -N` entry, an unmerged path, or an LFS filter error makes the dirty-tree `git stash create` fail. ste-lint then prints unknown and skips the committed range that main would have linted (actions/ste-lint/run.sh). Decision: none
- ste-lint-scope-all-exit-dropped: scope `all` reads `git ls-files` inside `< <(...)` and drops its exit status, so a bad exclude prints "no markdown changed". The path is report-only, so it never fails a build (actions/ste-lint/run.sh). Decision: none
- ste-lint-ci-untracked-md-linted: in CI, an untracked, un-ignored `.md`, or a tracked `.md` rewritten by an earlier step, is now linted (actions/ste-lint/run.sh). Decision: none
- ste-lint-output-omits-tree: nothing in the output says the working tree was linted, so an unstaged fix can pass locally while the committed head fails in CI (actions/ste-lint/run.sh, bin/ste-changed). Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job (no silent pass)
- ste-lint-unknown-reads-as-pass: bin/ci-job and `merge --lane-lint` judge the step by exit code, so a `ste-lint: unknown` line shows as PASS there (bin/ci-job, merge/merge.py). Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job (no silent pass)
- ste-lint-action-yml-stale-text: the `description` and the `scope` input still say "diffs against the base branch", and the `changed_paths` input still says `git diff --name-only` (actions/ste-lint/action.yml). Decision: none
- stash-create-comment-two-reads: the stack-arm comment near `shared_tree_match` still says "the two reads". The read set is now list, show, help and create (hooks/guard_shared_tree.py). Decision: stash-stack-is-a-shared-subject (the stash stack is a shared subject)
- stash-option-before-action-word: `stash_action` reads option-before-action shapes, such as `git stash -u create` or `-u list`, as the action word. Git may run them as a push with that word as a pathspec (from reading builtin/stash.c, not run). Harm needs a changed file named after the word and a reversed option order (hooks/guard_shared_tree.py `stash_action`). Decision: stash-stack-is-a-shared-subject (the stash stack is a shared subject)
- stash-create-docstring-reads-stack: the docstring of the session-home stash-create test in hooks/test_guard.py says create "reads the stack". It reads the working tree and index. Decision: stash-stack-is-a-shared-subject (the stash stack is a shared subject)
- stash-export-print-unmeasured: `git stash export --print` (git 2.51+) probably writes only objects, as create does, and is still refused (hooks/guard_shared_tree.py). Unmeasured. Decision: stash-stack-is-a-shared-subject (the stash stack is a shared subject)
- silent-undo-plain-unknown-drops: only `Octopus` carries findings past an Unknown. A plain Unknown from `lost_lines`, or from a read after `revived_notes`, still drops them, so the reason flag can lift a real revival. It needs a git read failure and a revival together. A test in lint/test_check_silent_undo.py, near the octopus cases, pins it. Decision: notes-merge-union.
- silent-undo-merge-deletion: a deletion made inside a two-parent merge commit is no event in `revived_notes`, so a later union revival of that note passes. Rare: it needs a note deleted inside a merge. Decision: notes-merge-union.
- silent-undo-nonslug-asym: a non-slug note the branch edited in place, with main appending beside it, gets the old and new lines by union and is refused as a true copy. The slug form of that shape passes. Main rewording a non-slug note the branch deleted is not caught either. Decision: notes-merge-union.
- stamp-loose-tokens: a loose token backs a premise. A Bash `description` or an `echo <token>` backs it, since `backed_premises` joins all string input. Source `PR 5` gives token 5, which `git log -5` matches. A camelCase MCP read tool (`getPullRequest`) is not read-type, since `_is_read` splits names on non-letters, not on case. A premise wrapped over two lines keeps its stamp unseen. Renumbered ids mis-judge. Where: `hooks/precompact_handoff.py`, `source_tokens`, `_is_read`, `premise_stamps`. Decision: `precompact-handoff` (checkpoints keep the handoff current), item 15.
- child-session-writes-refused: in Degen cloud sessions started by another session (2026-11-08), the auto-mode classifier refused GitHub writes and rule commits as "External System Writes" and "Self-Modification", even after the owner's grants (Degen lanes docs-cloud-records and cloud-allow-rules). Each refusal stalls the lane until the owner acts on the Mac or types `override`. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner. Fix: none.
- review-record-subst-refused: in Degen cloud lane small-pr-reads (2026-11-08), the auto-mode classifier refused a print-only `merge --review-record` command that held `$(...)`; the same command without it ran. A possible false alarm: the command posts nothing. Decision: refusals-reach-the-owner. Fix: none.
## Cannot-claim follow-up 6 (PR #2309 round 3 notes)
- cannot-claim-prep-outside-list: prepositions outside `PREPOSITIONS` (inside, within, during, under, via, through) before "this session" read it as the writer. `PREPOSITIONS` lands with PR #2309. Fix: none — no incident yet, low harm. Until: 2026-11-21.
- cannot-claim-neg-new-subject: negation stops at a new subject unless a speech verb (say, claim, mean, write) comes first. So "It is not that I cannot push; I chose not to.", "I don't think I'm unable to push." and "There is no way I cannot push." each block once, with no try. "I did not say I am unable to push." passes. Harm: one block on a true negation. Ruling: lane ruling, 16:08 CST Nov 8, process-only. Fix: none — no incident yet, low harm. Until: 2026-11-21.
- cannot-claim-verbs-hold-not-no: `VERBS` holds not and no, so "Not sure why, I cannot push." passes as a reason after a failed try. `VERBS` lands with PR #2309. Fix: none — no incident yet, low harm. Until: 2026-11-21.
- cannot-claim-prep-pins-untested: a test gap. Nothing pins "against" or "per", or that `_PREPOSITION_END_RE` derives from `PREPOSITIONS`. `_PREPOSITION_END_RE` and `PREPOSITIONS` land with PR #2309. Fix: none — no incident yet, low harm. Until: 2026-11-21.
- cannot-claim-retired-test-names: test_clause.py names the retired `CANNOT_WRITER_RE` and `CANNOT_FROM_HERE_RE`. test_clause.py lands with PR #2309. Fix: none — no incident yet, low harm. Until: 2026-11-21.
- cannot-claim-access-noun-passes: an access noun in "I don't have access to GitHub" passes, because `CANNOT_ACTS` reads the act from a verb. `CANNOT_ACTS` lands with PR #2309. Fix: none — no incident yet, low harm. Until: 2026-11-21.
- cannot-claim-per-target-backing: failed tries back a claim per act, not per PR. With the failed merge of PR 13 still open, "I cannot merge PR 12: `merge` exited 1." passes, though PR 12's merge succeeded or was never tried. Cancels already key by target, but backing does not. Harm: low, since a real failure of the same act exists. Fix: none — no incident yet, low harm. Until: 2026-11-21.
- cannot-claim-nt-form-new-phrases: the claim-backing record's list of phrases says "The n't form of each counts too." That is false for authorization. On #2058, "I wasn't authorized to merge PR 12." and "I haven't been authorized to merge PR 12." give no claim, but "I was not authorized to merge PR 12." blocks. Access takes n't: "I don't have access to merge PR 12." blocks. Main gives no claim for the three authorization lines. Applies once PR #2058 (branch cannot-claim-follow-up-4) lands. Fix: say which forms take n't, or add the n't forms to `CANNOT_FORMS` in lint/_clause.py. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing (the claim-backing record).
- cannot-claim-git-merge-run-route: the claim-backing record says a run of `git merge` is that act. On #2058, "I can't run git merge." gives no claim. The `git merge` arm of `CANNOT_RUN_ROUTE_RE` (lint/report_guard.py) matches, but `CANNOT_TEXT_ROUTE_RE` reads only `git push`, so the run names no act and that arm does nothing. Applies once PR #2058 lands. Fix: make the record say what the code does, or let `CANNOT_TEXT_ROUTE_RE` read `git merge`. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing.
- cannot-claim-non-pr-object-window: `_non_pr_object` (lint/report_guard.py) reads only the 6-word window (`WINDOW` in lint/_clause.py). On #2058, "I can't merge the config files because they conflict." blocks, as on main. The object read runs on through "because they", and its last word, "they", is no file word. "I can't merge the config files." passes on #2058 but blocks on main, so this is no regression. Applies once PR #2058 lands. Fix: none — a recorded limit, no regression. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: claims-carry-backing.
- records-page-octopus-merge-head: an octopus merge reads only the first MERGE_HEAD line, so a page from a later parent is refused. Where: hooks/guard_merge.py _merge_head_page. Decision: records-index-at-merge.
- records-page-chained-theirs-refused: a chained `git checkout --theirs docs/records.md && git add && git commit` is refused, because the hook reads the pre-chain page; split calls pass. Where: hooks/guard_merge.py records_page_hit. Decision: records-index-at-merge.
- records-page-chain-edit-after-read: a chain that edits the page after an allowed pre-chain read passes, the same class as the base-page pass. Where: hooks/guard_merge.py records_page_hit. Decision: records-index-at-merge.
