- script-file-js-tostring-hole: node `const p='tests/a.test.js'; fs.writeFileSync(p.toString(),'x')` was refused on main and is now allowed, because the `_JS_STR` lookahead in hooks/guard_frozen.py skips a bound name followed by `.` or `(`. Unlikely mistake form. Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests.
- script-file-unknown-join-false-alarm: `python3 janitor/mutate_sweep.py` is refused, because python leaves an unknown first piece of `os.path.join(param,'test_sweep.py')` out, as it does `sys.argv[1]` and `tmp_path/'tests'`. Node judges it as nothing. 1 of 322 repo scripts. A debt if a builder is blocked on it. Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests.
- script-file-js-comment-false-alarm: a node file with `// fs.writeFileSync('tests/a.test.js')` in a comment, or the call text in a printed string, is refused. The JS reader is a regex, not a parser, as on heredoc bodies in main. Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests.
- script-file-cwd-deny-row-missing: no row pins the deny for node `const root=process.cwd(); fs.writeFileSync(path.join(root,'tests','a.test.js'),'x')`. The guard refuses it today. Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests.
- page-suite-continue-on-error: a `continue-on-error` on the waiting page suite step passes both workflow checkers (lint/test_windows_split.py waiting_page_problems and scope_problems). The builder guard refuses a builder edit that adds it (hooks/guard.py). Fix: none; no incident.
- duo-fold-negative-angle-case-missing: no case in `lint/test_duo_fold.sh` reads a negative folded angle other than -0.0, so a folded reading of -90 has no case of its own. The open cases already go red when the absolute-value step in the tolerance check is dropped. Decision: duo-fold-tool.
- duo-fold-newline-test-redundant: the newline test at `bin/duo-fold` `duo_fold` is already covered by the anchored `DUO_FOLD_ANGLE_RE`. It is kept as ported. Decision: duo-fold-tool.
- process-args-leading-redirect: `2>/dev/null ps aux` passes, because a leading redirect hides the command word; `ps aux 2>/dev/null` is refused. Low likelihood. Also open: `$(which ps) -ef`, `busybox ps aux`, a `python3 -c` subprocess call. Decision: process-args-guard.
- env-chdir-abbrev-unread: an abbreviated `env --ch=X git commit` is not read by `_wrapper_chdir` (hooks/guard_core.py), so it still passes other-checkout; GNU env accepts the prefix (unmeasured here); a test row "late probe, env --ch= abbreviation" (hooks/test_guard.py, shared-tree block) pins ask. Decision: an-agent-outside-its-home-tree-must-stop.
- sudo-chdir-unread: `sudo -D <dir>` and `sudo --chdir <dir>` also move the program's folder and are not read by the run-folder reader (hooks/guard_core.py `_segment_git_c_target`; `WRAPPER_CHDIR_FLAGS` names only `env`). Decision: an-agent-outside-its-home-tree-must-stop.
- env-chdir-shared-tree-own-root: shared-tree (hooks/guard.py ~4061, the own `-C` branch) roots a segment that names its own folder at `_absolute(<folder>, cwd)`, joined onto the shell cwd, not onto an earlier `cd`. So `cd PRIM && env -C . git <discard>` (or `env -C sub`) is judged in the cwd's folder, where main judged PRIM through `command_root`. It matters only from a cwd in no checkout, since other-checkout refuses the `cd` from a lane. It is the reading `cd PRIM && git -C sub <discard>` already has, which the coordinator ruled to leave alone. Decision: an-agent-outside-its-home-tree-must-stop.
- env-chdir-last-folder-wins: `_run_dir` (hooks/guard_core.py) uses an env -C folder only when every git segment carries an env -C and no segment uses `git -C`. Otherwise it reads the shell folder: the last `cd`, else the cwd. `env -C` moves only its own program, so in `env -C CLEAN git status && git <discard>` the bare git is judged where it runs. With several env -C folders the last one wins, since one folder serves the whole command: `env -C PRIM git a && env -C CLEAN git b` reads CLEAN, though the first git runs in PRIM. Low likelihood in normal work. Decision: an-agent-outside-its-home-tree-must-stop.
- run-dir-relative-cd-chain: a relative `cd` after an unreadable move, or a second relative `cd`, joins onto the shell cwd, not the folder the first `cd` left: `cd a && cd b && env -C c git status` reads LANE/b/c where the real folder is LANE/a/b/c; `cd PRIM && env -C sub1 git a && env -C sub2 git b` reads PRIM/sub1/sub2, real PRIM/sub2. The first is the older `cd`-chain flaw; the second is new with env. Decision: an-agent-outside-its-home-tree-must-stop.
- ios-probe-make-forms: a target-specific `test: FLAGS = ...`, a `define` block, `$(FLAGS:a=b)` substitution and a value from the environment are not read (lint/compliance_probes.py expand_make_vars). Decision: none.
- ios-probe-scan-limits: Five limits in lint/compliance_probes.py `xcodebuild_runs`. A `case` pattern `a)` inside `$(...)` closes the substitution early. A quoted lone `")"` is read as a command mark. A quoted lone `"("` is read as a mark too. A multi-line `$(` with the flag after its `)` gaps. A Makefile variable line `X = xcodebuild test ...` is scanned as a run, so a flag the recipe appends still gaps. Decision: none.
- ios-mockup-font-face-caption: lint/check_ios_mockup.py faults an @font-face family and the system keywords font: caption/menu/icon as other fonts. Decision: blind-briefs-use-a-template.
- ios-mockup-keys-cite-skill: lint/check_ios_mockup.py's docstring cites "skill ios-apps, Type" for the inherit/unset pass (lines 14 and 126), but skills/ios-apps/SKILL.md says only "starts with -apple-system or system-ui", and the trailing-monospace pass has no source there. All three passes come from PR 1489's fix (commit 12061b8cb), so the docstring should name it (lint/check_ios_mockup.py). Decision: blind-briefs-use-a-template.
- resolver-ps-comment-close-line: `<# c #> $D = 'T'; Set-Content $D x` and `Write-Host hi <# c #>; $D = 'T'; Set-Content $D x` allow, because the `#` arm reads `#>` as a line comment (hooks/guard_frozen.py, the `#` branch of `_resolve_into`). Main refuses both. Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — same-line code after `#>` is rare. Until: 2026-11-22.
- resolver-ps-here-string-quotes: PowerShell here-strings (`@'...'@`, `@"..."@`) are read as code, so an apostrophe in the body can pair with a later quote and hide a later assignment (hooks/guard_frozen.py `_resolve_into`). Main does the same with ASCII quotes, so only the smart-quote variants shift. Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — rare. Until: 2026-11-22.
- resolver-merge-code-test-gap: two mutants of the merge's new code survive the frozen cases: `cmd = str(src)` at the start of `_resolve_into`, and cutting the output marks from the straightened text. Cases to add: ``Write-Host ‘a "b’; $D = "T"; Set-Content $D x`` refuses, and ``$D = ‘T’; Set-Content ‘$D’ y`` allows (PowerShell does not expand a smart single-quoted `$D`). Decision: frozen-path-resolves-shell-vars. Fix: none — a test-author adds them in a docs-and-tests PR. Until: 2026-11-22.
- merge-ff-main-moves-cloud-mirror: `ff_main` (merge/merge.py) fast-forwards main in the tree that holds it, taken from the caller's cwd. A `merge <pr> --confirm` run from inside a cloud mirror moves the mirror's main without a mirrorSha update. When origin moved, the next refresh checks full history first, and keeps main with a "could not tell" WARN if that fetch fails (install.sh mirror_refresh). Read from code, not run. Only the batch runner merges, from its own checkout, so the mirror is unlikely. The SessionStart refresh (settings.json) and merge/install_from_main.sh `sync_main` fast-forward main the same way, and neither skips a `cloudMirror=true` tree. To fix it, skip such a tree in all three, as launch.py does.
- launch-cloud-mirror-marker-reads-stderr: merge/launch.py `git()` joins stderr to stdout, so a git warning on `config --get claude-settings.cloudMirror` hides the marker and the mirror is fast-forwarded. install.sh and hooks/session_start.sh read stdout only.
- launch-cloud-mirror-hides-other-reason: a marked mirror that is also dirty or off main prints only "cloud mirror" in launch.py's left-as-is line.
- launch-cloud-mirror-test-gaps: no case pins a dirty marked mirror or a non-literal true spelling (`yes`, `1`); the code matches install.sh's literal `true`.
- release-check-verdict-grep-allowed: `merge --release-check ... | grep "^#N "` is not refused by hooks/guard_stream.py CHECK_FLAGS (only tail and head are), so a cut verdict line reads as silence through grep.
- release-check-equals-form-allowed: `merge --release-check=5 6 | tail` is not matched by the exact-token test in hooks/guard_stream.py CHECK_FLAGS, since it is the first check flag that takes a value. Claude rarely types the equals form.
- gh-body-text-path-qualified-gh: a path-qualified `./gh pr comment 1 -b "a > cfg"` is read as gh, so its quoted `>` is text, though a local program named gh could write the file. `_kind` in `hooks/guard_frozen.py` takes the name through `_name`, which strips the path; the printer rule refuses a path-qualified name. Only a local program named gh reaches it, and the repo ships none. Decision: guard-guards-mistakes-only.
- gh-body-text-remaining-false-alarms: a quoted `>` in a gh body is still refused with a leading `GH_TOKEN=x`, with `gh -R o/r`, in a `--body-file -` heredoc, with `-F`, and inside `bash -c '...'`. All are refused on main too. Decision: guard-guards-mistakes-only.
- gh-body-text-missing-rows: no row pins these denies after a quoted body: `gh pr review 1 --approve > CFG`, `--body > CFG`, a `| cat > CFG` chain, and `-b "a > CFG" > CFG`. No row pins the allow for a glued `-b"a > CFG"`. The guard refuses or allows each correctly today (round 1 probes). Decision: guard-guards-mistakes-only.
- records-page-stacked-sibling-pass: the stacked pass cannot tell a lane's base from a sibling lane that shares its unmerged history. A sibling's page passes. So do a re-merged squash-merged base and a shallow-clone depth cut. The merge tool rebuilds the page before ready (hooks/guard_merge.py _merge_head_page). Decision: records-index-at-merge.
- records-page-family-not-in-module-map: GUARD_MODULE_FAMILIES maps hooks/guard_merge.py to the merge family only. So `--tests-for hooks/guard_merge.py` omits the records-page family. A generic mutant of records_page_hit then skips its cases. The hand-written mutants still run them (hooks/mutate_guard.py).
- test-temp-root-mismatch: `lint/test_sim_test.sh` line 62 puts its run folder under `claude-settings-tests`, while `lint/_test_temp.py` `TEMP_ROOT`, the one home, uses `claude-settings-test-temp`, so a repo has two temp roots. `lint/test_duo_fold.sh` uses the one home, `lint/_test_temp.py`. Decision: test-temp-standard.
- duo-fold-clone-skip-case-missing: no case in `lint/test_duo_fold.sh` reaches the clone-skip branch at `bin/duo-fold` `duo_fold_fetch_hinge`, and dropping its `|| return 1` still passes the failed-clone case, because the missing hinge file refuses it. Decision: duo-fold-tool.
- duo-fold-move-failure-note-unasserted: the stderr note a failed hinge move prints (`bin/duo-fold` `duo_fold`) is named in the header, but no case asserts it. Decision: duo-fold-tool.
- duo-fold-ci-scope-omits-test-temp: the `bin/duo-fold` arm in `.github/scripts/ci-scope.sh` omits `lint/_test_temp.py`, which the suite imports, so a PR that edits only that file skips the macOS suite. The `bin/sim-test` arm has the same form. Decision: duo-fold-tool.
- process-args-systemctl-status: `systemctl status <unit> -l` prints the unit's process command lines and passes. Low to moderate likelihood. Not read by the rule. Decision: process-args-guard.
- process-args-false-alarms: `ps aux | wc -l` and `ps -ef | wc -l` (count only), `ps -p $(pgrep node)` (the space inside the substitution makes `node)` a BSD operand) and PowerShell `ps node` (alias of Get-Process) are refused. Each costs one retry; the safe forms are `pgrep -c`, `ps -p <pid>` and `Get-Process node`. Decision: process-args-guard.
- plumbing-test-comment-stale: the comment at `hooks/test_guard.py` lines 17552-17554 says a `-m` message with `<` reads as extra trees and logs unread. `message_end` now reads such a message whole. Fix: a test-author edit. Merge after: #2435. Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests.
- plumbing-allow-case-gaps: the plumbing block in `hooks/test_guard.py` has no designer allow case and no parentless clean-tree allow case, so a mutant that always denies for one of them survives. Fix: a test-author edit. Merge after: #2435. Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests.
- plumbing-final-deny-source: the final deny for all four roles on a plumbing commit is a coordinator pick from its lane brief of 05:44 CST Nov 9, not an owner ruling. It stands. Merge after: #2435. Decision: builders-cannot-edit-tests.
- pfr-unrecordable-report-denies-all: a pending reviewer report clears only through a posted review record, but `merge --review-record` stops once the PR is closed or its head moved (merge/merge.py ~3648). An unrecorded report on such a PR denies every later `git push` in that session, the `archive/<branch>` push included, until a typed override. Decision: push-needs-review-record.
- pfr-powershell-block-create-miss: PowerShell one-line script blocks (`if ($?) { gh pr create ... }`, `try { }`, `& { }`, `ForEach-Object { }`) and Bash one-line `case ... in x) gh pr create;; esac` or `f() { gh pr create; }; f` read as no PR, so a later push after an unrecorded FAIL passes. Decision: push-needs-review-record.
- pfr-inner-commands-untested: no case has `bash -c`, `sh -c`, `timeout .. bash -c` or `env` around a `gh pr create`, so a mutant that empties the inner-command read survives. Decision: push-needs-review-record.
- pfr-powershell-create-untested: no case has a `gh pr create` from the PowerShell tool, so a mutant that reads PowerShell text as POSIX survives (e.g. `--body "C:\dir\"`). Decision: push-needs-review-record.
- iceberg-prose-rules: iceberg's "condition first" and "numbers, not 'fast'" prose rules are ste-lint candidates if an incident shows the need; no incident is recorded, so no port (decision building-guard-needs-incident; source: docs/plans/plugin-assess-three.md, PR #1036 merged).
- lint-kill-leaves-children: a kill of `bin/ci-job --lint` by merge's timeout (SIGKILL reaches only ci-job) leaves its temp log folder and up to one verdict child per worker running. The run ends near 380 s of the 1200 s cap: 1 sample at 4 workers; below 4 CPUs the run is unmeasured (bin/ci-job run_step, merge/merge.py ci_job_run)
- lint-cap-cases-probabilistic: the cap and finish-order cases of ParallelLint catch a wrong build about 90% of runs (13 to 14 of 15 red), so a cap regression shows as a flaky red. Fix: loop the case with `fresh_run` (lint/test_ci_job.py).
- lint-wrong-build-spins: a serial or under-parallel build spins in `RV.wait_for` until the CI job timeout, and a kill by merge reaches only ci-job, so up to four spinners outlive it (lint/test_ci_job.py RV.wait_for). Owner ruling P5 chose unbounded waits.
- lint-parallel-other-repos: parallel `--lint` is shown safe for this repo only. Another repo with a generator CI step before a checker, or two `pipx run` steps on one tool, is unmeasured. A parallel-only FAIL gives no hint of `CI_JOB_LINT_WORKERS=1` (bin/ci-job).
- refusal-cite-helper-reach-forms: the refusal cite scan (lint/test_rule_audit.py, `_Helpers`) misses some block dicts passed to a printing helper. Unread forms: a `{**p}` spread, a print inside a nested def, `*args`, a method printer, a name bound first and then passed, and `{"hookSpecificOutput": p}`. A both-branch if/else rebind of the parameter reads a false alarm. A helper that overwrites the reason key before printing reads the caller's cited reason as printed. Also unread on main: a print with `+ "\n"` and `sys.stdout.write`. Scan cost can grow again above MAX_DEPTH about 32, because the `_call` memo keys use fresh locals. Found by step stop-block-cites reviews, rounds 1 to 3.
- close-form-shape-check-sibling-only: the shape check in merge/test_merge.py (`adjacent_whitespace_repeats`) sees two sibling whitespace repeats only. A quadratic respelling with the second repeat in a capture group or an alternation arm (`\s*(?:|:)\s*`, `(\s*):?(\s*)`) stays green. Claude would write one only on purpose. Round 2 note, PR 2524.
- guard-merge-families-miss-push-delete: the `hooks/guard_merge.py` row of GUARD_MODULE_FAMILIES (hooks/mutate_guard.py) lists only `merge`, so a changed-mode run on a guard_merge.py edit cannot reach the push-delete case that kills the _push_calls substitution mutant. Fix: add push-delete to the row (own lane).
- gh-body-text-heredoc-body-false-alarm: `gh pr comment 1 --body "$(cat <<'EOF'` with a `> CFG` line in the heredoc body, a `<<<` here-string body, and a quoted `"-b"` are refused as frozen-path, on main too. The `$(` rule stays by design; the heredoc body is text. Decision: guard-guards-mistakes-only.
- run-dir-env-later-git-unsplit: `_run_dir` (hooks/guard_core.py, the `bare_git` test) counts only a later git that `split_segments` splits off. A lone `&`, a `$(...)` and a `bash -c "git ..."` body are not counted. So after `env -C X git a`, a later git in one of those forms leaves folder X as the whole command's folder. `env -C X git status & git log` reads X; main reads the cwd. Same class as `cd PRIM & git b` on main. Round 3 measured it. Likelihood in normal work: about zero. Decision: an-agent-outside-its-home-tree-must-stop.
- run-dir-env-folder-ln-over-dir: If only env-carrying git segments come before a later non-git `ln -s`, `ln-over-directory` (hooks/guard.py, the `_run_dir` caller) reads the env folder. `env -C X git status && ln -s a b` reads X, but the `ln` runs in the shell folder. Round 3 measured it. Likelihood in normal work: about zero. Decision: an-agent-outside-its-home-tree-must-stop.
- env-chdir-var-folder-diff-unread: for `env -C "$VAR" git commit` the folder holds a variable, so the reader returns a nonexistent root. The builder-role diff check (`diff_violation`) returns None, notes `role-diff-unread`, and allows. Shared-tree denies a discard there, because the subject state of a nonexistent root is false. Same reading as `git -C "$VAR"` on main. Round 3 measured the reader and read the callers statically. Likelihood in normal work: low. Decision: an-agent-outside-its-home-tree-must-stop.
- relayed-grant-other-settings-keys: the Relayed Grant "Never covers" sentence in `settings.json` does not list keys such as `autoMode.soft_deny`, `environment`, `apiKeyHelper` or `statusLine`; a label naming one by exact key would be covered. Only an owner pick reaches it. Decision: relayed-grant-read-from-page.
- relayed-grant-settings-file-has-no-repo: "settings.json" in the Relayed Grant Covers text names no repo or path, so a label could cover another repo's `.claude/settings.json`. Decision: relayed-grant-read-from-page.
- relayed-grant-label-read-at-fetch: `skills/waiting-page/page_grant.py` `label_of` reads the label from the item as fetched and compares only `n`, so an option reworded after the pick with `n` unchanged is not seen. Decision: relayed-grant-read-from-page.
- windows-guard-no-pr-cap-end: Before R62, shard 1 of the Windows guard suite ran on a PR as the Windows slice (step "Guard fixture suite (Windows slice)", since removed). Its job, `checks-windows-guard`, could show a cap end on a PR run. Shards `-2` and `-3` skipped pull_request (`if: github.event_name != 'pull_request'`). So a PR run never showed their cap end. Since R62 all three shards run in full on a ready PR, so a ready PR run can show a cap end for each of them. Decision: ci-job-caps.
- ci-wake-claim-uncited: the What line of `docs/debts/ci-wake-cloud-unproven.md` (line 3) says the hook prints "CI unknown" with the reason "gh is not on PATH" and cites no read for either. The claim came from PR #2593 (commit 2e5ebe19e, which added the debt). The prose judge flagged it on main at commit 2684959d6 (the batch 82e merge). Fix: cite the read that backs it, or cut the claim.
- mirror-refresh-no-mirrorsha-slow-path: install.sh `mirror_refresh` sends a mirror with no `claude-settings.mirrorSha`, no `main` branch, or an erroring `is-ancestor` to the full-history check once; no test covers these three inputs. A kept clone with local commits repeats the depth-1 plus full-history fetch each resume (size unmeasured).
- sync-main-no-remote-silent: `merge/install_from_main.sh` `sync_main` returns silently with no origin remote, but docs/decisions/merge-reinstalls-from-main.md item 6 says it warns; no test covers it. With no `timeout` binary (stock macOS) its fetch has no 30 s cap and no low-speed limit, so a stalled fetch holds the install lock until it goes stale.
- sync-main-refusal-remedy-names-push: the `sync_main` refusal text says "push them or move main back to origin/main", which names a push to main and a reset; reword to point at `git log origin/main..main` and the owner.
- mirror-refresh-m18-m19-no-mutant-red: hooks/test_install_src.sh caseM18 (failed full-history fetch keeps main) and caseM19 (failed mirrorSha write is logged) pass on the code they pin and have no recorded mutant red: the frozen-path guard refused scratch copies of install.sh with INSTALL_SH_UNDER_TEST. Round 3 reviewed the cheap-path mutant for caseM17 only.
- move-check-per-file-not-per-line: the move test in `lint/diff_moves.py` judges each file, not each line's context, so a line moved from a string literal or a fixture into a real comment or name passes. `lint/check_comment_narration.py` and `lint/check_glossary.py` inherit it. Found in PR 2638. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- move-language-reads-disk: `language_of` in `lint/check_comment_narration.py` reads the shebang from disk, so a move out of a deleted extensionless script is a false alarm. Found in PR 2638. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- move-unquote-quoted-path-uncovered: `unquote` in `lint/diff_moves.py` has no case for a quoted old path. The nearest case, `test_a_diff_header_with_no_readable_path_is_unknown_and_red_in_ci` in `lint/test_check_glossary.py`, reads a new path. Found in PR 2638. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- move-copy-ties-follow-diff-order: `take_move` in `lint/diff_moves.py` gives a copy tie to the earlier diff line, so an earlier unscanned copy can use up the deleted copy a later scanned move needed. False alarm, safe direction. Found in PR 2638. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- build-late-append-symlink-windows: `build_late_append` in `hooks/test_guard.py` still symlinks, so its check skips on Windows. `run_cases` in the same file calls each log check with no try, so any other OSError kills a shard. Found in PR 2635. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- link-problem-runner-only: `link_problem` in `lint/rule_audit.py` reads only the runner text, so a row that cites a runner case with a ref only a section names gets a false "neither imports nor runs". No current row hits it. Found in PR 2635. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- load-sections-skips-non-py-silently: `load_sections` in `hooks/test_guard.py` skips non-.py files with no message. Found in PR 2635. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- tests-for-silent-empty: `tests_for_main` in `hooks/mutate_guard.py` prints nothing on a runner parse error or a non-static SECTION_DIR. Found in PR 2635. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- scratchpad-rm-rf-unguarded: no guard refuses `rm -rf` on a scratchpad folder that another agent of the session wrote. A builder deleted one. Queued as a debt. Decision: scratchpad-owner (the pattern).
- install-from-main-refusal-ignores-pin: `sync_main` in `merge/install_from_main.sh` (about lines 205-222) still refuses with "local main has N commit(s) origin/main lacks", although a stable pin installs no local main. Its "installing local main as it is" WARN (about line 209) is wrong when a stale origin/stable exists. Found in PR 2819, the act 2 pull request of lane merge-flow-after-anthropic. Decision: merge-reinstalls-from-main (item 6).
- install-from-main-stable-miss-pins-main: a transient failure of only the stable fetch, with the main fetch fine, pins local main without the green-only rule that the helper header states (`merge/install_from_main.sh` line 4). This follows the orchestrator's pick; the owner may overrule it. Found in PR 2819.
- install-stable-move-race: a fetch that moves origin/stable between the helper's pin move and `pin_off_main` in `install.sh` (about line 228, called at about line 245) makes the installer exit 1. Found in PR 2819.
- install-from-main-picks-unruled: The re-pointed cases 23, 49, 52, 56 and the stable-fallback tests rest on orchestrator picks (lane merge-flow-after-anthropic, 2026-11-12), not on an owner ruling; the owner may overrule them. Found in PR 2819.
- launch-stable-miss-runs-main: a failed stable fetch while the main fetch worked makes `merge/launch.py` (lines 49-58) run origin/main, so a short network blip runs an unproven merge tool. Same shape as install-from-main-stable-miss-pins-main. The settings.json SessionStart hook's nonzero ls-remote (g=1) counts as no stable branch: it writes a false no-stable WARN and runs the helper once more, but pins the right commit. Found in PR 2819. Decision: merge-reinstalls-from-main.
- stable-absent-warn-each-start: the settings.json SessionStart hook and README.md log one WARN line per session start while origin has no stable. Merging act 2 before act 1 of lane merge-flow-after-anthropic fills the install log and launcher stderr with it. Merge act 1 first. Found in PR 2819. Decision: merge-reinstalls-from-main.
- ff-tracked-change-warns: a tracked change in a tree that holds main no longer skips the fast-forward in the SessionStart hook or in sync_main. Overlapping edits now warn instead of refusing. Git still blocks overlapping edits, so no data is lost. It rests on an orchestrator pick, not an owner ruling; see install-from-main-picks-unruled. Found in PR 2819. Decision: merge-reinstalls-from-main.
- cloud-stable-fail-wrong-warn: when stable exists, the checkout holds no origin/stable record, and its fetch fails once, install.sh (about lines 526-532) falls back to main. The WARN then says "origin has no stable branch", the wrong cause for a transient failure. No test pins this case. Found in PR 2819. Decision: merge-reinstalls-from-main (each merge reinstalls from main).
- install-fetch-comment-stale: the comment above the cloud block's fetch (about lines 520-522) says a branch that does not hold the ref pays no fetch. That is false when REF is not main: that branch pays one extra fetch, capped by the 50 s budget. Found in PR 2819. Fix: with the next edit to that block. Decision: merge-reinstalls-from-main (each merge reinstalls from main).
- cloud-detached-no-main-fallback: the detached-checkout arm of the cloud block has no main fallback, so a detached checkout with no stable keeps its old WARN path. Found in PR 2819. Decision: merge-reinstalls-from-main (each merge reinstalls from main).
- red-two-head-files-singular: Two head-group files (two push workflows in a child repo) can each name a breaking merge. The text says "the breaking merge named above", singular, and the segments do not name their workflow file (`base_state`, `red_base`, merge/merge.py). Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-broke-wins-over-first-red: A breaking merge in one group suppresses the first-red re-run advice for another group. `broke` wins over `first_red` (`red_base`, merge/merge.py). The first red shows again after the revert. Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-breaking-line-error-unknown: An exception inside `breaking_line` turns a red verdict into unknown. Both refuse; only the text degrades. The catch sits in `base_state` (merge/merge.py), around its `breaking_line` call. Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-cancel-stepped-over: A cancelled run between red and green is stepped over, so the named run may skip a cancelled suspect commit. Push runs on main are cancelled only by hand (`breaking_line`, merge/merge.py). Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-cross-group-clear-stale: The cross-group clear is stale. `base_state` keeps only the newest run per group in `red_runs`, but the walk reads older runs of the same group without their clears. So a red cleared by a newer green run of another group can still be blamed (`base_state`, `breaking_line`, merge/merge.py). Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-timeout-run-differs: A run that failed only by timeout counts as red in the walk but not in `base_state` (`breaking_line`, `base_state`, merge/merge.py). Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-first-attempt-hidden: A first-attempt run still going below a completed red re-run is invisible to the walk, because `judged` drops it (`breaking_line`, `base_state`, merge/merge.py). Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-decision-text-stale: The decision `no-merge-onto-red-main` still says the refusal names jobs, run and URL, and that the way out is the carrying batch. The planned decision rewrite, act 5, changes it. The owner's pick (a) on question Q3 i is open and not recorded as his pick. Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- stable-mover-token-in-argv: `actions/stable-mover/run.sh` puts the token's base64 in git argv (`-c http.extraheader=...`), so any process on the runner that reads argv can see it. `actions/checkout` writes its header to a config file instead. Found in lane-stable-mover review (act 1). Fix: none yet; the config-file form is the cheap fix. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- stable-main-fetch-untested: `actions/stable-mover/run.sh` line 33 exits 2 when the fetch of origin's main fails, and no case in `test_action.sh` reaches it. An unreadable origin dies at the `ls-remote` read on line 18 first. Found in lane-stable-mover review (act 1). Fix: none yet; a case where the `ls-remote` read passes and the main fetch fails. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- stable-test-concurrency-key-only: `job_concurrency` (lines 44-47) and the test that uses it (lines 101-103) check only that the job's `concurrency:` key exists. A group other than `stable`, or `cancel-in-progress: true`, passes. Harm is low, since the push has no force. Found in lane-stable-mover review (act 1). Fix: none yet; assert the group and `cancel-in-progress` values. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- stable-skipped-run-concurrency-unmeasured: `stable.yml` lines 19-20 say a run whose `if:` skips never enters the job's concurrency group. Unmeasured: whether GitHub enters a skipped job into the group. Worst case, stable waits for the next green main run. Found in lane-stable-mover review (act 1). Fix: none yet; a live run with a skipped job would measure it. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- ruleset-held-by-exact-ref: `protect.py` `main` finds the held ruleset only by an exact `refs/heads/<branch>` include condition, so a `~DEFAULT_BRANCH` ruleset gets a second POST. `differences()` compares no conditions, so a ruleset that excludes `main` reads as in form. Decision: main-branch-protection. Fix: none, since no repo has a ruleset yet.
- ruleset-put-drops-bypass: `protect.py` PUT sets the bypass list to the Admin role alone, so any other bypass actor is dropped. `skills/ci-hygiene/snippets.md` says so. Decision: main-branch-protection. Fix: none, since the snippet states it.
- ruleset-read-scope-unmeasured: unmeasured whether `rules/branches/<base>` needs admin scope. A push-only login that reads classic protection today could get a 403, and `ruleset_names` stops every merge on a failed read. Also unmeasured: the rules read through the cloud agent proxy (`pages`, merge/merge.py :967), and a base with a slash (`rules/branches/release/1`). Both fail closed on a 403 or 404: `ruleset_names` raises Stop (merge/merge.py :1254-1270). Decision: main-branch-protection. Fix: none until measured.
- ruleset-pin-compare-untested: no test covers the pinned `merge.requiredChecks` list against a ruleset. A mismatch stops the merge; an unreadable ruleset says so and the pin stays (merge/merge.py `refuse_pinned_mismatch`, :1307). Decision: main-branch-protection.
- ruleset-only-batch-untested: `batch_running` in guard mode (`unprotected_counts_all`) with a ruleset-only repo has no test (merge/merge.py :1176, called at :1699). Decision: main-branch-protection.
- ruleset-put-adds-admin-bypass: a PUT adds the Admin bypass at ruleset level, so a held ruleset that also carries a pull_request approval rule gets the admin bypass on that rule too (skills/project-ops/scripts/protect.py `ruled_body`, :86; PUT at :136). Decision: main-branch-protection.
- ruleset-and-classic-names: while both a ruleset and classic protection exist, the tool names the ruleset's checks only (merge/merge.py `protection_names`, :1272-1278). GitHub's own merge check still enforces both, so a classic-only check fails the merge with a clear refusal. Decision: main-branch-protection.
- protect-read-failure-untested: no case in `test_scaffold.py` covers a failed ruleset list or detail read in `protect.py` `main` (:112-114, :122-124). Each such read exits 1 and refuses, so the direction is safe. Decision: main-branch-protection.
- admin-role-id-unconfirmed: `ADMIN_ROLE_ID = 5` in `protect.py` (:29) is unconfirmed against a live ruleset. The only fix for a wrong id is a read-back at the first live ruleset. Decision: main-branch-protection.
- stable-mover-token-in-argv: `actions/stable-mover/run.sh` puts the token's base64 in git argv (`-c http.extraheader=...`), so any process on the runner that reads argv can see it. `actions/checkout` writes its header to a config file instead. Found in lane-stable-mover review (act 1). Fix: none yet; the config-file form is the cheap fix. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- stable-main-fetch-untested: `actions/stable-mover/run.sh` line 33 exits 2 when the fetch of origin's main fails, and no case in `test_action.sh` reaches it. An unreadable origin dies at the `ls-remote` read on line 18 first. Found in lane-stable-mover review (act 1). Fix: none yet; a case where the `ls-remote` read passes and the main fetch fails. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- stable-test-concurrency-key-only: `job_concurrency` (lines 44-47) and the test that uses it (lines 101-103) check only that the job's `concurrency:` key exists. A group other than `stable`, or `cancel-in-progress: true`, passes. Harm is low, since the push has no force. Found in lane-stable-mover review (act 1). Fix: none yet; assert the group and `cancel-in-progress` values. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- stable-skipped-run-concurrency-unmeasured: `stable.yml` lines 19-20 say a run whose `if:` skips never enters the job's concurrency group. Unmeasured: whether GitHub enters a skipped job into the group. Worst case, stable waits for the next green main run. Found in lane-stable-mover review (act 1). Fix: none yet; a live run with a skipped job would measure it. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- reap-date-lock-off-linux: a date-form host lock on macOS reads own=True and stale=False, so a finished tree locked that way can be removed off Linux (janitor/sweep.py `stale_agent_lock_verdict`, `decide_worktree`). Decision: when-a-worktree-may-be-removed.
- reap-root-veto-untested: the veto in `host_is_session_root` (janitor/agent_end_reap.py) has no test, and `session_root_pid` finds no root under the tests. Decision: when-a-worktree-may-be-removed.
- reap-finish-recheck-window: the finish is read before ls-remote, status and the process scan, with no re-check at unlock. An agent resumed in those seconds and then idle 30 min with no process would be read as finished (janitor/agent_end_reap.py `reap_worktrees`). Decision: when-a-worktree-may-be-removed.
- reap-foreground-servers-unmarked: a server an agent starts in a foreground Bash call holds no agent marker, so the agent-end reap keeps its tree (hooks/stall_watch.py `hook`, janitor/sweep.py `host_child_stops`). Measure: does an `export CLAUDE_AGENT_ID=...` line before an agent foreground call prompt in a local default-mode session. Fix: none — needs the Mac. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: when-a-worktree-may-be-removed.
- reap-stop-list-decided-early: the stop list is read at decide time, and the mark and cwd are not re-checked before each signal; a wrong stop needs a pid reused within seconds (janitor/agent_end_reap.py `stop_and_settle`). Decision: when-a-worktree-may-be-removed.
- reap-host-child-stops-no-deadline: `host_child_stops` takes no deadline, so a very large tree could outrun the hook in decide, before the unlock, which keeps the tree; unmeasured (janitor/sweep.py `host_child_stops`). Decision: when-a-worktree-may-be-removed.
- agent-marker-proves-lineage: the agent marker proves lineage, not who started a process. A shared daemon (tmux, screen) that an agent starts in a background call passes its marker to windows a session later opens in that tree, and those would be stopped (janitor/sweep.py `host_child_stops`). Decision: when-a-worktree-may-be-removed.
- ticks-lock-makes-own-lock-live: the ticks parse in janitor/sweep.py `_ticks_lock_stale` also makes `own_lock` live for the harness lock form, in the SessionEnd sweep and the merge tool. Gate: merged, idle 30 min, clean, pushed, no process. GitHub item #2783 does not touch it. Decision: when-a-worktree-may-be-removed.
- ticks-lock-pid-namespace-stale: `_ticks_lock_stale` (janitor/sweep.py) reads a missing /proc/<pid> as dead, so a lock pid in another pid namespace, such as a sandboxed shell or a container, reads stale. The date form has the same blind spot through its `ps` read. Decision: when-a-worktree-may-be-removed.
- test-sweep-duplicate-import: commit 01907692cd drops a duplicate `_test_temp` import in janitor/test_sweep.py, in a carry diff. Import-only, no behaviour change. Decision: when-a-worktree-may-be-removed.
- records-page-deleted-debt-row: docs/records.md line 363 still lists the deleted debt `finished-agent-shells-keep-tree`. The merge tool rebuilds that page. Decision: when-a-worktree-may-be-removed.
- session-start-fetch-bound: worst case is one ls-remote then one fetch, each at the 2 s bound: 4 s per checkout, 8 s with a separate pointer clone, against the 10 s hook timeout. Grant lines print first. Windows unmeasured (hooks/session_start.sh `bounded_fetch`). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-12-12
- session-start-update-ref-untested: the `bf_rc -eq 0` condition on `update-ref -d refs/remotes/origin/main` has no red case, and a failed delete (a ref lock) is silent, so a stale origin/main is compared with no "unrefreshed" note (hooks/session_start.sh). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-12-12
- session-start-shallow-clone-history: an explicit main fetch in a shallow single-branch clone pulls main's history (1 commit became 4 in a probe); size cost unmeasured (hooks/session_start.sh). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-12-12
- session-start-renamed-default-nudge: the behind-nudge is skipped when the remote HEAD names a different branch than local origin/HEAD; advisory only (hooks/session_start.sh pull check). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-12-12
- batch-ready-no-touched-tests: the ready route (`merge <pr> --ready`) runs no lane lint and no touched tests. The touched tests run only when `--touched-tests` is given to `merge --lane-lint <target>`, which any lane-lint target takes (merge/merge.py main, the `lane_lint(... a.touched_tests)` call). Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes. Fix: none until a miss.
- touched-tests-ps1-skipped-in-pass: `run_touched_tests` in merge/merge.py skips a `.ps1` test when there is no pwsh and prints `skip <test>: no pwsh on this runner`. The PASS line counts it under skipped, not run (merge/merge.py `run_touched_tests`). CI's Windows job runs it. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes. Fix: none until a miss.
- touched-tests-selector-from-head: `run_touched_tests` in merge/merge.py execs `.github/scripts/touched-tests.py` from the lint worktree, which is the head. A lane can move its own test out of the fast check; CI still runs the heavy shards. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes. Fix: none until a miss.
- incomplete-tree-before-clash-fail: `refuse_incomplete_tree` in merge/merge.py runs before the stack and cross-measure checks, so a clash FAIL plus a missing file reads UNKNOWN until the worktree is fixed; both refuse. The missing-file stop also runs before the base-conflict FAIL (merge/merge.py `lane_lint`: `refuse_incomplete_tree` runs before `merge_reason`, the base-conflict check). Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job. Fix: none, both refuse.
- lint-worktree-name-in-tests: the test file merge/test_lane_lint.py and the identifiers `LINT_TREE_FILES` and `missing_from_lint_tree` still say "lint tree", not "lint worktree". Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job. Fix: none — rename when next touched.
- cost-pins-test-names-narrow: `*Tests.swift` and `*.spec.ts` are not pin candidates for check_cost_pins, so a Cost sentence that names a Swift or JS test file reads as an unpinned file token. A false alarm for iOS and JS records, none in this corpus yet (lint/check_cost_pins.py `TEST_NAME`). Decision: cost-line-pinned.
- cost-pins-mid-line-cost-unread: a `Cost:` after other text on its line starts no Cost paragraph, so `Then the Cost: reads x.json` is not read (lint/check_cost_pins.py `COST_START`). Decision: cost-line-pinned.
- cost-pins-eg-splits-sentence: `e.g.` and `i.e.` end a sentence, so a "not pinned" mark or a test name in one half misses a token in the other half. A false alarm only (lint/check_cost_pins.py `read_paragraph`). Decision: cost-line-pinned.
- cost-pins-double-backtick-hidden: a double-backtick span reads as two empty spans, so the file name inside it is not seen (lint/check_cost_pins.py `read_paragraph`). Decision: cost-line-pinned.
- cost-pins-hash-line-ends-cost: a wrapped line that starts with `#` (such as `#1850`) reads as a heading, so it ends a Cost paragraph and a Cost section; file names after it are not read (lint/check_cost_pins.py `HEADING`). Decision: cost-line-pinned.
- cost-pins-test-only-sentence: a Cost sentence that names only a `.spec.*` or `*Tests.swift` test file passes, whether or not that file exists (lint/check_cost_pins.py `TEST_NAME`). Fix: none — such a sentence names no file the check must pin, as with `test_*` names on main. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: cost-line-pinned.
- cost-pins-abbrev-merges-exempt: since `e.g.` and `i.e.` end no sentence, a "not pinned" mark or a test name on one side of one covers a file on the other side (lint/check_cost_pins.py `abbreviation`). Fix: none — that is the plain English reading of the sentence, and the split it replaced only caused false alarms. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: cost-line-pinned.
- cost-pins-mid-line-prose-cost: a mid-line sentence that opens with the word Cost is read as a cost line, even in a paragraph that is not one. A file it names is then flagged (lint/check_cost_pins.py `mid_line_start`). Fix: none — a false alarm only, loud and fixed by a "not pinned" mark, and no record has one. Until: 2026-11-21. Decision: cost-line-pinned.
- tmp-write-outside-scratchpad: an agent wrote a scratch file to `/tmp` once, outside its scratchpad, and no shell check refuses that. Decision: none.
- mutant-folder-no-control: a hand-made mutant folder can lack the test files or mutate too broadly, so the mutant looks killed when it is not, and no unmutated control run is required (lint mutants, cost-pin check). Decision: none.
- cost-count-hollow-pin: the nearest span by position can be a plain word the test happens to hold. Decision: cost-line-pinned. Fix: none — a reviewer reads the pin. Until: 2026-11-21.
- cost-count-units-gap: secs, hrs, KiB, days and weeks, "line N of", and "N percent" are flagged. Decision: cost-line-pinned. Fix: none — none seen in the corpus. Until: 2026-11-21.
- cost-count-misses: the check misses seven to nine and twelve; fetch, stat, invocation, append and round trip as nouns; "two-process"; "up to date" or "at least" read as a bound; "a second time". Decision: cost-line-pinned. Fix: none — the corpus marks show no such form slipped through. Until: 2026-11-21.
- janitor-hook-total-starts: `SessionEndHookStartsOneSweepProcess` asserts one sweep, no `ps` and two git starts, but no total, so a fourth start by the hook would pass. Decision: the-janitor-is-one-machine-wide-sweep.
- cost-pins-exempt-no-word-end: `EXEMPT` (lint/check_cost_pins.py:64) has no trailing `\b`, so "no testing overhead" exempts a sentence that names an unpinned file. A false pass. Decision: cost-line-pinned. Fix: none — becomes a debt after a real miss.
- cost-pins-once-conjunction: lint/check_cost_pins.py:291 flags the conjunction "once" after a work verb, so "runs once the session has loaded" reads as a count. Zero corpus hits. Decision: cost-line-pinned. Fix: none — becomes a debt after a real miss.
- cost-pins-unreadable-record-skipped: lint/check_cost_pins.py:318-319 skips a decision record that cannot be read, with no finding. An italic `*Cost:*` label is not read as a Cost paragraph (zero corpus hits). A mark exempts a whole sentence, as ruled. Decision: cost-line-pinned. Fix: none — becomes a debt after a real miss.
- cost-pins-bare-name-root: coordinator ruling, 07:55 CST Nov 12: a bare test name resolves only at the repo root, since a tree-wide name can match two files and pin the wrong one. Example: skills/waiting-page/test_stale_pages.py cannot be pinned by its bare name. Decision: cost-line-pinned. Fix: none — becomes a debt after a real miss.
- cost-pins-id-count-blanked: lint/check_cost_pins.py:88 blanks the id pattern `(rule|step|decision|item|ruling) N` even when a count follows, so "the guard applies this rule 3 times per call" reads no count and passes. Zero corpus hits. Decision: cost-line-pinned. Fix: none — becomes a debt after a real miss.
- cost-pins-empty-folder-passes: lint/check_cost_pins.py:314-315 takes the first records folder that exists, so when neither exists it scans zero records and exits 0. No test covers that. Zero corpus hits. Decision: cost-line-pinned. Fix: none — becomes a debt after a real miss.
- cost-pins-mark-clears-false-alarms: lint/check_cost_pins.py counts a non-count as a count, and a mark clears each false alarm: "measured Nov 12 on a CI run" counts 12; "sha 1234567 reads" counts 1234567; "on Windows 11 the job starts" counts 11; a number in a PR URL before "run" counts; "not more than 3 reads" counts 3, since BOUND (:81) has "no more than" only. Zero corpus hits in a Cost sentence. Decision: cost-line-pinned. Fix: none — a false alarm only, loud and cleared by a mark.
- reader-claim-needs-grep: a brief and a doc said "no check reads .github/pull_request_template.md" with no grep for readers, so a guard-path exception was built on a false premise and failed review round 1 (lane pr-template-stays-guard). A claim that a file has no reader needs a grep for it before it is briefed. Queued as a debt. Decision: pr-review-record.
- pr-template-copies-notes: lint/test_layout.py line 1362 says "the line the .github copy holds today". That is false now that the copies are equal. No case covers a CRLF-only or no-final-newline difference. The check_layout step name in checks.yml and its docstring say readers only. Found in PR 2708 review round 1. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- find-copies-twin-gaps: lint/find_copies.py reports a child `.github/pull_request_template.md` that is byte-equal only to a drifted same-name twin at another path. A `--threshold` below 0 lists a quoting child at score -1. Found in PR 2707 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- find-copies-twin-name: lint/find_copies.py `own_twins` excuses a near-equal twin of the parent's PR template only by the same file name, but a byte-equal twin at any name. `OWN_FILES` holds only the lowercase `.github` path. The clause `or read(parent, p) == own` has no test; a mutant without it passes all 26. Likelihood is low: the real parent keeps its only other template under `skills/project-ops/templates/`, which is skipped. Found in PR 2707 review round 2. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- mac-slice-reach-unmeasured: hooks/test_guard.py MACOS_REACH picks families by hand. The shared-tree family may reach `_process_start_ms` through worktree_remove_subject (guard_shared_tree.py:505) but its row names only kill and branch-worktree. A missing family only narrows the macOS slice. `_platform_truth` returns None for `sys.platform == "darwin" and os.x()`, so that arm is missed with no message. Found in R61 review round 2. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- nightly-skip-live-read-untested: .github/scripts/nightly-skip.py has no test of its live read. `http.client.IncompleteRead` is not an OSError, so it escapes: the job fails and the nightly reads red, against the "exits 0 on every path" docstring. Found in R61 review round 2. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- touched-tests-uname-word: lint/test_touched_tests.py lists `uname -s` as a macOS word, but MACOS_WORDS (.github/scripts/touched-tests.py) has no such word. The fixture passes only because it also holds "Darwin". Found in R61 review round 2. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- scope-dead-windows-outputs: checks.yml outputs checks_windows_merge, checks_windows_rest and checks_windows_rest_2 of the scope job have no reader after R62, and ci-scope.sh:3 still calls them per-job flags. Tests derive the names. Found in R62 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- ci-coverage-off-pr-shard-stale: two notes (ci-coverage-off-pr-shard, around notes.md:1868 and :1873) say a Windows shard "skips pull_request", and lint/test_windows_split.py:7 says the Windows slice runs only on pull_request. Both are false since R62. Found in R62 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- push-skip-premise-unpinned: no test pins that a ready batch-* PR runs every step a push runs. A future push-only step would hide behind push-skip. Found in R62 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- nightly-skip-incompleteread: .github/scripts/nightly-skip.py:55 lacks the catch-any-Exception that push-skip.py got, so a cut-off read fails the job. Scope still runs everything, so the nightly reads red but the suites run. Found in R62 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- fix-check-reads-no-edit-time: `merge --fix-check` (merge/merge.py, the first-fix branch near `elif not last["fix"]`) reads review log bodies only, never the created and updated stamps, so it accepts a fix verifier on a hand-edited log. `merge --release-check` does flag a log edited after it was posted. Found in PR 2969 review rounds 1 and 2. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes
- mac-pick-predicate-limits: lint/test_windows_split.py is_macos matches the substring needs.scope.outputs.mac_runner, so an inline comment on a Windows or Ubuntu runs-on line is read as macOS (adds problems only, a false alarm). A literal `[self-hosted, macOS]` or `needs.scope.outputs['mac_runner']` form is missed. The pick-form defect cases use assertTrue(any problem), so a blind predicate passes them. MAC_PICK_RUNS_ON copies the Mac runner change's RUNS_ON constant; point it at that constant once both live in one module. Found in R61 fix review round 1. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- push-skip-no-clear-red-nightly: a push-skip push does not clear a red nightly (actions/ci-alarm/clear_rule.py `clears`; test 3 in actions/ci-alarm/test_clear_rule.py). A fix merged through an integration PR leaves main red until the next nightly runs in full, or until a dispatch, and merges wait meanwhile. Costs availability, not safety. The cross-group clear in docs/decisions/no-merge-onto-red-main.md needs every job of the red run in the clearing run with success, and a push-skip run has none. Found in R62 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- alarm-empty-exit-ambiguous: actions/ci-alarm/alarm.sh reads clear_rule.py's exit 1 as "not empty", but an uncaught exception also exits 1, so a crash could let an empty run stand as the verdict. API data does not reach it in normal work. Found in R62 review round 1 of the red-main fix. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- red-main-run-pages-differ: merge/merge.py judges 50 runs and actions/ci-alarm/alarm.sh judges 100, so the two can disagree on one red. Found in R62 review round 1 of the red-main fix. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- push-skip-job-hides-skipped-steps: a job that succeeds while its test steps were skipped by a scope flag reads as a passed suite in push-skip. The only guard is the all-flags arm in ci-scope.sh. No incident reaches it today. Found in R62 review round 1 of the red-main fix. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- option-freeze-held-multi-untested: skills/waiting-page/page_grant.py `moved_reason` has held paths for a multi item (an optionId list, a label list, a multi choice key) that no case in test_page_grant_held.py covers. A probe gave the right verdicts. Found in PR 2682 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes
- hooks-probe-trusts-line-scorecard: the `hooks` probe's indented `  trusts: ...` line has no grade, so `scorecard` reads it as a `finding` row, and an `out` run's diff lines add junk rows. No "probe output is a scorecard group" test covers the group. Fix: print the line unindented, or as a graded item. Found in PR 3095 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes
- hooks-probe-usage-root: lint/compliance_probes.py line 13 says `hooks <child root>`; the group reads the parent clone's settings.json, so a child root prints `n/a` exit 0, which reads as clear. The docs pass the parent. Fix: `hooks <parent clone root>`. Found in PR 3095 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes
- fix-check-reads-no-edit-time: `merge --fix-check` (merge/merge.py, the first-fix branch near `elif not last["fix"]`) reads review log bodies only, never the created and updated stamps, so it accepts a fix verifier on a hand-edited log. `merge --release-check` does flag a log edited after it was posted. Found in PR 2969 review rounds 1 and 2. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes
- retire-link-workspaces: the linked `node_modules` in `lint/check_debt_retire.py` resolves npm workspace links into the install checkout's source, not head's. A Retire test that imports a workspace package by name can read a false green. This repo has no workspaces. Found in round 3 of lane retire-missing-deps-unknown. Decision: debt-close-runs-retire.
- retire-link-dirty-tree: the linked `node_modules` shows as untracked in `git status`, because the ignore pattern `node_modules/` does not match a symlink, so a Retire test that asserts a clean tree reads red. No test in this repo does. Found in round 3 of lane retire-missing-deps-unknown. Decision: debt-close-runs-retire.
- staged-cap-read-fails-open: the staged note cap (`--check-staged`, dropped from PR 2825) exits 0 when its git read fails, where the verdict must be unknown. Read path: `staged_notes_over_cap` (origin/roadmap roadmap/build.py:114); its `git diff --cached` (:144) and `git ls-files` (:150) reads ignore the return code. Bad staged JSON is skipped with a stderr warning (:165), exit 0. Found in PR 2825 narrowing. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- note-cap-second-home: the hook that runs the note cap is on the roadmap branch (roadmap/hooks/pre-commit:20 runs `roadmap/build.py --check-staged`; `def check_staged` is at roadmap/build.py:176). The `--check-staged` copy in skills/roadmap/build.py had no caller in this tree, so it was dropped from PR 2825. Decision: building-one-home-per-capability.
- scratchpad-mv-rm-rewrite-empty: agent A's one call `rm -f D/x.tmp; echo ... > D/x.tmp; mv D/x.tmp D/x.py` leaves x.py with an empty record, so another agent's run of x.py passes. `writer_after` in hooks/guard.py reads a removed source as only moved, never written again in the same call. The round-2 reviewer of branch spo-r3-cases reports it on main too. Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only its own read copy of a scratchpad file, and never overwrites another agent's).
- scratchpad-cp-new-folder-file: `mkdir -p D/new && cp src D/new` (no trailing slash, the folder made in the same call) records `new` as the copied file, so `new/src` has no record and another agent's run of it passes. The copy reader cannot see that `new` is a folder before the call makes it. The round-2 reviewer of branch spo-r3-cases reports it on main too. Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent runs only its own read copy of a scratchpad file, and never overwrites another agent's).
- scratchpad-folder-find-delete: `find S -delete` and `find S -exec rm {} +` on a scratchpad folder another agent wrote are not read as a folder removal. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-folder-rmdir: `rmdir` of a scratchpad folder is judged as a write to the folder itself, which has no record. It only removes an empty folder, so nothing is lost. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-folder-python-rmtree: `python -c 'import shutil; shutil.rmtree(...)'` on a scratchpad folder is not read, as the guard cannot see a path inside program text. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-folder-git-clean: `git clean -fdx` run inside a scratchpad checkout removes untracked files without a folder name for the guard to read. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-folder-over-cap: a folder check reads 5000 entries. Past that, a file of another agent that comes later in the walk is not seen and the removal passes. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-folder-parent-rm: `rm -rf` of the session folder above the scratchpad, or `S/../*`, resolves outside the scratchpad, so no scratchpad rule applies. It deletes the files of every agent, and it escapes the folder check. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-folder-records-rm: an agent's `rm -rf S/.writers` is skipped by the folder walk, so it passes. It wipes every record, and a later overwrite of another agent's file then passes. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-folder-env-target: an `rm` target built from `$TMPDIR` or `$(...)` is not read, as `shell_abs` expands no environment variable. A folder named that way passes the folder check. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-folder-dotdot-last: `rm -rf S/sub/deep/..` and `rm -rf S/rl/.` are refused by the folder check, though coreutils `rm` refuses a `.` or `..` last word itself. The refusal is harmless. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-brace-range-pad-step: the folder walk expands the zero-padded range `S/e{01..03}` to `e1 e2 e3`, and keeps the step range `S/d{1..3..1}` literal. Bash makes `e01 e02 e03` and `d1 d2 d3`, so agent B's `rm -rf` of agent A's `e02` or `d2` passes (`expand_shell_braces`). Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-brace-over-cap: a brace list that makes more than 256 words is read only up to 256 (`SCRATCHPAD_BRACE_CAP`). So agent A's folder named past the cap is not read, and the removal passes. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-brace-at-word-head: a brace at the head of an absolute word, as in `rm -rf {S/sub,S/x}`, reads as a relative path outside the scratchpad. It never reaches the folder walk, so it passes. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-brace-quoted-expands: a quoted or variable-held brace list, as in `rm -rf "S/{sub,x}"`, is expanded though bash keeps it literal. So it is refused. This false alarm widens a refusal and opens no miss. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-folder-move-loses-records: a moved scratchpad folder keeps no records for the files in it, because each record is keyed by the old path. Later runs of those files pass. Decision: scratchpad-owner.
- scratchpad-folder-mv-named-twice: an `mv` into a folder agent A wrote in is refused when the same call names that folder again and another agent's file sits in it. Examples are `mkdir -p S/sub && mv own S/sub/` and `mv own S/sub/ && ls S/sub`. The remedy text says to remove only your own files, which does not fit a move. Fix: split the call. Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent never removes another agent's file).
- scratchpad-folder-wrapped-rm: the folder walk runs only when a segment's command word is `rm` or `mv`. So `bash -c 'rm -rf S/sub'`, `sh -c`, `( rm -rf S/sub )`, `echo $(rm -rf S/sub)` and `eval 'rm -rf S/sub'` pass. A single-file rm in the same wrappers is refused. Decision: scratchpad-owner (an agent never removes another agent's file).
- words-probe-scope-unsaid: The `words: in shape` line does not say its scope. With a base, the probe reads only what the change adds. Without one, it reads the whole tree (lint/compliance_probes.py, `probe_words`). Fix: print the scope on the group line; re-point the tests that pin the line. Step: words-probe-skips-kept-names (pull request 3098).
- compliance-skill-words-stale: skills/compliance-check/SKILL.md line 45 describes the `Words` group as reading "each retired word the child uses". The group now reads only the words the child's change adds. Fix: rewrite line 45 in place to say so. Step: words-probe-skips-kept-names (pull request 3098).
- words-probe-committed-untested: No test commits a retired word, fixes it in the work tree, and expects `out` (the committed-lines read). A mutant that empties `committed_lines` (lint/check_glossary.py) passes the Words tests. Fix: add that test. Step: words-probe-skips-kept-names (pull request 3098).
- words-probe-index-quadratic: `names.index(rel)` in `probe_words` makes the loop quadratic. On 30,000 files it costs 4.4 s, against 0.56 s on main. The case is rare. Fix: build an index dict once. Step: words-probe-skips-kept-names (pull request 3098).
- words-probe-norev-fallback: `except NoBase` in `probe_words` falls back to the whole tree when `git rev-parse origin/main...HEAD` fails, not only when no base exists. The fallback is conservative but silent. It ties to words-probe-scope-unsaid. Fix: none yet. Step: words-probe-skips-kept-names (pull request 3098).
- session-messages-stale-open: docs/decisions/session-messages.md lines near 37, 46, 113 and 153 still call the `send_message` doorbell "open" or say "No build is started". The doorbell section says the owner rejected it. Found in PR 2990 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- mail-wait-poll-fail-silent: hooks/mail.py `wait` keeps polling after a failed poll, as the decision says. A run of only failed polls ends with the same "ended, no mail" line as a run with no mail. The session cannot tell them apart. Found in PR 2990 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- mail-wait-pid-alive-untested: hooks/mail.py `_pid_alive` is never run for real in any case, because each case injects it. On Windows a denied `OpenProcess` reads as a dead parent, so the waiter would exit 3 after one interval. Windows CI cannot see it. Found in PR 2990 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- mail-wait-line-unsanitised: the wake line prints `from`, `kind` and `id` unsanitised, as turn-start does on main, and leaves left-to-right marks, zero-width and tag characters in the subject. Found in PR 2990 review round 3. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- mail-wait-ignores-hold: bin/mail runs the `wait` branch before the `mail.held(env)` check, so a platform in HELD_PLATFORMS would not hold `mail wait`. HELD_PLATFORMS is empty today, so nothing is held. Found in PR 2990 fix check on the merge of main. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- open-messages-stray-cr: hooks/mail.py `open_messages` splits `git grep` rows with `splitlines()` and unpacks each with `row.split(":", 2)`. A message whose line ends in CR CR LF, or a lone CR, gives an empty row or a row with no colon. That raises a ValueError, in the turn-start read on main too. Found in the Windows red of batch 115. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- lint-suites-indirect-call-missed: hooks/mutate_guard.py `--lint-suites` misses indirect call forms: os.system, os.popen, subprocess.getoutput and getstatusoutput, and a suite path built from constants that are not call arguments. No suite uses them today. Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job.
- lint-suites-no-delete-pin: no test pins that deleting a .py file nothing imports picks no suite. A mutant that picks on any deletion would only run extra suites. Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job.
- ci-job-after-fail-runs-suite: bin/ci-job: a column-0 suite line after a line ending in `&&` joins the marker echo to that command, so the suite runs even when the guard before it failed. Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job.
- ci-job-suite-blocker-wording: a suite step whose preamble fails sets `blocker` (bin/ci-job), so later lint steps print "install step <name> did not finish". The result stays unknown, which is safe, but the wording calls a suite step an install step. Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job.
- mutate-select-drops-unparsable-suite: `--tests-for` prints an unparsable suite that sits beside the file. A suite that is not beside the file is skipped (hooks/mutate_guard.py `tests_for_main`). Lane lint is unaffected, because a changed suite is re-added. Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job (a lane runs only the suites its change touches).
- lint-suites-no-guard-section-rows: `--lint-suites` has no guard-section runner or GUARD_MODULE_FAMILIES rows, so a guard_<x>.py change picks no hooks/test_guard.py under lane lint (hooks/mutate_guard.py `lint_suites_main`; `--tests-for` reads those rows). Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job.
- select-deleted-suite-passes: a suite deleted or renamed while its checks.yml step stays reads PASS under --lint, since the selector prints nothing and exits 0; CI fails on the missing file.
- select-chain-skips-reads: the import chain follows imports of reached files only, not their path reads; a suite opening entry.py, which reads leaf.py, is not picked by a leaf.py change.
- select-loop-path-unfollowed: a path named by a loop variable, as in `for n in ("x.py",): open(join(HERE, n))`, is not followed.
- select-new-guard-picks-none: a new hooks/guard_<x>.py named by no list picks 0 suites, though test_checks_never_raise.py and test_wrappers.py enumerate it.
- select-bash-suites-never-picked: bash suites such as githooks/test_pre_commit.sh and hooks/test_*.sh are never picked; outside scope.
- lane-step-dup-untagged: A `create_session` with no `cs-step` tag, a carrier row with no `cs-repo` tag, and `list_sessions` filters other than `before_id`, `tags` and `mine` are not judged by rule lane-step-dup. Decision: session-tiers.
- lane-step-dup-archived-after-read: A carrier archived after the whole `list_sessions` read still reads live until the session reads the list again, so the guard may refuse a step it could pass. Decision: session-tiers.
- lane-step-dup-one-rule-id: Part two (open pull request) refuses under rule id `lane-step-dup`, the same as part one, so one override lifts both parts. Decision: session-tiers.
- widening-never-wording-left: the hooks/test_call_judge.py docstring still says "a shared tool never grows a second accepted form" after the ruling became a guideline (item 278, 2026-11-06 12:41 CST). A builder cannot edit tests. No behaviour hangs on it. Decision: uniform-over-variants.
- stamp-readme-wait-item: actions/stamp/README.md item 4 says "The wait leaves out a check". confirm has no wait under the lock now, so the word may be stale. Unverified.
- tests-for-silent-empty: `tests_for_main` in `hooks/mutate_guard.py` prints nothing on a runner parse error or a non-static SECTION_DIR (`_guard_section_sources` returns no file on a parse error, or when `rule_audit._section_dir` finds no folder). Found in PR 2635. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- scratchpad-rm-rf-unguarded: no guard refuses `rm -rf` on a scratchpad folder that another agent of the session wrote. The owner rule (`scratchpad_owner_write` in `hooks/guard.py`) keys its records per file. Decision: scratchpad-owner (the pattern).
- round-text-left-in-two-files: old round text left in docs/decisions/standing-merge-grant.md (net test after round 3 for every guard change) and agents/reviewer.md (round 1 with no safety hole skips rounds 2 and 3). Found by the rounds-rules-one-record round 1 review; pre-existing. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- readonly-for-case-refused: a read-only agent's shell call is refused for `for ((i=5; i>0; i--)); do ...; done`, `case x in a) [[ a > b ]] && echo y ;; esac` and `f() { [[ a > b ]]; }; f`, though each writes nothing. `_command_start` (the read-only shell rule in hooks/guard.py) starts no command after `for`, after a case arm's `)` or after `f()`, so the `>` reads as a redirect. Decision: guard-guards-mistakes-only (a guard stops Claude's mistakes, not an attacker's). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-27
- readonly-xargs-touch-unread: `echo f | xargs touch` writes a file for a read-only agent and is not refused. `xargs` takes its targets from stdin, so the read-only shell rule reads no path (`_verb_written_paths` in hooks/guard.py). Decision: guard-guards-mistakes-only (a guard stops Claude's mistakes, not an attacker's). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-27
- readonly-brief-not-judged: the read-only shell rule judges an agent's type, not its brief, so a general-purpose agent briefed read-only may write. `READ_ONLY` in hooks/agent_brief.py is the only list of read-only roles. Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-27
- readonly-write-verbs-unread: `cp`, `mv`, `sed -i`, `dd of=`, `install`, `curl -o` and Python `open()` writes by a read-only agent are outside the rule's verbs, which are `>` redirects, `tee`, `touch` and `mktemp` (`_verb_written_paths` in hooks/guard.py). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-27
- worktree-red-run-refused: a red-run checkout of an old commit under the agent's worktree folder is refused even with a full path. Reportedly the hand-made-worktree rule (`hand_made_worktree_hit` in hooks/guard.py) and a second worktree guard (`worktree_home_hit`, same file) refused it; which one is unverified. `agents/test-author.md` (line 29) prescribes that checkout, so a test-author cannot follow that line if the refusal is real. Possible false alarm, unmeasured. Decision: red-run-trailer-on-test-commits (a commit that adds test lines carries its red run). Fix: none — rare and harmless. Until: 2026-11-27
- red-two-head-files-singular: Two head-group files (two push workflows in a child repo) can each name a breaking merge. The text says "the breaking merge named above", singular, and the segments do not name their workflow file (`base_state`, `red_base`, merge/merge.py). Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-broke-wins-over-first-red: A breaking merge in one group suppresses the first-red re-run advice for another group. `broke` wins over `first_red` (`red_base`, merge/merge.py). The first red shows again after the revert. Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-breaking-line-error-unknown: An exception inside `breaking_line` turns a red verdict into unknown. Both refuse; only the text degrades. The catch sits in `base_state` (merge/merge.py), around its `breaking_line` call. Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-cancel-stepped-over: A cancelled run between red and green is stepped over, so the named run may skip a cancelled suspect commit. Push runs on main are cancelled only by hand (`breaking_line`, merge/merge.py). Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-cross-group-clear-stale: The cross-group clear is stale. `base_state` keeps only the newest run per group in `red_runs`, but the walk reads older runs of the same group without their clears. So a red cleared by a newer green run of another group can still be blamed (`base_state`, `breaking_line`, merge/merge.py). Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-timeout-run-differs: A run that failed only by timeout counts as red in the walk but not in `base_state` (`breaking_line`, `base_state`, merge/merge.py). Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-first-attempt-hidden: A first-attempt run still going below a completed red re-run is invisible to the walk, because `judged` drops it (`breaking_line`, `base_state`, merge/merge.py). Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- red-decision-text-stale: The decision `no-merge-onto-red-main` still says the refusal names jobs, run and URL, and that the way out is the carrying batch. The planned decision rewrite, act 5, changes it. The owner's pick (a) on question Q3 i is open and not recorded as his pick. Found in PR 2889 (blame each red group's breaking run). Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no batch merges onto a red main).
- push-skip-narrowed-clears-red-main: A push run after a skip looks the same for a narrowed or a full PR run (push-skip success, scope skipped), so `empty_without_evidence` (actions/ci-alarm/clear_rule.py) reads it as evidence. Path: main is red on suite X (direct push, admin merge or tree mismatch), the revert merges under `--base-red-ok`, its PR run scoped X out, push-skip fires, and main reads green with X never re-run. A full run would have run X. Accepted: a revert usually touches the broken paths. Decision: no-merge-onto-red-main (no merge onto a red main). Found in PR 3282 round 1.
- push-skip-test-gaps: Four gaps in the push-skip tests. The `full_run` key in `read_heads` is a stale name for "the run passed". The test `test_a_full_run_whose_only_skipped_steps_are_mutation_report_steps_skips` no longer proves an exemption, since no skipped step stops the skip. The batch case beside a narrowed lane case cannot tell the batch path from the lane path. The run-level completed/success check in `pull_request_run_gap` is not pinned for a cancelled or an in-progress run, because the fixtures carry no job rows. Decision: push-skip-on-proven-tree (a push skips on a proven tree). Found in PR 3282 round 1.
- push-skip-stack-stale-text: After PR 3282 four lines still read as before the stack. Decision no-merge-onto-red-main says the empty-run evidence is a `batch-*` head only, but a lone-lane head skips too. Notes `push-skip-job-hides-skipped-steps` and `push-skip-premise-unpinned` rest on the all-flags arm of ci-scope.sh, which is gone. Ruling own-769-a-narrowed-pr-push accepts skipped steps. Decision test-strategy-reset says the integration PR run is the only macOS run, but lone-lane PR runs start macOS jobs too. The docstring of lint/test_push_skip.py still points at the copy of push-skip.py under .github/scripts. That copy no longer exists. Decision: push-skip-on-proven-tree (a push skips on a proven tree). Found in PR 3282 round 2.

- push-skip-child-snippet-unpinned: the child-repo push skip snippet in skills/ci-hygiene/snippets.md section 20 is pinned by no test; lint/test_push_skip.py reads checks.yml only, so a job-level `if` could return there. Found in review round 3 of PR 3302. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- draft-guard-only-skips-one-output: draft_guard_only and push_guard_only in lint/test_windows_split.py vary the nightly-skip output only, never the push-skip output. Harmless today: a shard needs only scope. Found in review round 1 of PR 3302. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- ci-hygiene-slice-text-stale: skills/ci-hygiene/SKILL.md steps 16 and 19 still say a Windows PR runs a slice; since R62 a ready PR runs the full shards behind the draft guard. Found in review round 2 of PR 3302. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- dead-host-lock-off-linux-never-frees: off Linux a dead host's live-form lock reads as unproven, so Windows and macOS keep the tree and never free it (janitor/sweep.py `_ticks_lock_stale`). Two Linux-only mutants are a bandaid for this. Fix: step:dead-host-lock-form-off-linux
- repaired-merges-docstring: merge/repaired_merges.py:15 docstring says "read on demand, never on a timer". Rule outcomes-mechanize-rules (CLAUDE.md:11) on main dropped that blanket wording and allows a timer whose record shows it costs less than it saves (plan: docs/plans/restart-plan.md:604). The docstring is stale; fix: say "read on demand" and name the rule, no timer claim.
- cited-ids-no-caller-or-test: hooks/question_form.py:1425 `_cited_ids` has no caller in the tree and no test. Found in PR 2682. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- chat-short-form-word-boundary: hooks/question_form.py:1417 `_chat_short_form` counts any non-alphanumeric next character as a word boundary (:1423), so chat `Node` passes against page `Node.js rewrite`, and `Python 3` against `Python 3.12`. Found in PR 2682. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- option-labels-linux-only-ci: .github/workflows/checks.yml:187 runs hooks/test_question_form_option_labels.py only in job `checks` (ubuntu-latest). Its sibling test_question_form_option_gap.py also runs in checks-macos (:1104) and checks-windows-rest (:1852). The tail read on macOS and Windows is unmeasured. Found in PR 2682. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- recommended-option-wording: skills/waiting-page/page.html:1478 and :1487 read "The recommended option goes with your next Send." Wording only: a shorter "Recommended option goes with your next Send." is a judgment call. skills/waiting-page/tests/page.test.mjs:9791 and :9794 pin the long text. Found in PR 2682. Decision: roles-findings-are-debts-or-notes.
- wrong-pr-number-lane-lint: Mistake: lane lint ran on PR 2829, not on the draft PR 2841, a run of minutes that proves nothing about the draft. No guard exists: `lane_lint` (merge/merge.py:3226) prints no PR title or head ref before its checks run. `merge --lane-lint` could print both first, so a wrong number shows at once. Found in review of draft PR 2841 (branch model-auth-key-build). Decision: lanes-run-no-full-ci-job.
- claim-unread-record-title: Mistake: a report called the title of docs/decisions/mods-no-cli-key-headless.md stale without a read. The title (line 1) matches the ruling in the record, so it holds.
